Program Name:  
Telecom and Radio Equipment  
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
This funding project is for the replacement and upgrade of capital components of our 
Land Mobile Radio (LMR) system and associated telecom infrastructure. This LMR 
system covers all upstate New York and is used for the voice communications between 
our control/dispatch centers and all vehicles in the field.  Mobile and portable radio units 
installed in supervisor vehicles, distribution and transmission line vehicles, and other 
field vehicles are all used for daily communications to support substation and power line 
construction, switching,  maintenance, and storm restoration work.  Examples of  
components covered under this funding project are radio transceivers, DC power 
supplies, radio shelters, power back-up generators, and radio towers.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
The LMR system and associated telecom infrastructure have been in service for many 
years.   Components may fail, become obsolete and unsupported by manufactures, or be 
damaged during storms.  Replacements and upgrades are required and prioritized 
annually to keep the system functioning as designed and to maintain the reliability of 
voice communications for National Grid field personnel.  Our crews rely on this system 
for daily operations and safety.  An example is the code-blue emergency procedure, 
where voice communications are used to provide details to the dispatch center when there 
is a health and safety emergency in the field.

C004157 
Telecom and Radio equipment 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Reliable voice communications from field personnel to control centers/dispatch centers 
and corporate offices is needed to support efficient execution of substation and power 
line construction, switching,  maintenance, and storm restoration work.  The LMR system 
directly supports the company’s ability to maintain the reliability of the electric networks.   
 
Alternatives:  
 
There is not an acceptable alternative to our LMR system.  No other system, including 
cellular, provides reliable coverage for instant voice communications within our areas of 
operations. ( Ex: Northern NYS mountain area). 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
As the project need is driven by Communications/Control Systems, it does not meet the 
NWA suitability criteria thresholds. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Communication and Control programs directly improve the Company's ability to 
efficiently interconnect and operate DER. These technologies enable lower cost 
interconnections by allowing the Company to explore techniques such as flexible 
interconnection and advanced dispatch for programs like NWA. Telecom and Control 
(consistency in name of program) will enable more PV and ESS to interconnect to the 
system more efficiently. 
 
EMS / SCADA gives the company higher visibility into system conditions, allowing 
planners to make fewer assumptions, and be less conservative when interconnecting DG. 
This may lead to higher penetration of DG at lower costs

Program Name:  
Side Tap Fusing 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: 
 
C015509 (West), C015510 (East), C015511 (Central) 
 
Description: 
 
This Project involves installing fuse protection at side tap locations on distribution 
circuits across the company’s Upstate New York Service Territory.  It is recommended 
that all single phase and three phase side taps, greater than one section, be fused.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
Currently in the company’s Upstate New York Service territory there are side taps greater 
than one section that are not protected by fuses. These unfused side taps have the 
potential to create larger outages than necessary. Additionally, these unfused side taps 
increase the time it takes to restore power to customers in the event of an outage. The 
increase in time comes from more circuit miles to patrol and locate a fault. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This effort will result in improvement in service quality throughout NY.  Added fusing is 
expected to reduce quantity of Customers Interrupted and reduce duration of interruptions 
when they do occur.

C015509, C015510, C015511

Alternatives: 
 
Do Nothing - this is not recommended 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since this is 
a program, and not a specific project, it was determined that this project will not be 
evaluated for a potential NWA 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

Program Name: 
Substation Power Transformer 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  None 
 
Description: 
 
This program is to replace distribution power transformers with anomalous Dissolved 
Gas Analysis (DGA) results that have been, or are expected to be confirmed, as in poor 
condition through electrical testing. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The Company’s maintenance program includes performing DGA on distribution 
transformers annually. DGA is a cost effective condition assessment tool that detects 
abnormal behaviors within transformers which may indicate a developing fault. Analysis 
of this data is performed using the IEEE Standard C57.104.1991. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The failure of a typical distribution station power transformer could lead to the loss of 
power for approximately 17,000 customers. The prolonged time needed for restoration 
(either through the installation of a spare or mobile substation) can translate into millions 
of customer minutes interrupted.

C025801 
NY ARP Transformers 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Refurbishment: This option would be to undertake a major refurbishment 
as opposed to replacement. Components would need to be refurbished back to original 
design tolerances and replacement of any worn-out or degraded parts would need to be 
acquired. In addition, refurbishment may only provide a minimal extension of useful life. 
Refurbishment is a one-off activity and cannot be repeated indefinitely, but refurbishment 
may have limited application where it is not possible to replace the transformer due to 
outage or other constraints. 
 
Alternative 2: Do nothing: This option would have no initial cost, however, there will be 
indirect costs associated with increased maintenance levels. This option would involve no 
proactive replacement of equipment, only replacing when failure occurs. This option is 
unacceptable because leaving degraded transformers in service places the Company and 
customers at risk of long-term interruptions on the distribution system and violent failures 
of this equipment have the potential to cause extensive damage to other equipment as 
well as serious injuries to Company employees. All distribution power transformers 
should be replaced before the onset of significant unreliability. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since this is 
a program, and not a specific project, it was determined that this project will not be 
evaluated for a potential NWA

Program Name: 
Substation Power Transformer 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  None 
 
Description: 
 
The NY ARP Spare Substation Transformer Program was implemented to purchase spare 
distribution Transformers. The spares are purchased based on analysis conducted by the 
company identifying gaps in coverage, in the event of a failure.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
Distribution power transformers are large capital items with long lead times. Their 
performance can have a significant impact on reliability and system capacity. Condition 
data and condition assessment are the key drivers for identifying the need for system 
spares. Replacements are prioritized through a risk analysis which includes feedback 
from operations personnel. The distribution element covers transformers which are 
identified as replacement candidates through test and assessment procedures. A ‘watch 
list’ of candidate transformers has been identified and recorded in the annual Asset 
Condition Report. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The impact of power transformer failure events on customers is historically substantial. 
By proactively replacing units in poor condition with available system spares there will 
be a direct benefit to customers due to a reduced impact on power transformer

The planned replacement of the system spares reduced the lead time to long term 
interruptions on the distribution system in the event of a failure. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Refurbishment: This option would be to undertake a major refurbishment 
as opposed to replacement. Components would need to be refurbished back to original 
design tolerances and replacement of any worn-out or degraded parts would need to be 
acquired. In addition, refurbishment may only provide a minimal extension of useful life. 
Refurbishment is a one-off activity and cannot be repeated indefinitely, but refurbishment 
may have limited application where it is not possible to replace the transformer due to 
outage or other constraints. 
 
Alternative 2: Do nothing: This option would have no initial cost, however, there will be 
indirect costs associated with increased maintenance levels. This option would involve no 
proactive replacement of equipment, only replacing when failure occurs. This option is 
unacceptable because leaving degraded transformers in service places the Company and 
customers at risk of long-term interruptions on the distribution system and violent failures 
of this equipment have the potential to cause extensive damage to other equipment as 
well as serious injuries to Company employees. All distribution power transformers 
should be replaced before the onset of significant unreliability. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since the 
justification for this project is Asset Condition in nature, it was determined that this 
project will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

Program Name:  
I&M Program 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  None 
 
Description: 
 
I&M is a mandatory program in NY.  These funding projects are for the annual D-Line 
Inspections. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
In an order in Case 04-M-0159, the New York Public Service Commission (PSC) 
required the following: 
 
• Annual elevated voltage testing be conducted on all utility facilities that are 
capable of conducting electricity and are publicly accessible, including municipal-
owned streetlights.  Elevated voltage testing shall be performed based on a 1 volt 
standard set by the PSC. 
 
• An inspection program on a five-year cycle that includes, at a minimum, visual 
examination of towers, poles, guy wires, risers, overhead cables and conductors, 
transformers, breakers, switches, other above ground equipment and facilities, the 
interior of manholes, service boxes, vaults, and other underground structures.

          Page 13 of 457

Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan

Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 14 of 457 
C026161, C026160, C026159, C026164, 
C026163, and C026162 
I&M -NYW, NYC, NYE D-Line OH & UG 
Work From Insp. 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
• A quality assurance program to ensure timely and proper compliance with safety 
standards. 
 
In addition to meeting the requirements above, the program will drive a consistent 
inspection approach to benefit customers by evaluating asset health and allowing for a 
safe, adequate system.  This program is intended to meet National Electrical Safety Code 
(NESC) Section 214, which outlines inspection of equipment guidelines.  Additionally, 
this program will allow for the avoidance of potential environmental issues related to 
some assets, such as transformers and capacitor banks. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Asset replacement prior to failure provides incremental employee and public safety 
benefits and avoidance of potential environmental problems related to some assets (i.e., 
transformers and poles).  This program will satisfy Section 214 of the NESC, which 
outlines inspection of equipment guidelines for electric utilities.  In addition, 
implementation of this program addresses safety concerns relating to contact voltage on 
all publicly accessible Company distribution line facilities. The I&M program is 
mandated in New York as discussed in Section 1.2.  The main customer benefits from 
this program are elimination of elevated voltage hazards, reduced safety risks, improved 
reliability, and maintaining a sustainable system.  The program retains assets in service 
until condition warrants their replacement, as opposed to time based replacement. 
Condition based repair / replacement will maintain reliability and support the creation of 
a sustainable system.  Collectively deteriorated equipment related interruptions are one of 
the main drivers of poor reliability.  
 
Alternatives:  
None 
 
DER/NWA Alternative:   
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since this is 
a program, and not a specific project, it was determined that this project will not be 
evaluated for a potential NWA 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA): N/A

Program Name: 
Replacement - Safety and Reliability 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
Systematic replacement of above-ground, wooden transformer/fuse structures in Raquette 
Lake and St. Lawrence areas with standard padmounted equipment.  These structures 
require periodic maintenance and do not meet current approach distance requirements. 
Conversion to 13.2KV will necessitate replacement of approximately 5 miles of 5KV 
cable. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Wooden transformer structures at ground level create a safety concern.  Many of these 
installations are extremely hard to reach due to island locations or foot-only accessibility 
in the Adirondack park.  There is no arc flash protection for qualified personnel working 
in these structures.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Wooden Transformer structures do not provide proper arc flash protection. Replacement 
of these wooden transformer structures will ensure a member of the public does not come 
in contact with live electrical equipment.

Program Name:  
Substation Batteries 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  None 
 
Description: 
 
Substation Batteries and Chargers are one of the more crucial assets within a Substation. 
These batteries provide backup power to the relays that operation equipment in the yard 
and communication equipment upon loss of power to the control house. This program is 
for the purchase and replacement of substation batteries and chargers in the Company's 
Upstate NY operating territories due primarily to asset condition issues. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The primary driver for this program is asset condition of battery and charger systems 
which are critical components of a substation and required to ensure full operational 
capability during both normal and abnormal system conditions in substations. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
By proactively replacing substation batteries and chargers the Company is able to 
maintain safe operating conditions for both Company personnel and customers. This 
allows for restoration activities to be executed during system contingencies, mitigating 
the amount of time needed to restore power to customers.

C032252, C032253, C032261, C049257,

Substation 4- 69kV Circuit Breaker and 
Recloser Program 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020

T SubT D

Multi-Value

(MVT) 
  
  
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
There are several circuit breaker families that have been recommended for replacement 
due to their maintenance, reliability concerns and/or obsolescence of parts.  The circuit 
breaker families include Allis-Chalmers, Condit, Federal Pacific, General Electric, ITE, 
McGraw Edison and Westinghouse pertaining to voltage levels 4kV - 69kV. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The method for managing substation circuit breakers and reclosers consists of periodic 
maintenance and asset condition-based replacements. This approach is augmented by a 
replacement program targeting obsolete and unreliable circuit breaker families and units 
in poor condition. Obsolete units have been specifically identified for replacement 
because they are difficult to repair due to a lack of available spare parts and manufacturer 
support. Unreliable breaker families also have been identified for replacement because 
their replacement will reduce the number of customer interruptions.

C032252, C032253, C032261, C049257, 
C049258, C049260 
Substation 4- 69kV Circuit Breaker and 
Recloser Program 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The program will help maintain reliability and mitigate the risk of asset failure by 
proactively replacing circuit breakers identified as being in poor condition.  In addition, 
replacement of several of the targeted circuit breaker families will reduce potential 
environmental and safety hazards (oil, asbestos, arc flash, ground contamination and 
isolation issues).   
 
Alternatives: 
 
Maintenance – Continue to repair failed and/or obsolete circuit breakers. This is not a 
viable alternative in the long-term and will result in greater cost, deteriorated reliability 
performance, and increased safety risks. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
As the project is driven by asset condition, it does not meet the NWA suitability criteria 
thresholds.

C032446 
Harris 54 Relief 
Version 1 –

Multi-Value 
Transmission 
(MVT) 
  
  
  
X 
  
 
 
Program Name:  
None 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: CD01088 - Harris Road Substation Expansion 
 
Description: 
 
This is a continuation of the Harris Rd expansion project.  Phase I was completed in 
2016.  For this phase, the plan is to retire Glenwood Substation by installing 
approximately 4,000' of cable, approximately 6,000 feet of double circuit overhead and 
2,000 feet of new overhead.  Also, the existing Glenwood 4.16kV feeders will be rebuilt 
and converted to 13.2kV and placed on this new feeder. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The remaining driver is the retirement of Glenwood Substation (2,585 customers), which 
would utilize the new 23555 feeder from Harris Rd.  Glenwood is a 34.5:4.16kV metal 
clad station with no external ties and numerous asset condition issues.  Most critical is the 
rodent damage to several control cables. This was first identified during the initial stages 
of a planned RTU installation project. The wiring repairs was not undertaken for fear of 
inadvertent or breaker mis-operations. The use of a mobile substation was not considered 
viable as the entire station would need to be de-energized and the RTU project was 
withdrawn and replaced with this project to retire Glenwood.

          Page 25 of 457

Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 26 of 457 
C032446 
Harris 54 Relief 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
 This project will eliminate the 4.16kV island at Glenwood. 
 
Alternatives:  
None 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Due to the 
timing of the need, it was determined that this project will not be evaluated for a potential 
NWA.    
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally 
increases the threshold for certain higher cost interconnection upgrades (such as 3V0). 
This will enable PV and ESS to interconnect at higher volumes with a lower marginal 
interconnection cost.  
 
VVO directly contributes to system efficiency, contributing to EE targets, as well as 
reducing energy required from carbon heavy generation (by reducing the total energy 
procured from NYISO). Increasing efficiency also reduces peak loading, which may 
decrease the need for fossil fuel peaking plants. 
 
Studies/References:

Riverside 28855 UG Cable Replacement 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Type: 
 
T SubT D

Multi-Value

(MVT) 
  
  
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name:  
None 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
Replacement of approximately 22,580 ft total of PILC cable.  Cable sizes consist of 500 
Cu, 4/0 Cu, and sections of parallel sets of 3/0 Cu.  Additionally, as a result of congested 
duct-lines and conduit sections too small to accommodate single sets of standard cable, 
the project also includes the installation of approximately 5,925 ft of new manhole and 
duct-line.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
The Riverside 28855 circuit was targeted for replacement as a result of asset condition.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The number of customers served during normal operation is approximately 930.  The 
operation of older conductors / cable potentially creates and condition where the electric 
system and customers are more susceptible to interruptions / outages.  Additionally, the 
condition of insufficient conduit availability and size results in problematic replacement 
in the event of cable failures.

Alternatives: 
 
Option 1:  Replace sections as necessary as a result of failures and etc.  This option was

Additionally, replacement is in accordance with the current New York Underground 
Cable Strategy. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since the 
justification for this project is Asset Condition in nature, it was determined that this 
project will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

Project C015754 provides for the replacement of the existing 4.16kV feeder cables from 
the circuit breakers to the first manhole with 500kcmil Cu 15kV cables. 
 
Project C015755 provides for the replacement of the 23kV cables (1K,2K, 3K, 4K)  from 
the first manhole outside of the substation to the 23kV reclosers with 500kcmil Cu 
Cables. As well as, full EMS metering and control equipment including 3VO protection 
capability. In addition, building civil work is required to repair the building masonry, 
foundations and roof

C046519,C015755, C015754 
BFLO STA 30 Rebuild 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Buffalo Station 30 is an indoor substation with deteriorated equipment and is a risk to 
reliable service to customers and safety for employees. The station has a design capacity 
of approximately 10 MVA. In addition to rebuilding for asset condition reasons the 
station is also reviewed for capacity upgrades. For an outage to one transformer, the 
remaining transformers would be at 100 % of Emergency rating. The 23kV cables 
supplying the transformers would also be 100% over the cables normal rating 
 
The following concerns have been cited as reasons to rebuild Buffalo Indoor Substations: 
 
• Overload Conditions; 
• Lack of Spare Transformers, oil switches and relays; 
• Transformers have unusable conservator tanks; 
• Regulators have gears worn to razor sharp edges; 
• Misoperation of relays due to age and wear; 
• Protective relay scheme is of an obsolete design; 
• Misoperation of feeder [4.16 kV] breakers due to age and accumulated wear; 
• Feeder breakers with limited interrupting capability; and 
• Poor Station cooling leading to transformer overheating 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The proposed program reduces the likelihood of customer service interruptions. This is 
done by rebuilding the substation with a more robust arrangement and removing 
equipment in poor condition from service. Additionally, these projects re-enforce the 
distribution system and improve operational flexibility while reducing outage durations. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Both indoor and outdoor construction will be considered for this project. Given the main 
driver is Asset Condition, any additional alternatives such as relocation of the station 
wouldn’t make sense from a financial standpoint. Both high side and low-side cables are 
in concrete encased duct and manhole systems which limit where the station can be 
situated. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative:   
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Based on the

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C047877, C047885, C051495 
 
Description: 
 
Delamater substation is a 115/13.2kV single 13/18/22MVA transformer station with over 
20% of the Angola operating district customers. This project proposes the replacement of 
the existing transformer, installation of a second bank with a 20/26/33 MVA transformer, 
and installation of a 4-position metal clad. The existing transformer has severe asset 
condition concerns and a MWHr violation that identifies this station as a 
reliability/resiliency concern. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Part of an Eden/Angola Area study of the Erie South region, adding a new 115/13.2 kV 
substation transformer to fulfill reliability and capacity concerns at the single bank 
substation Delamater Road.  While adding a second transformer and additional feeder 
positions, the feeders will be configured to displace two of the limited capacity feeders’ 
load onto two new feeders.  In conjunction with the other project in the Eden/Angola 
Area study, a new 34.5/13.2kV 10MVA substation at Eden Switch Structure, the two 
projects will be able to retire asset condition ridden Eden Center 88 substation and 
mitigate capacity concerns at North Eden 82 substation.

Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

of North Eden, Eden Center, and North Collins feeders, as well as the sub-transmission

Eden switch structure -install 2-10

Customer Benefit: 
 
Enhanced reliability, operational flexibility, system resiliency, increased substations

Alternatives:  
 
 
N/A 
 
DER/NWA Alternative:   
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Due to the 
timing of the need, it was determined that this project will not be evaluated for a potential 
NWA.    
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally 
increases the threshold for certain higher cost interconnection upgrades (such as 3V0). 
This will enable PV and ESS to interconnect at higher volumes with a lower marginal 
interconnection cost.  
 
VVO directly contributes to system efficiency, contributing to EE targets, as well as 
reducing energy required from carbon heavy generation (by reducing the total energy 
procured from NYISO). Increasing efficiency also reduces peak loading, which may 
decrease the need for fossil fuel peaking plants. 
 
Studies/References:

13.2kV Eden Switch Station

Program Name:  
None 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  None 
 
Description: 
 
Replace the existing 9.375MVA TB1 at Gilbert Mills with a 15/20/25 MVA transformer, 
replace the 115kV fuses with a 115kV Circuit Switcher and install EMS. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Gilbert Mills TB1 was loaded to 108% of its Summer Normal rating in 2018 and 94% of 
its Sumer Normal rating in 2019.  It is predicted to be at 103% of its Summer Normal 
rating in 2020.  There is also a 3MW (104MWHr) load at risk at Gilbert Mills. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This will eliminate the normal overload on the transformer at Gilbert Mills.  Also, if the 
bank were to fail due to a thermal overload, not all of the customers would be able to be 
picked up on feeder ties and would be out until a mobile transformers was installed. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Add a second transformer to Gilbert Mills. In addition, install at least one additional 
feeder and transfer one of the existing feeders to the new bank.

C046563 
Gilbert Mills Transformer Upgrade

DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
An NWA RFP was released to solicit an alternative, but all DER alternatives were 
evaluated and ruled out as preferred alternatives (due to failing BCA scores). 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally 
increases the threshold for certain higher cost interconnection upgrades (such as 3V0). 
This will enable PV and ESS to interconnect at higher volumes with a lower marginal 
interconnection cost.  
 
VVO directly contributes to system efficiency, contributing to EE targets, as well as 
reducing energy required from carbon heavy generation (by reducing the total energy 
procured from NYISO). Increasing efficiency also reduces peak loading, which may 
decrease the need for fossil fuel peaking plants.

Description: 
 
This project will involve reconductoring and replacing poles for approximately 10.15 
miles of Poland 62258 on NYS Route 8 beginning at P85 all the way to P259 at the 
corner of Haskell Rd. Also, relocate approximately 1.2 miles from off-road areas.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
Poland 62258 has been on the Reliability Top 5% Worst Performing feeders every year 
for the last 10 years.  Overhead three phase portions of Poland 62258 beyond SW X62-11 
(pole 87) is small conductor that is old and brittle. Sections break free and come down at 
least annually due to weather related or equipment related problems.  Difficult for crews 
to repair because of the small size for its voltage class (8.32kV). Some of the sections are 
off road which increases the restoration time. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This project will ensure the 670 customers on this portion of the Poland 58 feeder 
continue to be served safely and reliably.

DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Due to the 
timing of the need, it was determined that this project will not be evaluated for a potential 
NWA.    
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

Program Name:  
None 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C046609 Milton Ave Second switchgear. 
 
Description: 
 
This funding project is for the overhead and underground work associated with the 
completed C046609 Milton Ave substation expansion.  The work is necessary to 
facilitate the reconfiguration of supply to circuits in the area.  Milton 26657 will be 
transferred to the new R530 (26653) position.  Milton 2655 will be reconfigured to pick 
up both Camillus feeders (1066 and 1067).  Milton 26654 will be a new feeder and it will 
pick up all the Hinsdale feeders (2186, 21862, 21863 and 21864). Milton 26652 will be a 
new feeder and it will pick up most of Milton 26655 Load.  Milton 26651 will be a new 
feeder and portions pf Milton 26657 and 26658 will be transferred to it.  The distribution 
line project will also convert and rebuild both Camillus and Hinsdale feeders to 13.2kV.  
Protective fusing will be upgraded, and the duct system will be installed at the existing 
Milton Avenue substation for four new feeder getaways. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The primary driver for this project is capacity; TB#1 projected to be at 112% of its 
thermal rating in 2016.  Additionally, the 26655 underground feeder is projected to be at 
115% of its thermal rating in 2016 (which is 3-1/C 500kcmil Al EPR 15kV cable located 
in a 2X2 conduit bank, each conduit 5 inched in diameter, with other Milton Ave 
feeders).

C046643 
Milton Ave DLine 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Another driver for this project is the 5kV "electrical islands" that exist at Hinsdale #218 
and Camillus #10 substation, both of which are single ended stations with no field ties. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Milton distribution feeders serve approximately 9,400 customers.  The addition of a 
second bank will address the projected normal loading issues on TB#1 as well as 
providing redundancy and eliminate MWh outage exposure in the event of a loss of either 
bank.  Reliability enhancement is also expected with the retirement of the Hinsdale and 
Camillus 5kV substations by creating additional feeder ties between existing 13.2kV 
circuits. Camillus substation serves 752 customers and Hinsdale substation serves 1,897 
customers. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Load relief by switching, utilizing existing distribution circuits.  Moving distribution load 
from the Milton Avenue transformer by utilizing field ties was looked at and determined 
unfeasible to keep the transformer under its summer thermal rating.  The problem is that 
there are already double circuits head toward Milton, so there would be some extensive 
UG to get the circuits towards Milton.  And there is still load growth to the east 
(Onondaga Community College area) and those circuits will still be heavily loaded after 
Harris is complete.  Therefore, the Milton Avenue expansion is the preferred option to 
serve any potentially new large load in the area. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Due to the 
timing of the need, it was determined that this project will not be evaluated for a potential 
NWA.    
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Resiliency spends improve the system's ability to re-energize portions of the system post 
contingency. These projects will generally reduce the duration of outages for load and 
generation customers. This means clean generation (such as solar PV) will have increased 
deliverability, increasing the average carbon emissions per MWh in NY with a cleaner, 
more deliverable, supply.

(MVT) 
  
  
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name:  
Specific 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C082809: Station 124 Roof and Building 
 
Description: 
 
This project will remove 34.5-4.36kV 3.75/4.68MVA transformers 1, 2, 3, and 4 and 
replace with 34.5-4.36kV 4.2/5.25MVA power transformers with Load Tap Changer 
(LTC) and high and low-side surge arresters. Manufacturer dates for the transformers 
(TRF1 1955, TRF2 1955, TRF3 1962, TRF4 1965).  Additionally, the containment in 
each bay will be improved by installing concrete secondary oil containment. Three (3) 
high side load break disconnect switches will be replaced with 1200A, 38kV motor 
operated vertical break disconnects and twelve (12) fuses and fuse bypasses will be 
replaced and six (6) potential transformers will be installed. The line protection relays 
will be upgraded with the installation of dual 3V0 numerical 64 relays on both the 606 
and 605 lines. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
This is an asset condition project to replace the deteriorated high side equipment at 
Station 124 - Almeda Ave. All four (4) transformer banks have elevated gas levels and 
deteriorated insulation. The banks are also heavily loaded. Dissolved gas analysis on TR4 
show significant levels of ethane and ethaline indicating increased likelihood of failure. 
The high side motor operated double end break disconnects with load break capability are 
experiencing issues from outdoor exposure and need to be replaced.

C046670

Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacement of the four transformers with upgraded capacity and related equipment will 
mitigate the risk of an in-service failure due to asset condition issues impacting 3993 
customers. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Transformer and Breaker replacement: Replace the existing four (4) power 
transformers with the current standard sized 34.5-4.36kV, 4.2/5.25MVA power 
transformers with LTC, replace three (3) motor operated double end break disconnects 
with new motor operated vertical break disconnects, and replace twenty (20) draw-out 
type circuit breakers. This alternative is not recommended as the breakers have not been 
experiencing issues and will be replaced under the breaker replacement program at a later 
date. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative:

driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since the 
justification for this project is Asset Condition in nature, it was determined that this 
project will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

C046671 
Rock City Station - 13.2kV Rebuild 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Type: 
 
T SubT D 
 
 
X 
 
 
Spending Rationale: 
Asset Condition 
Communications/ 
Control Systems 
Customer 
Requests/ Public 
Requirements 
DER Electric 
System 
Access

X 
  
  
  
  
 
Non- 
Infrastructure 
Reliability 
Resiliency 
System 
Capacity 
Multi-Value 
Transmission 
(MVT) 
  
  
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
This project is to rebuild Rock City substation at 13.2kV. This project will include 
building a new low side bus and replacing TB1. 
 
The existing transformer will be replaced with one (1) new 46kV/13.2kV 7.5/9.375MVA 
w/LTC transformer with high & low side surge arrestors. One new 46kV 1200A vacuum 
125VDC operated breaker and One (1) new 46kV 1200A gang operated disconnect will 
be installed for high side protection. A new 13.2kV bus will be extended over the existing 
structure to connect the new 13.2kV bus to the transformer. This project will also include 
the addition of 3V0 protection & bi-directional LTC controls. No Sub-transmission line 
work is anticipated. 
 
The substation yard will be expanded approximately 30ft to the north by the current 60ft 
width of the station. National Grid owns 65ft of land in the direction of the station 
expansion. A new 15kV low side bus will be built in the station expansion that includes 
four (4) new 15kV class 1200A, 48VDC operated vacuum breakers, eight (8) 15kV class 
disconnect switches for the breakers, one (1) main bus disconnect for the transformer, 
and one (1) 15kV class disconnect for a mobile connection. Outside of the station 3 pad 
mount ratio transformers will be installed for the 4.16 feeders. The new distribution will 
require five riser poles with gang operated disconnects, 2 new poles, and 3 additional 
gang operated disconnect switches for the feeder ties.

          Page 56 of 457

Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 57 of 457 
C046671 
Rock City Station - 13.2kV Rebuild 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification: 
 
An Asset Condition Repot was completed for Rock City substation in October 2018. The 
46-4.16kV, 7MVA transformer bank 1 at Rock City Station 623 is in poor condition, with 
PCB contamination and evidence of very high temperature overheating occurring in the 
oil. The bus has some cracked brown glass insulators. The breakers are older type 
vacuum breaker designs with self-contained relays running off 240VAC. All 4 breakers 
sit on deteriorating wooden rail road tie foundations. While the station was built to 
required clearances at the time of construction it does not meet current spacing required 
for worker clearance. A broken switch prevents the bus from being de-energized, so no 
maintenance can be performed without customer outages.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The Rock City substation is located within Little Falls, NY and supplies approximately 
1,480 customers in and around the Little Falls area. The increased transformer size from 
7MVA to 7.5/9.375 will allow for load growth. Converting to 13.2kV will give the ability 
to transfer load from other stations when feeders are converted in the future. The rebuild 
of the Rock City substation will ensure that this portion of Utica continues to be served 
safely and reliably. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Option 1: Replace transformers at Rock City substation and South Washington 
substation. 
Option 2: Rebuild Rock City substation at 13.2kV. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
As the project need is driven by asset condition, it does not meet the NWA suitability 
criteria thresholds. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA): N/A

N/A

USSC-19-201

N/A

C036459 Buffalo Station 32 Rebuild-Sub

Description: 
 
These Projects provide for the removal of all the existing equipment and the installation 
of four (4) new 23/4.33kV 3.75/4.687 MVA transformers with LTCs. The (4) 
transformers will relieve contingency loading issues on the 9K, 13K 11K and 12K 23 kV 
supply cables.  Also, the project will install new 23kV tie switches, disconnect switches, 
and reclosers on the high side of the transformers and new 15kV rated indoor metalclad 
switchgear for the 4.16kV feeders. Full EMS metering and control equipment and 3VO 
protection capability will be installed. Civil will be performed to repair the roof, masonry 
and foundations.  
 
Additionally, they provide for the replacement of the existing 4.16kV feeder cables from 
the circuit breakers to the first manhole with 500kcmil Cu 15kV cables. 
 
Along with the replacement of the 23kV cables (9K, 13K,11K and 12K) from the first 
manhole outside of the substation to the 23kV reclosers with 500kcmil Cu Cables.

Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 60 of 457 
C036459, C036461, C036460 
BFLO STA 32 Rebuild 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Buffalo Station 32 is an indoor substation with deteriorated equipment and is a risk to 
reliable service to customers and safety for employees. The station has a design capacity 
of approximately 10 MVA. In addition to rebuilding for asset condition reasons, the 
station is also reviewed for capacity upgrades. For an outage to one transformer, the 
remaining transformers would be 105% over the emergency rating. The 23kV cables 
supplying the transformers would also be 105% over the cables normal rating 
 
The following concerns have been cited as reasons to rebuild Buffalo Indoor Substations: 
 
• Overload Conditions; 
• Lack of Spare Transformers, oil switches and relays; 
• Transformers have unusable conservator tanks; 
• Regulators have gears worn to razor sharp edges; 
• Misoperation of relays due to age and wear; 
• Protective relay scheme is of an obsolete design; 
• Misoperation of feeder [4.16 kV] breakers due to age and accumulated wear; 
• Feeder breakers with limited interrupting capability; and 
• Poor Station cooling leading to transformer overheating. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The proposed program reduces the likelihood of customer service interruptions. This is 
done by rebuilding the substation with a more robust arrangement and removing 
equipment in poor condition from service. Additionally, these projects re-enforce the 
distribution system and improve operational flexibility while reducing outage durations. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Both indoor and outdoor construction was considered for this project. Given the main 
driver is Asset Condition, any additional alternatives such as relocation of the station 
wouldn’t make sense from a financial standpoint. Both high side and low-side cables are 
in concrete encased duct and manhole systems which limit where the station can be 
situated. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative:   
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Based on the 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10)

element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

Description: 
 
Project C046945 provides for the removal of all the existing equipment and the 
installation of four (4) new 23/4.33kV 3.75/4.687 MVA transformers with LTCs. The 4 
transformers would also relieve contingency loading issues on the 22K, 23K and 33K 23 
kV supply cables by transferring some load to a new fourth cable (21K)  and transformer. 
Also install new 23kV tie switches, disconnect switches, and reclosers on the high side of 
the transformers and new15kV rated indoor Metalclad switchgear for the 4.16kV feeders. 
A fourth bay will need to be added to the existing three-bay brick structure.  Also 
upgrade station Protection, EMS, Metering and civil work to repair the station Roof, 
Masonry and foundations.  
 
Project C046929 provides for the replacement of the existing 4.16kV feeder cables from 
the circuit breakers to the first manhole with 500kcmil Cu 15kV cables.. 
 
 Project C046928 provides for the replacement of the 23kV cables (22k, 23K and 33K 
and the new cable 21K)  from the first manhole outside of the substation to the 23kV 
reclosers with 500kcmil Cu Cables.

C046945, C046929, C046928 
BFLO STA 53 Rebuild 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Buffalo Station 53 is an indoor substation with deteriorated equipment and is a risk to 
reliable service to customers and safety for employees. The station has a design capacity 
of approximately 7.5 MVA. In addition to rebuilding for asset condition reasons the 
station is also reviewed for capacity upgrades. For an outage to one transformer, the 
remaining transformers would be 105% over the emergency rating. The 23kV cables 
supplying the transformers would also be 105% over the cables normal rating 
 
The following concerns have been cited as reasons to rebuild Buffalo Indoor Substations: 
 
• Overload Conditions; 
• Lack of Spare Transformers, oil switches and relays; 
• Transformers have unusable conservator tanks; 
• Regulators have gears worn to razor sharp edges; 
• Misoperation of relays due to age and wear; 
• Protective relay scheme is of an obsolete design; 
• Misoperation of feeder [4.16 kV] breakers due to age and accumulated wear; 
• Feeder breakers with limited interrupting capability; and 
• Poor Station cooling leading to transformer overheating 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The proposed program reduces the likelihood of customer service interruptions. This is 
done by rebuilding the substation with a more robust arrangement and removing 
equipment in poor condition from service. Additionally, these projects re-enforce the 
distribution system and improve operational flexibility while reducing outage durations. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1; Rebuild Buffalo 53 as a (4) Transformer 4 23k kV cable station: By 
refurbishing existing building and replacing all existing equipment one bus section at a 
time and also adding a fourth Bay to the building without the fourth transformer 
 
Alternative 2: Rebuild Buffalo STA 53 as a (3) Transformer Station with Non -Wires 
Alternative: replacing the three existing transformers with new 23kV/4.33 KV  
3.75/4.687 MVA transformers with LTCs and all associated equipment in the 
recommended option and add an additional fourth by however postpone the installation 
of the Fourth Transformer and instead use a NWA distributed generation to lower the 
total station load below the emergency capacity of the two of the three new transformers.  
 
The company is actively soliciting 1 MW of Distributed Energy Resources DER on the 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10)

BFLO STA 53 Rebuild

feeders for Station 53 and the feeders on adjacent Station 21. Adding DER on the feeders 
of the two stations would reduce the overload on the 23 kV cables (22K, 23K, 33K) that 
supply both stations and would postpone the need to add a fourth station transformer at 
Station 53. Going forward an additional 300 kVA per year of DER will be required to 
keep pace with area load growth. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
A non-wires RFP was submitted to encourage the addition of distributed generation 
resources to the feeders of STA 53 & the surrounding feeders. No proposals passed 
evaluation (BCA), and National Grid had to progress with the ultimate buildout of STA 
53 to a four bay, four Transformer station with four 23 kV cable supplies.

Description: 
 
Rebuild Buffalo STA 35 
 
Project C046954 provides for the removal of all the existing equipment and the 
installation of four (4) new 23/4.33kV 3.75/4.687 MVA transformers with LTCs. The 4 
transformers would also relieve contingency loading issues on the , 16S, 17S, 18S, and 
27S 23 kV supply cables. Also install new 23kV tie switches, disconnect switches, and 
reclosers on the high side of the transformers and new15kV rated indoor Metalclad 
switchgear for the 4.16kV feeders. Upgrade station protection and repair building 
masonry and roof.  
 
Project C046934 provides for the replacement of the existing 4.16kV feeder cables from 
the circuit breakers to the first manhole with 500kcmil Cu 15kV cables.. 
 
 Project C046933 provides for the replacement of the 23kV cables from the first manhole 
outside of the substation to the 23kV reclosers with 500kcmil Cu Cables.

C046954, C046934, C046933 
BFLO STA 35 Rebuild  
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Buffalo Station 35 is an indoor substation with (deteriorated equipment and is a risk to 
reliable service to customers and safety for employees. The station has a design capacity 
of approximately 10 MVA. In addition to rebuilding for asset condition reasons the 
station is also reviewed for capacity upgrades.  
 
The following concerns have been cited as reasons to rebuild Buffalo Indoor Substations: 
 
• Overload Conditions; 
• Lack of Spare Transformers, oil switches and relays; 
• Transformers have unusable conservator tanks; 
• Regulators have gears worn to razor sharp edges; 
• Misoperation of relays due to age and wear; 
• Protective relay scheme is of an obsolete design; 
• Misoperation of feeder [4.16 kV] breakers due to age and accumulated wear; 
• Feeder breakers with limited interrupting capability; and 
• Poor Station cooling leading to transformer overheating 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The proposed program reduces the likelihood of customer service interruptions. This is 
done by rebuilding the substation with a more robust arrangement and removing 
equipment in poor condition from service. Additionally, these projects re-enforce the 
distribution system and improve operational flexibility while reducing outage durations. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
1) Add enough feeder ties to unload an entire transformer bay of the station to allow 
removal of all equipment in that bay while serving customers which are on feeders 
originating from that portion of the station bus.  
 
2) Install PS1 and PS2 Mobile transformer and switchgear equipment to offload (an 
entire transformer bay of the station) at a time while serving the customers which are on 
feeders originating from that portion of the station bus.  
 
DER/NWA Alternative:   
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since the

C046955,C046935, C046936 
BFLO STA 38 Rebuild 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Buffalo Station 38 is an indoor substation with deteriorated equipment and is a risk to 
reliable service to customers and safety for employees. The station has a design capacity 
of approximately 10 MVA. In addition to rebuilding for asset condition reasons the 
station is also reviewed for capacity upgrades. For an outage to one transformer, the 
remaining transformers would be 105% over the emergency rating. The 23kV cables 
supplying the transformers would also be 105% over the cables normal rating 
 
The following concerns have been cited as reasons to rebuild Buffalo Indoor Substations: 
 
• Overload Conditions; 
• Lack of Spare Transformers, oil switches and relays; 
• Transformers have unusable conservator tanks; 
• Regulators have gears worn to razor sharp edges; 
• Misoperation of relays due to age and wear; 
• Protective relay scheme is of an obsolete design; 
• Misoperation of feeder [4.16 kV] breakers due to age and accumulated wear; 
• Feeder breakers with limited interrupting capability; and 
• Poor Station cooling leading to transformer overheating 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The proposed program reduces the likelihood of customer service interruptions. This is 
done by rebuilding the substation with a more robust arrangement and removing 
equipment in poor condition from service. Additionally, these projects re-enforce the 
distribution system and improve operational flexibility while reducing outage durations. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Both indoor and outdoor construction was considered for this project. Given the main 
driver is Asset Condition, any additional alternatives such as relocation of the station 
wouldn’t make sense from a financial standpoint. Both high side and low-side cables are 
in concrete encased duct and manhole systems which limit where the station can be 
situated.  
 
DER/NWA Alternative:   
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Based on the

(MVT) 
  
  
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name:  
Asset Replacement 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C083963, C083962, C081613 
 
Description: 
 
This project calls to install a second bank at Ruth Road, with associated civil work for 
getaways, the replacement of the metal-clad due to asset condition, and distribution 
conversions and rebuilds. This work will allow us to retire the existing Karner Substation, 
eliminating a 4kV island in the middle of Colonie, NY.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
The Primary driver for retirement is asset condition. Karner TB1 has a URS LTC that is 
leaking oil from the main tank into the LTC from at least 10 posts that penetrate the 
barrier board.  
 
The Secondary driver is reliability. Karner transformers TB1 & TB2 are overloaded 
under contingency (N-1). Neighboring feeders cannot pick up the entire load due to the 
constraint of the tie ratio banks capacity to the neighboring feeders. There are existing 
MWHr violations on the neighboring substations of Rifle Range and Pinebush. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
By addressing the asset conditions at Ruth Road and Karner and removing of 4kV island 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 95 of 457

C081613, C083963, C083962, C083961, 
C052309 
Ruth Road TB2 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
this will increase the resiliency and reliability of the circuit now and in the future. Over 
25k customers in the Albany Central West area on these multiple feeders would be 
affected by this improvement to reliability. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Do nothing - Customers being served off of Karner, McKownville, Sand Creek, Patroon, 
Ruth Road are currently all vulnerable to N-1 contingencies. All customer growth and 
load will have to be curtailed in the future in this area unless improvements to capacity 
are performed. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative:  
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since the 
justification for this project is Asset Condition in nature, it was determined that this 
project will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

Program Name:  
None 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  None 
 
Description: 
 
C050746 is the rebuild of Galeville substation to 13.2kV.  The project is to install a 
9,375kVA, 34.5/13.2kV transformer with two feeder positions.  C050749 is to rebuild 
and convert the Galeville feeders to 13.2kV.  C081890 and C081891 are similar to 
Galeville, rebuild and convert the Fayette St feeders and rebuild the Fayette Street 
substation and convert it to 13.2kV respectively.  Two 20MVA transformers and 4 
feeders will be installed at Fayette Street. 
 
C082032 is the rebuild and conversion of the remaining Ash St 4.16kV feeders. 
The combination of these projects will convert three 4.16 kV stations and feeders to 13.2 
kV.   
 
Project Justification: 
 
The Ash St 4.16kV, Fayette St and Galeville substations are 34.5:4.16 kV with numerous 
asset condition issues.  All 3 substations metal clads are deteriorated and need 
replacement.  Galeville also has operating issues with the LTC, requiring the station to be 
de-energized or installing a mobile transformer installed to make repairs as the feeder ties 
are insufficient to support the load. These projects will address asset conditions and 
establish 13.2 kV feeders with numerous feeder ties with the existing 13.2 kV system in 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 98 of 457

C050746, C050749, C081980, C081981 and 
C082032 
Syracuse 5kV Area Study 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
the area. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
With new equipment,  there is a less of a chance of equipment failure, which would lead 
to customer  interruptions.  And by converting to 13,2kV, these stations will no longer be 
4.16kV islands and will strengthen the feeder ties in the area, so if there are interruptions 
then there is a greater chance the customers can be restored via feeder ties. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Rebuild the substations without converting to 13.2kV  With this option, each station 
would still be a 4.16kV island with no external feeder ties. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
Since the justification for this project is Asset Replacement in nature, it did not satisfy the 
NWA suitability criteria.

Station 61 Rebuild

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
Station 61 is an open-air distribution substation with two (2) 115-4.16kV, 9.375MVA 
Transformers with load tap changers and eight (8) distribution circuits. The substation 
was originally commissioned in the 1950's and much of the equipment is from the 
original installation. This project is for the refurbishment of Station 61 due to asset 
condition. The two (2) existing 115-4.16kV, 9.375MVA transformers will be replaced in 
kind, and a metalclad switchgear will be installed on an adjacent vacant property. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The equipment at Station 61 has asset condition issues and is built in a configuration that 
no longer meets the Company's standards. The existing equipment requires increased 
maintenance due to deterioration, no spare parts, and lack of original equipment 
manufacturer support.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacement of this equipment in a controlled manner mitigates the risk of in-service 
failure of deteriorated assets which can lead to long-term interruptions and unplanned 
customer outages. Possibility of 5827 customer impact. The additional benefit of the 
installation of modern protection, control and monitoring capabilities have the potential 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 112 of 457


--- Page 114 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 113 of 457 
C051707 
Station 61 Rebuild 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
to improve system reliability through increased communication and real time system 
information in Regional Control Centers and help to promote the increased penetration of 
Distributed Energy Resources. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Rebuild Station 61 in place 
This option will replace the two (2) 9.375MVA transformers, and eleven (11) 5kV circuit 
breakers in kind. All pin-type insulators, equipment disconnects, lightning arresters, and 
station service transformers will be replaced. A new control building will be constructed 
with modern protective relaying devices. This alternative is not recommended as the 
timeline and complexity are increased due to the construction and designs that would 
interface with existing equipment for the duration of the project. Customers served from 
Station 61 would also be supported using a mobile substation and existing feeder ties. 
These abnormal operating conditions present potential reliability risks. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
This project was reviewed for potential NWA opportunities and deemed unsuitable for 
NWA due to it being an in-kind replacement of structural and switching equipment 
driven by asset condition and without need for additional capacity.

Program Name: 
Mobile Substation Program 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  None 
 
Description: 
 
Mobile substation and transformer equipment are a critical asset class in the successful 
operation of our electrical distribution and transmission system. Mobile substations are 
relied upon for emergency situations, maintenance of substation equipment, and new 
construction projects. Mobile substations provide flexibility in their application, can be 
transported easily and connected quickly, especially if they are properly maintained and 
upgraded with a control & protection package. This program is for the purchase, 
refurbishment, or replacement of mobile substation in the Company's Upstate NY service 
territory.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
The mobile substation program allows National Grid to assess its mobile substations and 
mobile transformers in terms of health, importance factor, and rank mobile substations in 
terms of risk so that the mobile units are properly prioritized for asset replacement, 
rewind, and upgrade. Highly utilized mobile substations or uniquely configured mobile 
substations must be properly maintained, and some have been identified as needing 
refurbishment to extend their useful life. 
 
In order to improve the management of the mobile substation fleet, the Company 
conducted a review which considered system requirements, the amount of mobile usage, 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 118 of 457


--- Page 120 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 119 of 457 
C051744 
NY Mobile Substation Program 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
the uniqueness and condition of each individual unit to better understand the condition of 
all members of the fleet and their associated risks. Highly utilized mobile substations may 
present a risk if they are not properly maintained or refurbished. Based on this review, 
mobile substation control & protection upgrades, rewinds and replacements were 
recommended. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
A mobile substation is the quickest method for restoring service to customers when an 
outage occurs in a substation, typically occurring within sixteen to twenty-four hours. By 
refurbishing, upgrading, replacing and purchasing new mobile substations, as necessary, 
via system reviews and condition assessments, the risk of extended customer outages will 
be significantly reduced. In addition, properly addressing the needs of the mobile fleet 
will allow us to schedule maintenance for substation transformers in a timely manner 
since they are one of the most valuable assets on the system. Lastly, having an adequate 
number of mobile substations on hand will promote the completion of new construction 
projects on-time and on-budget. 
 
Alternatives:  
N/A 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since the 
justification for this project is Asset Condition in nature, it was determined that this 
project will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

C052344 
81452 Westminster Park Rd - Rebuild 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020

Multi-Value 
Transmission 
(MVT) 
  
  
  
X 
  
 
 
Program Name:  
Specific 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  None 
 
Description: 
 
There is an overloaded step-down ratio transformer on Westminster Park Road in 
Alexandria.  To eliminate the overload, it is necessary to retire the step-down ratio 
transformer and convert the single phase 7.62kV and single phase 4.8kV to 3 phase 
7.62kV. To facilitate growing load and improve reliability it is necessary to convert 1,800 
feet of underground wire from single phase 7.62kV #2 AL conductor to 3 phase 13.2kV 
750AL conductor from S/G # 9 (TD 6233 LN 37) to Pole 17 (TD 6233 LN 37). There 
currently exists 2 cables, a normal and backup feed for the single phase. There is a need 
for the installation of 2 cables and 2 backup cables to be installed as direct buried 
construction. This project will facilitate a future project to convert the submarine cable on 
Ferry Island to 7.62kV (the submarine cable needs to be replaced due to Asset 
Condition). 
 
Project Justification: 
 
There has been load growth in the Westminster Park area on Wellesley Island in the 1000 
islands.  The transformer that feeds Westminster Park Road is overloaded.  Switchgear 
failure on the single phase underground have highlighted the reliability need to convert 
the 1,800 feet of underground to three phase.  Converting from Westminster Park Road to 
the Ferry Island group will eliminate the overload.  Converting to 7.62kV to the Ferry 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 121 of 457


--- Page 123 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 122 of 457 
C052344 
81452 Westminster Park Rd - Rebuild 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Island group will facilitate replacing the submarine cable in the future which will 
improve reliability. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Converting from Westminster Park Road to the Ferry Island group will eliminate the 
overload.  Converting to 7.62kV to the Ferry Island group will facilitate replacing the 
submarine cable in the future which will improve reliability.  Completing this work will 
facilitate future load growth and will improve the reliability for over 240 distribution 
customers.  
 
Alternatives: 
 
The alternative is to convert single phase to Ferry Island and assume the reliability risks 
associated with not completing the 3 phase underground conversion. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since the 
conversions are required to replace the submarine cable (due to Asset Condition), it was 
determined that this project will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.  
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally 
increases the threshold for certain higher cost interconnection upgrades (such as 3V0). 
This will enable PV and ESS to interconnect at higher volumes with a lower marginal 
interconnection cost.  
 
VVO directly contributes to system efficiency, contributing to EE targets, as well as 
reducing energy required from carbon heavy generation (by reducing the total energy 
procured from NYISO). Increasing efficiency also reduces peak loading, which may 
decrease the need for fossil fuel peaking plants..

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
Station 162 is a 23-4.16kV distribution substation originally commissioned in 1952, and 
much of the equipment is from the original installation. This project will rebuild Station 
162. The (2) existing 23-4.16kV power transformers will be replaced in kind with 
standard 23-4.16kV, 4.687MVA transformers with Load Tap Changer. The two (2) 
existing 23kV oil circuit breakers will be replaced with two (2) new 23kV vacuum circuit 
breakers. The existing 5kV straight bus metalclad switchgear will be replaced with a 
main-tie-main configured switchgear with the potential to improve reliability for 
customers served from Station 162. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The equipment at Station 162 is in need of replacement due to asset condition issues. This 
includes problematic mechanisms and lack of spare parts and original manufacturer 
support. This station was originally customer built and owned and not constructed to the 
Company's standards. Majority of the station is identified as an increased risk of failure.  
test and assessment identified equipment needs to be replaced or refurbished in less than 
5 years.

          Page 124 of 457

C052706 
Station 162 Metalclad Replacement 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacement of this equipment in a controlled manner mitigates the risk of in-service 
failure of deteriorated assets which can lead to long-term interruptions and unplanned 
customer outages. The additional benefit of the installation of modern protection, control 
and monitoring capabilities have the potential to improve system reliability through 
increased communication and real time system information in Regional Control Centers 
and help to promote the increased penetration of Distributed Energy Resources. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Rebuild Station in kind 
This option will replace all existing equipment in kind. While this option would address 
the asset condition issues at Station 162 this option is not recommended as it does not 
proactively address the opportunity to improve area reliability through the 
implementation of a more reliable configuration. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
This project was reviewed for potential NWA opportunities and deemed unsuitable for 
NWA due to it being an in-kind replacement of structural and switching equipment 
driven by asset condition and without need for additional capacity.

Program Name:  
Load Relief 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C053137, CD00893, C043952 
 
Description: 
 
The recommended response to load growth and asset condition issues on Rensselaer 
Substation is to construct a new 115-13.2 kV substation on Forbes Avenue in the City of 
Rensselaer, NY, south of the New York State Thruway.  
 
This substation will be supplied by the Riverside - Reynolds Road #4 115 kV 
transmission line, tapped using a loop-in-loop-out (LILO) arrangement. 
 
The station will consist of two 115-13.2 kV power transformers and four 13.2 kV 
distribution feeders: two that will supply the future De Laet's Landing development and 
two that will permit the retirement of the existing 34.5-13.2 kV Rensselaer Substation, 
approximately two miles southwest of the proposed substation. 
 
This project will result in infrastructure upgrades to four different areas of the electric 
system: transmission line, substation, distribution line, and the retirement of the 
Rensselaer Substation. These sections describe, in more detail, the investment required in 
these areas. 
 
 
 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 129 of 457


--- Page 131 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 130 of 457 
C053137, CD00893, C043592 
Forbes station 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The electric transmission and distribution system cannot support the addition of 
2.5MVA load addition due to existing capacity constraints. It was determined that the 
best response to this concern is to construct a new 115-13.2 kV substation on Forbes 
Avenue in the City of Rensselaer, NY to add distribution capacity to the area.  
 
This substation will fully support the De Laet’s Landing development project, resolve 
asset condition issues and capacity constraints at the 34.5-13.2 kV Rensselaer Substation. 
Resolve existing capacity constraints on the 34.5 kV sub-transmission system by 
transferring load to the 115kV transmission system and provide capacity for future load 
growth in the area. 
 
In addition, a secondary driver is reliability due to asset condition issues.  The Rensselaer 
Substation is located in a flood plain and has had numerous failures.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
 The proposed substation will fully support the De Laet’s Landing development, resolve 
asset condition and age concerns at Rensselaer Substation and on the 34.5 kV sub-
transmission system, and accommodate future load growth in the area. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
An alternative recommended the installation of a new 115-13.2 kV substation on the 
same property as the Recommended Option. However, this alternative recommends 
installing only one half (or one side) of the substation, with provisions for the second half 
to be built at a later date. It is proposed that this substation be built with one 115-13.2 kV 
24/32/40 MVA transformer and one indoor metal-clad switchgear (IMCS) unit with one 
station capacitor banks, four distribution feeder positions, and one spare position. 
  
This alternative is not recommended because it is limiting in terms of capacity and 
operational flexibility in the event of a system contingency.  
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  This project 
is driven by both asset condition and system capacity. The station cannot be deferred to 
ensure the asset condition need it met, but the system capacity portion of the need drove 
the Company to develop a hybrid solution to meet the total need. The combination of the 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 130 of 457

Forbes station 
Version 1 –

Rensselaer NWA and Forbes Station will meet area needs in both the short- and long-
term planning horizons.  
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally 
increases the threshold for certain higher cost interconnection upgrades (such as 3V0). 
This will enable PV and ESS to interconnect at higher volumes with a lower marginal 
interconnection cost.  
 
VVO directly contributes to system efficiency, contributing to EE targets, as well as 
reducing energy required from carbon heavy generation (by reducing the total energy 
procured from NYISO). Increasing efficiency also reduces peak loading, which may 
decrease the need for fossil fuel peaking plants.
