Program Name:  
Load Relief 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
Rebuild Turnpike Rd., Brownell Corners Rd., & State Hwy. 22 as necessary to convert to 
13.2 kV operation. Reconductor where smaller than 2/0 CU with 336.4 MCM AL. Move 
to the road where appropriate. Add 1/0 neutral where necessary. Convert side taps. Fuse 
side taps and CSP transformers. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The Union St. 54 was the seventh worst performing feeder in 2013. It was discovered 
while reviewing the Union St. 54 feeder for the Reliability Report that the 1,500 kVA 
ratio transformer on Turnpike Rd., which has a source side line recloser had a peak load 
of 1,939 kVA in 2013. A review of the recloser load data today indicates that the ratio 
peaked at 2,044 kVA in January of 2014. Therefore, the ratio was loaded in excess of 
136% of nameplate and in imminent danger of failing. This 1,500kVA ratio transformer 
was replaced in 2016 with a 2,500kVA ratio transformer. A number of industrial 
customers in the area are expanding, which will increase load on this ratio transformer. 
The 2,500kVA ratio is the largest ratio transformer that National Grid utilizes. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Load Relief / Reliability

Alternatives: 
 
Load continues to increase downstream of this step-down ratio transformer. Since the 
2,500kVA ratio is the largest ratio transformer that National Grid utilizes, the only option 
would be to complete the rebuild and conversion to 13.2kV. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA. Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally 
increases the threshold for certain higher cost interconnection upgrades (such as 3V0). 
This will enable PV and ESS to interconnect at higher volumes with a lower marginal 
interconnection cost.  
 
VVO directly contributes to system efficiency, contributing to EE targets, as well as

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
Avenue A is a metal enclosed substation comprised of multiple sections. The first section 
houses the 38kV gear which contains two 34.5kV incoming lines and a tie breaker. Also, 
within are protective fuses for transformers #1 and 2. The transformers are throat-
connected to the switchgear units.  
 
There are two 5kV metal-clads that contain 3 feeders each, and a tie in between. The 
transformers are 34.5/4.16kV, 5/6.25MVA in size with LTCs. The majority of the 
equipment was manufactured by Westinghouse from 1954 and 1958. 
 
This project calls to replace the transformers along with both the 34.5kV and 5kV 
switchgears and uprate the station to13.2kV and increase the capacity of the station.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
 The metal-clad switchgears at Avenue A have a history of flashovers resulting in 
tracking on the bus and damage to equipment and has no/limited spare parts and lack of 
original manufacturer support. Significant rusting has also been identified on the 
equipment. Engineering is in the process of identifying all concerns of deterioration 
through field inspections and engineering analysis. The asset condition report showed the 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 145 of 457


--- Page 147 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 146 of 457 
C056609, C081583 
Avenue A 291 Metal Clad replacement 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
need to replace the 34.5kV switchgear and the 4.16kV metal clad due to numerous asset 
condition issues. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacement of this equipment in a controlled manner mitigates the risk of in-service 
failure of deteriorated assets which can lead to long-term interruptions and unplanned 
customer outages. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Do nothing / Replace MC or 34.5kV transformers upon failure 
This alternative is not recommended as it presents a higher potential for in service failure, 
and unplanned customer outages.   
 
Alternative 2: Replace all of Avenue A 4.16kV with new 13.2kV from New Krumkill 
Substation. This would just leave the replacement of the 34.5kV metal-clad and could 
become a set of open air disconnects in place of a metal-clad. The 34.5kV metal-clad 
replacement will still be needed due to asset condition issues. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
Since this justification for this project is Asset Replacement in nature, it was determined 
that non-wire alternatives (NWA) does not meet the NWA suitability criteria.

Study Report Name (s): Avenue A Asset Condition Report 
Final ACR report Completed: 12/31/2018 
 
STATION EQUIPMENT RECOMMENDATIONS 
The following equipment is recommended for replacement or upgrades in order of 
priority. 
a. The switchgear units have significant surface rust. No rust holes were noted. Rating a 3 
due to history of flashovers on 38kV bus and insulators, oil leaks, and uniqueness of 
38kV switchgear.  
b. Metal Clad substation. All equipment is metal enclosed. High arc flash energy on 
34.5kV bus: 35.8 cal/cm2 (HRC 4). Arc flash on 4.16kV bus is 9.0 cal/cm2 (HRC 3) 
c. 34.5kV Buss Cascade Tracking on insulators caused outage in 2016, insulators 
replaced. Problem/trouble noted 34.5kV 99D bus in 2010. 
d. PT’s Replace butyl type PT’s. 2.5 gal oil added to LN10 ph C PT in 2009.

Program Name:  
Specific 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C064446: Tuller Hill Dline - 13kV Getaway,  
 
C065087: Tuller Hill 115kV Tap Replacement 
 
Description: 
 
This project is to rebuild Tuller Hill substation (C056611) with a new 115-13.8kV Load 
Tap Changer Transformer and second 13.8kV feeder position. This includes expanding 
the station yard and fence north of the substation to allow for installation of new 
equipment in the existing National Grid right-of-way. The existing Metalclad will be 
replaced with an open-air configuration. The existing 115kV station equipment and meter 
house will be removed.  
 
Transmission line work (C065087) is required to build a new tap off the 115kV Cortland-
Clarks Corners #1 line to supply the new portion of the substation. The existing 115kV 
tap will be used for a mobile substation connection.  
 
Distribution line work (C064446) required for this alternative will include a new getaway 
pole and all 13.2kV conductors to connect to the existing Distribution circuit. The 
existing line recloser located two spans south of the existing station will be removed as 
part of this project.

Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 151 of 457 
C056611 
Tuller Hill Substation Rebuild 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Metalclad switchgear units manufactured prior to the 1970's are being targeted for 
replacement due to their asset condition. The enclosure is not of a robust design; the 
gaskets are thin and deteriorated allowing moisture ingress. These types of enclosures 
have a history of failure and are being addressed under the Metalclad Switchgear 
Strategy. 
 
The Tuller Hill transformer is throat connected, which makes it difficult to replace with a 
conventional spare if the need arose. It is also difficult to make any necessary 
replacements to the low side protection equipment. Due to condition issues related to the 
circuit breaker, the circuit breaker was by-passed and a recloser was installed outside the 
substation in the fall of 2010. The high side protection consists of three 115kV fuses, 
which Substation Operations and Maintenance personnel have expressed is a safety 
concern. If the 115kV fuses require replacement, there would be potential 
safety/clearance concerns. The breaker by-pass switch is also in poor condition and the 
entire metalclad has issues with the standoff insulators, such that the bus may flash over. 
 
Substation Operations and Maintenance personnel in the Central Region have indicated 
that this substation is very difficult to maintain due to its asset condition issues and 
obsolescence. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The upgrade of Tuller Hill Substation and replacement of equipment in poor condition is 
in line with National Grid's goal to maintain reliability and a sustainable network. 
 
The upgraded station layout will increase safety for employees during switching and 
maintenance operations. The removal of equipment in poor condition will also maintain 
reliability. Potential hazards associated with safety will be reduced due to the 
replacement of the insulators. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Rebuild Substation with Non-LTC Transformer and Voltage Regulators: 
Replace existing substation equipment with one new 115-13.8kV Non-LTC transformer, 
one 115kV circuit switcher, one 15kV recloser, three voltage regulators, control and 
metering equipment, and an upgraded control house. The fence would be expanded south 
of the existing substation in order to allow provisions for a mobile. The existing 
transformer, 115kV circuit switch and fuse, control and metering equipment, and meter 
house will be removed. The existing line recloser would be removed by distribution line. 
Installation would be delayed until equipment removal is complete. In addition, this 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10)

design does not provide room for future expansion. This alternative is not recommended, 
as it will require a mobile substation for the entire duration of demolition and 
construction. 
 
Alternative 2: Rebuild Substation with LTC Transformer: Rebuild existing substation 
equipment with one new 115-13.8kV LTC transformer, one 115kV circuit switcher, one 
15kV recloser, control and metering equipment, and an upgraded control house. The 
fence will be expanded south of the existing substation in order to allow provisions for a 
mobile. The existing transformer, 115kV circuit switch and fuse, control and metering 
equipment, and meter house will be removed. The existing line recloser will be removed 
by distribution line. Installation would be delayed until equipment removal is complete. 
This design does not provide room for future expansion. This alternative is not 
recommended, as it will require a mobile substation for the entire duration of demolition 
and construction 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since the 
justification for this project is Asset Condition in nature, it was determined that this 
project will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
McKownville substation is a 115-13.2kV,  two (2) transformer station with ratings of 
33MVA and 22.4MVA respectively. There are seven (7) distribution feeders located in 
two (2) metalclad switchgears supplying the customer demand. This project is for the 
replacement of equipment at McKownville Substation with asset condition issues. This 
includes replacing the 22.4MVA power transformer #2, the 115kV circuit switcher, and 
the 15kV metalclad #2. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The 115kV circuit switcher has posted manufacturer warnings, and no original 
manufacturer support. Transformer #2 is a 115-13.2kV 22.4MVa unit that has been 
subjected to several through faults and is known to have heating issues in the load tap 
changer, the unit has known PCB contamination. Metalclad switchgear #2 has been 
observed to have rusting, and signs of water ingress increasing the likelihood of an in-
service failure. It has been identified that Circuit Switcher 6299 has a high probability of 
failure and should be replaced now.  Transformer #2 and Metalclad #2 have been 
identified of a high probability of failure if not replaced or refurbished within 5 years.

Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacement of this equipment in a controlled manner mitigates the risk of in-service 
failure of deteriorated assets which can lead to long-term interruptions and unplanned 
customer outages which could possibly impact 5,586 customers.  The additional benefit 
of the installation of modern protection, control and monitoring capabilities have the 
potential to improve system reliability through increased communication and real time 
system information in Regional Control Centers and help to promote the increased 
penetration of Distributed Energy Resources. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Do nothing / Replace upon failure 
This alternative is not recommended as it presents a higher potential for in service failure, 
and unplanned customer outages.  
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
This project was reviewed for potential NWA opportunities and deemed unsuitable for 
NWA due to it being an in-kind replacement of structural and switching equipment 
driven by asset condition and without need for additional capacity.

Program Name: 
Metalclad Replacement Strategy 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  None 
 
Description: 
 
Replace the existing metalclad at Pine Grove substation with open air bus and circuit 
breakers.  Also, replace TB1 with a 40MVA transformer to match TB2, which was 
replaced with a 40MVA under damage/failure in the past.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
The metalclad switchgear at Pine Grove has numerous asset condition issues.  The major 
equipment with level 3 condition (items that should be replaced in 5 years) are: all of the 
13.2kV circuit breakers, the metalclad and the 13.2kV bus work. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
A new 13.2 kV bus will reduce the change of customer interruptions due to equipment 
failure from the existing metal clad.  
 
Alternatives: 
 
Replace with another metal clad at Pine Grove.  Customers served from Pine Grove 
would need to be supported using a mobile substation and existing feeder ties for this 
work.  These abnormal operating conditions present potential reliability risk.

Description: 
 
Station 140 is a 115-13.2kV distribution substation consisting of two (2) 33.6MVA 
Transformers with Load Tap Changers and eight (8) distribution circuits originally 
commissioned in 1973. This project is to address existing asset condition issues at Station 
140. Transformer #1, (2) 115kV circuit switchers, and Metalclad switchgears #1 and #2 
are in need of replacement. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Transformer #1 has elevated levels of combustible gases and is on an accelerated 
Dissolved Gas Analysis testing cycle to more closely monitor the unit. The two (2) 
115kV circuit switchers have published manufacturer warnings and no original 
manufacturer support. The 15kV metalclad switchgears have been observed to have 
advanced stages of rust and suspected of water ingress. The breakers inside of the 
metalclad units are targeted for replacement due to known operational issues and lack of 
manufacturer support.  The condition of the equipment list was identified as asset likely 
to need replacement or refurbishment in less than 5 years due to high risk of failure.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacement of this equipment in a controlled manner mitigates the risk of in-service

failure of deteriorated assets which can lead to long-term interruptions and unplanned 
customer outages with a possible 7452 customers impacted.  The additional benefit of the 
installation of modern protection, control and monitoring capabilities have the potential 
to improve system reliability through increased communication and real time system 
information in Regional Control Centers and help to promote the increased penetration of 
Distributed Energy Resources. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Build Greenfield Station in new location 
This alternative is not currently recommended as it will require procurement of property 
in a densely populated area, along with considerable amounts of transmission and 
distribution configuration which would not be cost effective. 
 
Alternative 2: Replace Transformer #1 and Breakers 
This alternative is not recommended as it would leave deteriorated equipment in service 
and does not mitigate the customer reliability risk associate with leaving deteriorated 
equipment in service. The metalclad switchgears will also require additional resources 
and funding to maintain. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
This project was reviewed for potential NWA opportunities and deemed unsuitable for

C046588 - Lakeville Station Retirement 
 
Description: 
 
This project involves installing a new (6) feeder switchgear at Sonora Way Station to 
supply the (2) existing feeders and install (3) new 13.2kV feeders to off load Lakeville 
Station after converting Lakeville feeders to 13.2kV. This will allow the retirement of 
Lakeville Station due to asset condition. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The driver for the project is based on the retirement of Lakeville Station 40 due to 
significant asset condition issues per asset condition report - Major assets need to be 
replaced in 5 years by (2024) the 4.8kV transformer bank and PTs, 4.8kV & 34.5kV 
switches, buses, bus supporting steel structure and arresters. Also, 4.8kV circuit breakers 
and station service transformer within 5-10 years.  
 
Sonora Way Station was built in a temp fashion in 2015 to relieve the failed Mobile 7W 
installed to relieve overloaded Lakeville & Geneseo stations. Installing new Switchgear

& feeders at Sonora Way Station will allow Lakeville load to be transferred when the 
Lakeville feeders are converted to 13.2kV.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This least cost option benefits customers by addressing the asset conditions at Lakeville 
Station, converts 4.8kV feeders to 13.2kV, increases reliability through creation of 
13.2kV feeder ties, reduces load at risk during N-1 contingences at North Lakeville 
34.5kV Station, relieves overall loading at Livonia Station, enables additional hosting 
capacity and enables the use of an existing mobile connection at Sonora way Station. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Option 1 - Rebuild Lakeville Station.  
 
Option 2 - Expand Sonora Way Station for Additional Capacity w/ 2nd Bank. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

Associated Funding Numbers: 
 
C064766 - Chrisler Rebuilt Station - Distribution Getaways 
C057132 - Chrisler Ave 25737 Conversion 
C057133 - Chrisler Ave 25735 
C080418 - Emmet St Station - Distribution Retirement 
 
Description: 
 
Replace existing 34.5/4.16kV, 3.0/3.65 MVA banks, 4.16kV switchgear, and existing 
control shed at Chrisler Ave. Station with one larger 12/16/20 MVA, 34.5/13.2 kV bank 
with LTC and new 15 kV switchgear fully enclosed in an outdoor walk thru switchgear 
building complete with all new AC/DC station auxiliaries and relaying panels 
 
The Emmet St 4kV substation will eventually be scaled down to a sub-transmission 
switching station with all distribution retired within. It's 3.9 MVA of load (Summer 2016 
peak) will be absorbed by Chrisler Ave, Weaver St, and McClellan St feeders. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The Chrisler Ave Station’s existing metalclad switchgear is of a 1965 vintage that is 
prone to partial discharge and to bus failures. In addition, the circuit breakers are in poor 
condition, obsolete, and underrated for fault duty. The two existing transformers are 
34.5/4.16kV 3.0/3.65 MVA banks with LTCs. 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 175 of 457


--- Page 177 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 176 of 457 
C068290 
Chrisler Rebuilt Station - Station 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
 
TB2 has elevated hydrogen gasses, elevated moisture in the oil and windings, and a low 
voltage breakdown of the insulating oil. TB1 has no gassing or insulation abnormalities. 
However, both transformers are connected in parallel with no means of isolation. 
Therefore, a fault in either of the bank’s windings will take out both banks. 
 
Meanwhile at Emmet St Station, Metalclad #1 & #2 are 1947 vintage equipment with 
5kV GE FK OCB’s, with obsolete mechanisms, faulty rectifier circuits, and cloth control 
wiring that is breaking and cracking. The 34.5 kV breakers are obsolete, have a lack of 
spare parts and are slow to operate. There are also cap-pin insulators present in this 
station that are targeted for replacement due to a high failure rate 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
These projects will reduce the chances of long term outages caused by equipment failure 
due to asset conditions. The project will also eliminate the 4kV islands in the middle of 
Schenectady, creating strong 13.2kV feeder ties between Chrisler Ave Station and the 
surrounding stations (McClellan, Weaver St., Watt St., Curry Rd., and Lynn St.) This will 
greatly improve the reliability within the area by providing more switching opportunities 
from more 13.2kV sources 
 
Alternatives: 
 
The alternative of rebuilding Emmet St and retiring Chrisler Ave was considered, as it 
was felt that the switching capabilities of the Sub-T at Emmet St would provide more 
reliability over the radial Chrisler Ave Station. However, the Asset Management group in 
Waltham confirmed that thermal constraints on the 34.5 cables at Emmet St would limit 
the size of the transformer to only a 7.0/9.375 MVA bank. With a lack of capacity 
already a concern within the Schenectady Area, this was not considered a viable option. 
 
The project was recommended since Chrisler St Station will provide larger capacity in 
the area. It upgrades the existing switchgear with a new outdoor metalclad switchgear 
building and adds EMS capability. The costs are considered reasonable among the other 
options in the conceptual engineering report prepared for this station 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since the 
justification for this project is Asset Condition in nature, it was determined that this 
project will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

Associated Funding Numbers: 
 
C064726 - Lasher Road Substation 
C064727 - Lasher Road Land, Bldg, and Roadway. 
C065886 - Lasher Road Substation - Land E&D 
C043672 - Lasher Road Transmission Line 
CD00897 - Lasher Road New Station Dist. Getaways 
C068326 - Lasher Road Feeder 52 OH 
C068348 – Lasher Road Feeder 53 OH 
 
Description: 
 
Create the Lasher Road 53 feeder by converting the existing Randall Road 57 feeder from 
4.8kV to 13.2kV construction. The over 3 miles of rebuild/conversion would be from 
Randall Road in the north down to Lake Hill in the south.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
The primary driver for the overall Lasher Road Station project is to relieve exposure to 
post-contingency thermal overloading of the Luther Forest-Eastover Road #308, 115kV 
line with planned expansions in the Northeast region. 
 
The secondary driver, which is apropos for this specific project, is reliability on the 
distribution side. The Ballston, Swaggertown, and Grooms Road Substations have either 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 179 of 457


--- Page 181 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 180 of 457 
C068348 
Lasher Road - 53 Feeder OH 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
outage exposure criteria violations or feeders that are approaching their summer normal 
ratings. The three new 13.2kV feeders from Lasher Road will address those concerns. 
 
The Lasher Road 53 feeder will provide strong 13.2kV ties to Swaggertown 52 and will 
allow for the retirement of the Shore Road 34.5/4.8kV substation and the Randall Road 
34.5/4.8kV substation. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This project would increase reliability of service and National Grid’s operating flexibility 
in terms of supplying the 115kV system which serves the Northeast Region and Global 
Foundries from Luther Forest Substation.  Global Foundries has publicly disclosed the 
possibility of future expansion plans, which would further increase their energy demand.  
Global Foundries is one of National Grid’s largest customers and this project is 
recommended to provide adequate transmission capacity for serving the expected load of 
Fab 8.1. 
 
On the distribution, it will greatly improve the reliability within the area by providing 
more switching opportunities from more 13.2kV sources. It will also alleviate the low 
voltage concerns on the existing 4.8kV Randall Rd 46357 feeder. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Specifically, for the 53 feeder, the option of leaving it as a 4.8kV feeder would be an 
alternative. However, it would not resolve the lack of feeder ties in the area or address the 
low voltage concerns on the feeder. It would also make it more difficult to retire the 
Randall Road and Shore Road Substations. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since the 
justification for this project is Asset Condition in nature, it was determined that this 
project will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

C069886, C069947, C069927, C084979, 
C084981, C084982 
New LED (Central, East, West NY) 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Type: 
 
T SubT D 
 
 
X 
 
 
Spending Rationale: 
Asset Condition 
Communications/ 
Control Systems 
Customer 
Requests/ Public 
Requirements 
DER Electric 
System 
Access 
Damage/ Failure 
  
  
X 
  
  
 
Non- 
Infrastructure 
Reliability 
Resiliency 
System 
Capacity 
Multi-Value 
Transmission 
(MVT) 
  
  
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name:  
D/SUB T_New Business 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C069886 (Central), C069947 (East), C069927  
(West), C084979 (East - Decorative), C084981 (Central - Decorative), C084981 (West - 
Decorative) 
 
Description: 
 
This LED conversion program is in direct support of the Company’s P.S.C. No. 214 tariff 
revision providing for the conversion to LED technology of existing non-LED roadway 
streetlight fixtures. The LED conversion program is intended to deliver energy savings to 
the Company’s UNY municipal customers and requires participating municipal 
customers to pay down the undepreciated net book value (NBV) of the targeted roadway 
fixtures within their municipality. These funds will be used for the purpose of preliminary 
systems and process work, capital purchases, design, installation, removal and full 
implementation in support of the early adopting municipalities taking advantage of the 
recently approved offering for LED Lighting in Upstate NY. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Our current Street Lighting product line is  a combination of High Pressure Sodium or 
LED (Light Emitting Diodes) luminaires and their associated fixtures. The UNY Outdoor 
Lighting LED Conversion Program has been operating since June 1, 2016. The UNY 
Outdoor Lighting business has endorsed a business strategy, aligned with the 
Corporation’s sustainability and climate change initiatives and acknowledged by the NYS

C069886, C069947, C069927, C084979, 
C084981, C084982 
New LED (Central, East, West NY) 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
PSC to adopt a proactive transition to facilitate the use of established energy efficient 
lighting technologies.   
 
Investing in this LED solution allows us to respond to our customers’ desire for a more 
energy efficient and lower maintenance solution. A Company-owned LED offering also 
provides an alternative for customers otherwise interested in purchasing the lighting 
systems that serve them in order to convert them to LED. 
 
This program also supports Niagara Mohawk Power Corporation’s ("the Company”) 
street lighting tariff (P.S.C. 214) LED conversion program approved by the New York 
State Public Service 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Participating municipal customers will reduce their annual energy consumption, equating 
to greenhouse gas reductions. Additional benefits include enhanced customer satisfaction 
and cost reduction, visible commitment to efficiency, improved safety through enhanced 
visibility, decreased light trespass and pollution, reduction in mercury and other chemical 
components requiring hazardous disposal, and lower maintenance costs via longer fixture 
life. 
 
The Company estimates an average customer payback period to be five to eight years, 
depending on quantity, distribution, and age of currently installed infrastructure, when 
accounting for the energy savings resulting from LED conversions. Additionally, there is 
a potential for greater customer savings if the energy supply prices are above forecast 
levels. 
 
Alternatives:  
None 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA. Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

C046367 - Market Hill Substation Retirement 
 
Description: 
 
Construct a new 115kV/13.2kV substation comprising a single 15/20/25MVA 
transformer and four feeders. Construct a loop of the Meco-Rotterdam #10 115kV 
transmission line into the station to serve the distribution transformer. Retire the existing 
Market Hill substation and portion of the 69kV double circuit north of the Mohawk River 
that supplies Market Hill.

None

C069909 
Maple Ave - New Feeder 52 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This project retires the Market Hill substation in Amsterdam, addressing the asset 
condition issues at that station. Additionally, the construction of the new station and 
feeders improves reliability by eliminating a 4.16kV island within the City of Amsterdam 
and eliminating 6.1 miles of double circuit sub-transmission line. This project will reduce 
outage exposure at the Church St. substation. The new station will be located close to an 
area of commercial load growth along NYS Route 30 in the Town of Amsterdam. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Rebuild the existing Market Hill station 
 
Rebuild the existing Market Hill substation as a 69-13.2kV substation with one 
transformer and four feeders. This project was not chosen due to the presence of a gas 
regulator station on the south side of the property, and limited space within the substation 
site. Additionally, this alternative would require refurbishment of the 69 kV double 
circuit supplying the station and would complicate future conversion of the Mohawk 
Valley 69 kV system to 115 kV, as recommended by the 69kV System Reconfiguration 
Proposal in Mohawk Valley Transmission Area to address issues on the sub-transmission 
system due to flooding at Amsterdam and Canajoharie substations. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA. Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA): N/A

Program Name:

Associated Funding Numbers: C074909 Station, C074906 Sub-T, C074911 D-Line 
 
Description: 
 
C074909 Substation 
Install a new 15kV Insulated Metalclad Switchgear (IMCS) with three(3) 1200A main 
breakers, one (1) 1200A tie breakers, and eight (8) 1200A feeder breakers. This will also 
include the purchase of three (3) new 10 MVA 23:13.2 kV transformers, three (3) 23kV 
circuit breakers and three (3) 23kV fused disconnect switches. 
 
Retirement of the existing 23:13.2 kV transformers, the existing temporary station 2738 
switchgear and all equipment associated with the temporary substation will be removed. 
 
C074911 Distribution Line 
Remove existing 13.2kV feeder cables 273851 & 273852 from the substation to the first 
manhole. Install trench and conduit from new IMCS to MH in Perry St.  Install approx. 
200’ of new 500kcmil Cu UG cable and make ties to existing feeders.  Extend UG from 
new station approx. 2900' along Perry Street and South Park Ave. and rebuild/convert 
approx. 8500' of OH along O’Connell St  to create new feeder 301252 and relieve F4168.  
Extend UG from new station approx. 700' along Perry St and rebuild approx. 5000' of OH 
along Smith Street to create new feeder 301256 and relieve F5964 & 5965. 
 
C033472 Sub Transmission line 
Extend 23kV feeder cables 13S, 14S & 32S to the location of the new substation.

C074909 Station, C074906 Sub-T, C074911 
D-Line 
Station 3012 Substation 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification:  
 
 
None 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This new station will enable us to continue supporting  the load requests in the 
Larkinville District in the city of Buffalo which has had several large commercial 
customers with new services and anticipating several others. Large commercial 
customers require capacity which exceeds what is normally available on a 4.16 kV 
feeder. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Two Transformer substation - Metal Clad 
Install a (8) feeder 15 kV feeder metal enclosed switchgear in the existing 59 building. 
The scope of this option is the same as the recommended plan but would not install the 
third transformer at this time.  This option was not recommended due to current load 
requirements pending in the area.   
 
Alternative 2: Install new 115-13.2kV station to be built with 15/20/25 transformer, one 
(1) Metal Clad at the former Terminal Station D location on Bailey Ave.  Two feeders 
would be installed with the capacity to expand with an additional bank and added feeders.  
This option was not recommended due to increase costs related to Transmission work 
involved as well as the increased distribution costs since the location was much further 
from load center. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA. Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally 
increases the threshold for certain higher cost interconnection upgrades (such as 3V0).

D-Line 
Station 3012 Substation 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
This will enable PV and ESS to interconnect at higher volumes with a lower marginal 
interconnection cost.  
 
VVO directly contributes to system efficiency, contributing to EE targets, as well as 
reducing energy required from carbon heavy generation (by reducing the total energy 
procured from NYISO). Increasing efficiency also reduces peak loading, which may 
decrease the need for fossil fuel peaking plants.

C076122 - EMS/RTU Telecom - D- Line NY WEST 
 
Description: 
 
These projects upgrade and extend the statewide substation communications network. It 
includes providing private fiber connectivity to a number of larger stations as well as new 
public or private communications capability to stations that currently have no connection. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The more complex distribution systems needed to support grid modernization objectives 
will require multiple new systems and technologies in the field that in many cases rely on 
some type of communications capability As new systems are deployed, the data load on 
backhaul networks that transport data from the field increases. By expanding the fiber 
network, we can increase capacity and decrease the need for services from public 
telecommunications providers, lowering the cost and complexity of deploying substation

However, in locations where private network expansion is not cost effective, the

Customer Benefit: 
 
This project supports multiple other projects and initiatives. Each of those projects has its 
own customer benefits, which are enabled by the new telecom infrastructure deployed by 
this project. Customers benefit directly through the reduction in on-going 
telecommunications costs when converting substations from public carriers to a private 
solution. In addition, a private solution offers more utility control in operations, 
maintenance, troubleshooting and repair resulting in improved reliability and security. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
The Company’s proposed hybrid communications solution includes a mix of private and 
public communications to provide a balance between operational needs and cost 
effectiveness. This will result in the flexibility to implement the capabilities required at 
the lowest cost possible.

Program Name:  
D/Sub Substation RTU 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: 
 
C076123 - EMS/RTU Installs - NY EAST 
C076124 - EMS/RTU Installs - NY CENTRAL  
C076125 - EMS/RTU Installs - NY WEST 
 
Description: 
 
The Company has been expanding the integration of substations with its SCADA system 
as part of each year’s capital investment plan. An element of the Company’s initial 
Distributed System Implementation Plan (DSIP) is to increase the pace of integrating 
substation monitoring and control. These projects provide for the additional Remote 
Terminal Units (RTUs) and related infrastructure at targeted substations presently lacking 
remote monitoring and control capabilities. RTUs within substations communicate with 
the Energy Management System (EMS) at the regional control centers and provide the 
means to leverage real time system data that provide operational intelligence and reduce 
response time to abnormal conditions. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
RTUs will allow for remote operation and management of the system at these substations 
providing benefits in contingency response and recovery and thus improving performance 
and reliability. In addition, RTUs are key components of automation and modernization 
of the Company’s infrastructure.

Customer Benefit: 
 
This program provides a means to leverage operational intelligence and reduce response 
time to abnormal conditions through real-time monitoring and control. The program also 
enables the distribution automation and distribution management necessary to enable 
other modernization strategies, all of which will enhance the service to customers. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Do nothing. This option would not improve situational awareness at stations presently 
lacking EMS / RTU facilities, nor enhance the system data required for DER integration. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA. Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):

efficiently interconnect and operate DER. These technologies enable lower cost 
interconnections by allowing the Company to explore techniques such as flexible 
interconnection and advanced dispatch for programs like NWA. Telecom and Control 
(consistency in name of program) will enable more PV and ESS to interconnect to the 
system more efficiently. 
 
EMS / SCADA gives the company higher visibility into system conditions, allowing 
planners to make fewer assumptions, and be less conservative when interconnecting DG. 
This may lead to higher penetration of DG at lower costs

Program Name: 
Distribution Line Sensors/Monitors 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: 
 
C076144 - Feeder Monitors/Sensors - NY WEST 
C076143 - Feeder Monitors/Sensors - NY CENTRAL 
C076142 - Feeder Monitors/Sensors - NY EAST 
 
Description: 
 
This project involves the deployment of National Grid approved overhead line sensors 
throughout the distribution electric system in Upstate NY, providing near real-time 
measurements enabling engineering and operations personnel to better manage the 
electric delivery system. Sensor measurements will yield interval feeder loading and 
voltage information that will foster more granular evaluations of system performance in 
support of distribution system planning, hosting capacity analysis and consideration of 
non-wires alternative solutions. These sensors will provide 8760 hour information in 
locations in which only peak hour information is currently available. Once ADMS is 
implemented the sensors will transition from EMS to ADMS and will be used to update 
the real time power flow model. 
 
The proposal is to deploy ~750 feeder sensors to enable the deployment of at least 1 
feeder sensor per feeder across NY. The number of sensors accounts for the following 
projects going on in parallel and ensures no overlap:  
 
• Current feeder sensor installations 
• Substation RTU  
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 202 of 457


--- Page 204 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 203 of 457 
C076144, C076143, C076142 
FEEDER MONITORS / SENSORS 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
• VVO/CVR and FLISR  
• Smart meter and AMI deployment 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The primary driver of this project is to provide more reliable and higher quality power at 
an overall lower cost to our customers by enabling engineering and operational 
personnel to have a greater degree of insight into the electric delivery system. 
 
Achieving the ability to load monitor step-down “ratio” transformers will provide the 
necessary data to evaluate and initiate system improvement opportunities. Upgraded 
transformers will reduce premature failures, mitigate voltage irregularities, reduce 
emergency manpower response, increase customer satisfaction and improve reliability 
indices. Feeder monitoring will provide essential loading to allow engineers and system 
control personnel to maximize circuit capabilities by providing a cost-effective method of 
measuring current, voltage, real and reactive power.  
 
Taken together, these drivers help satisfy the expectations of our customers and meet 
the intent of current and forthcoming regulatory orders. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Sensor technology will provide operational benefits; allow the distribution system to be 
operated in a more efficient manner resulting in lower costs to customers. The available 
data will enable better decisions regarding diagnosing and localizing a fault or load 
swapping during peak load conditions. Capturing loading information on an 8760 hourly 
basis across the entire year will also allow actual system data to be used for DER 
interconnection studies in locations where engineering assumptions on light load levels 
are required today. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Do Nothing 
The Company will continue to use the only data made available from existing sources 
such as line devices (e.g. reclosers) and station RTU’s where available. The reasoning for 
rejecting this alternative is, insufficient data for many feeders. Requiring engineers and 
operators to continue current practices of modeling and estimating system loading 
conditions, making critical system and asset decisions incurring the risks associated with 
those methods.

DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA. Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

Program Name:  
Load Relief 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: 
 
C046798 - Sodeman Road Station - New Station 
C046796 - Sodeman Road - Feeder Getaways 
C076785 – Sodeman Road 51 Feeder  
C076794 – Sodeman Road 52 Feeder  
C076796 – Sodeman Road 53 Feeder 
C076797 – Sodeman Road 54 Feeder  
 
Description: 
 
This project consists of the installation of a 15/20/25 MVA, 115/13.2 kV LTC 
transformer, a four-feeder metal-clad switchgear, extension of 115 kV taps 
(approximately 400 feet) to a new station to be called Sodeman Road. The associated 
distribution consisting of four (4) distribution feeder getaways, reconductoring and/or 
conversion of approximately 6 miles of overhead distribution and the reconfiguration of 
the area distribution feeders, and the retirement of the 34.5 kV Rock City Falls substation 
are including in the associated funding projects. The drivers of this project are load relief 
and reliability. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
This project addresses multiple issues at multiple substations beginning with resolving a 
MWHr violation at the Ballston Station for the loss of TB3, reducing it from 386 MWHrs 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 206 of 457


--- Page 208 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 207 of 457 
C077020 
Network Transformer DGA 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
to 78 MWHrs. It also resolves a SN loading issue for Ballston TB3 which is projected to 
reach 101% of its SN rating in 2016 and a SN loading issue on the Ballston 01253 
distribution feeder which is projected to be loaded in excess of 100% of its SN rating by 
the summer of 2018. 
 
At the Brook Road substation, the Brook Road 36953 and 36954 13.2 kV distribution 
feeders are projected to be loaded in excess of 100% by 2016 and 2017, respectively and 
a MWh criteria violation also exists on the Brook Road 36954 and 36955 feeders, which 
are projected to reach 25 MWh and 18 MWh, respectively, in 2016. In addition, The 
Brook Road 36955 feeder is a 13.2 kV distribution feeder serving parts of the Towns of 
Greenfield, Milton, Providence, and Saratoga. It is 143 circuit miles in length and has 
repeatedly been one of the worst performing feeders in the Northeast Region of National 
Grid’s territory, including being the third worst performing feeder in 2013. This project 
will allow the Brook Road 36955 distribution feeder to be split into two distribution 
feeders, each approximately 70 miles long, instead of one very long (143 mile) feeder. 
 
The Rock City Falls Substation is a small, single transformer 34.5/4.16 kV substation on 
a radial 34.5 kV sub-transmission line with a single distribution feeder. Neither the 
transformer nor the feeders are loaded in excess of 56% of their summer normal ratings. 
However, the Rock City Falls 40415 feeder is a 4.16 kV feeder surrounded by 
predominantly 13.2 kV feeders, and ties to only the Brook Road 36952 feeder through a 
single 1,500 kVA ratio transformer. This situation leaves load at risk should there be 
failure at either the Rock City Falls Substation or the radial 34.5 kV sub-transmission line 
feeding it. This project will allow for the retirement of the Rock City Falls Substation 
 
and part if not all of the Rock City Falls 40415 feeder could be converted to 13.2 kV and 
served from the new substation. 
 
Finally, the southwest corner of the Town of Greenfield, the northwest corner of the 
Town of Milton, the adjoining southeast corner of the Town of Providence, and the 
northeast corner of the Town of Galway are served by the Vail Mills 39252, 13.2 kV 
distribution feeder. Due to the fact that the load in this area is 14 miles from the Vail 
Mills substation, maintaining adequate voltage during peak loading times is a challenge, 
and this area has frequently needed improvements to boost voltage to acceptable levels. 
This project will transfer potions of this load to the new Sodeman Road substation 
eliminating the voltage issues and providing a feeder tie with the Vail Mills 39252. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This project directly improves reliability for customers of Ballston, Brook Road, Rock 
City Falls, and Vail Mills substations. The new substation will provide additional 
capacity in the area to support load growth associated with the Luther Forest Technology 
Campus in Malta.

Ballston station serves 7,607 customers 
 
Brook Road station serves 15,108 customers 
 
Rock City Falls station serves 604 customers 
 
Vail Mills station serves 8,053 customers 
 
Alternatives: 
 
1. Distribution switching to transfer load off of Brook Road and Ballston stations to 
adjacent stations. This alternative only addresses the load issues and not any reliability 
related issues. The adjacent substations do not have sufficient capacity to accommodate 
the load transfers that would be necessary which results in overloads at the adjacent 
substation which is unacceptable. 
 
2. Install a second transformer at the Ballston Station with 2 new feeders & retire Rock 
City Falls Station. This station though large enough to install a second bank is located in 
an area which is not conducive to the construction of new distribution feeders any of 
which would require the construction of new duct bank in excess of 1.5 miles in length 
for each additional feeder. While this alternative would address all the loading concerns 
at the Ballston and Brook Road substations, it too does not address any of the reliability 
and voltage concerns associated with the Brook Road 36955 or Vail Mills 39252 feeders. 
Approx. cost = $9.8M 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA. Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally 
increases the threshold for certain higher cost interconnection upgrades (such as 3V0). 
This will enable PV and ESS to interconnect at higher volumes with a lower marginal 
interconnection cost.

VVO directly contributes to system efficiency, contributing to EE targets, as well as 
reducing energy required from carbon heavy generation (by reducing the total energy 
procured from NYISO). Increasing efficiency also reduces peak loading, which may 
decrease the need for fossil fuel peaking plants.

Program Name: 
Volt-Var Optimization / Conservation Voltage Reduction 
(VVO/CVR) 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: 
 
C077098 - NY VVO Central D-Line,  
C077097 - NY VVO East D-Line,  
C082361 - NY VVO West D-Line,  
C076088 - NY VVO East Substation,  
C076103 - NY VVO Central Substation,  
C076105 - NY VVO West Substation,  
C083329 - NY VVO East - T Sub,  
C084063 - NY VVO West T Sub,  
C084065 - NY VVO Central T Sub 
 
Description: 
 
Volt Var Optimization and Conservation Voltage Reduction  (“VVO/CVR”)  is a 
distribution level program where voltage control devices, such as capacitors and voltage 
regulators, are intelligently controlled in a coordinated manner to optimize the 
performance of the distribution system.  This program is designed to reduce customer 
load and energy use, which may also reduce system losses.  
 
VVO refers to a process whereby the voltage and reactive power flows of the distribution 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 211 of 457


--- Page 213 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 212 of 457 
C077098, C077097, C082361, C076088, 
C076103, C076105, C083329, C084063, 
C084065 
NY Volt-Var Optimization (West, Central, 
and East Divisions) 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
system are managed to reduce the magnitude of voltage drop on a distribution feeder.   
CVR refers to a process whereby if the magnitude of voltage drop is reduced, voltage 
regulating devices will be controlled to operate the feeder at the lowest possible range 
within allowable standards (- 5% and +2.5% of nominal on distribution feeders, as 
outlined by PSC Case 90-E-1119 and National Grid Electric Service Bulletin 320) to 
foster customer energy savings.   
 
The Company has historically managed voltage primarily with the use of autonomously 
controlled Load Tap Changing Transformers (LTC), line regulators, and capacitors.  
When installed, regulators are typically programmed to maintain a specific voltage at its 
location as specified by a distribution planning engineer.  Capacitors, when installed, are 
usually fixed and manually switched on and off of the circuit seasonally or as needed. 
Newer installations may be switched autonomously with device settings programmed to 
operate under specific conditions (i.e.: voltage control, VAR control).The primary driver 
of this project is to provide more reliable and higher quality power by monitoring the 
voltage performance across the system in real time and automating the control of the 
various voltage regulating devices through an integrated centralized control scheme.  This 
proposed VVO/CVR program adds a layer of coordination, via communication and 
control, to optimize the use of regulators, capacitors, and line voltage monitors to respond 
to system dynamics in real time. 
 
The Company’s VVO/CVR efforts are on-going at the Clifton Park demonstration project 
and its initial rollout throughout the state.  As part of the program, an additional subset of 
distribution circuits from 44 substations between FY21-25 will be targeted for 
enhancement based on expected benefit. This will be achieved through the installation of 
switched capacitors, voltage regulators, and line voltage sensors with the addition of 
telecommunications and control through a centralized server. The subset will be 
determined with pre-engineering analysis and modelling efforts to determine to expected 
voltage and reactive power optimization. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
There are several anticipated benefits of a VVO/CVR deployment: 
 
• Improved feeder power factor, flatter voltage profiles, reduced feeder losses, 
reduced peak demand, and reduced energy consumption by customers.  The 
estimated reduction in energy consumption is expected to be approximately 3% 
but will vary based on the individual feeder characteristics.

Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
• The increased near real time operational data made available to the regional 
control centers, via data collected from automated capacitors and regulators, will 
support the improved management of the distribution system and assist in the 
integration of future distributed resources.  
 
• Actively maintaining proper voltage via intelligent centralized control will 
improve feeder voltage performance, keeping the voltage flat and low, allowing 
for higher DER penetration. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Modern electrical equipment, including air conditioning, refrigeration, appliances, and 
lighting are designed to operate most efficiently at 114V.  Delivering voltages at the 
optimal levels reduces energy consumption, improves service quality, and lowers costs. 
 
A benefit/cost analysis was completed in accordance with the BCA Handbook the 
Company published in its 2018 Distributed System Implementation Plan.  Through this 
assessment the cost effectiveness tests are estimated to have the following positive 
benefit to cost ratios:   SCT = 1.92,  UCT = 1.59 and RIM = 1.59. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Do Nothing:  The company would continue with autonomous local control of voltage 
regulating devices without a centralized control which would not realize the energy and 
system efficiency benefits of the optimization technology. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.  
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally

Program Name:  
None

Description: 
 
Install second 115/13.2kV 15/20/25MVA transformer at Sorrell Hill.  Install 2 new 
feeders and transfer one of the existing feeders to the new transformer.  The new feeder 
work will consist of installing approximately 2,000 feet of cable and approximately 6,000 
of double circuit.  Also, transfer portions of Lysander to Sorrell Hill on one of the new 
feeders. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Based on 2020 load projections, Sorrell Hill had a load at risk of 11.7MVA.  Its MWHr 
violation was 341.4MWHr's.  Lysander had a load at risk of 6.5MVA and a 211.0MWHr 
violation. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This will eliminate the MWHr violations at both Lysander and Sorrell Hill.  This will be 
accomplished with the expanded capacity and additional feeders.

Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

C077338 
Cable Replacement Ntwk Sec NYW 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Type: 
 
T SubT D

Multi-Value 
Transmission 
(MVT) 
  
  
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name: 
Cable Replacement Ntwk Sec NYW 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
The replacement of underground distribution secondary cable is included in the Upstate 
New York Underground Cable Strategy.  These replacements will be completed through 
a series of specific projects targeting cables considering their past performance, the 
history of failures, the asset age, cable construction, design deficiencies, loading, and 
critical customers served.  Additionally, cable replacements in support of new customer 
development and public works projects are also anticipated.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
Cable systems are often designed with greater redundancy than overhead systems, and 
cable failure often has a limited impact on customer reliability statistics.  However, if 
cable performance deteriorates significantly, the likelihood of concurrent failures 
increases.  Cable failures can result in increased operation and loading on parallel 
equipment, further increasing the risk of failure on the rest of the system.  The 
consequences of multiple secondary network failures could be significant. The proactive 
replacement of electric utility assets such as aged underground cable is expected to 
reduce the risk of failures plus unplanned events and enhance the reliability plus capacity 
of the overall system

          Page 219 of 457

Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs

Program Name:  
Substation RTU 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
Projects to enhance Regional Control Center Operations through the implementation of a 
dedicated distribution SCADA system along with a DMS requires the expansion of RTUs 
in the Company’s distribution substations and the reconfiguration of some RTUs that are 
managing both transmission and distribution assets. 
 
This project supports control center capabilities by installing and reconfiguring RTUs in 
substations in which the control and monitoring of T&D assets utilize a common RTU. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Increasing volumes of distribution assets to be monitored and controlled, both Company 
assets and those of DER providers, is driving the need to expand the capabilities of the 
SCADA system. The proliferation of distributed intelligent devices in support of 
distribution system operations also increases cyber security risks. As part of this 
expansion the Company proposes to separate T&D SCADA systems to improve database 
performance and enhance cyber security and NERC CIP protections related to the Bulk 
Electric System (BES).

Customer Benefit: 
 
This program provides a means to enhance the situational awareness of distribution 
system operations that is crucial for the integration of increasing penetrations of DER in a 
safe and reliable fashion. 
 
Alternatives:  
Do Nothing 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA. Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Communication and Control programs directly improve the Company's ability to 
efficiently interconnect and operate DER. These technologies enable lower cost 
interconnections by allowing the Company to explore techniques such as flexible 
interconnection and advanced dispatch for programs like NWA. Telecom and Control 
(consistency in name of program) will enable more PV and ESS to interconnect to the 
system more efficiently. 
 
EMS / SCADA gives the company higher visibility into system conditions, allowing 
planners to make fewer assumptions, and be less conservative when interconnecting DG. 
This may lead to higher penetration of DG at lower costs

C078270 
Cable Replacement - Ntwk Sec NYE 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Type: 
 
T SubT D

Resiliency 
System 
Capacity 
Multi-Value 
Transmission 
(MVT) 
  
  
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name:  
None 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
The replacement of underground distribution secondary cable is included in the Upstate 
New York Underground Cable Strategy.  These replacements will be completed through 
a series of specific projects targeting cables considering their past performance, the 
history of failures, the asset age, cable construction, design deficiencies, loading, and 
critical customers served.  Additionally, cable replacements in support of new customer 
development and public works projects are also anticipated.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
Cable systems are often designed with greater redundancy than overhead systems, and 
cable failure often has a limited impact on customer reliability statistics.  However, if 
cable performance deteriorates significantly, the likelihood of concurrent failures 
increases.  Cable failures can result in increased operation and loading on parallel 
equipment, thereby further increasing the risk of failure on the rest of the system.  The 
consequences of multiple secondary network failures could be significant. The proactive 
replacement of electric utility assets such as aged underground cable is expected to 
reduce the risk of failures plus unplanned events and enhance the reliability plus capacity 
of the overall system

C078428 
Union Fall - Flood Mitigation 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Type: 
 
T SubT D 
 
 
X

Multi-Value 
Transmission 
(MVT) 
  
X 
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C053167, C068248, C068247 
 
Description: 
 
This project calls for the retirement of Union Falls 844 Substation due to high risk of 
flooding.  The recommended option proposes rebuilding the Union Falls Substation on an 
adjacent property already owned by National Grid outside of the delineated wetlands and 
above 100 years flood elevation. The existing substation will be retired, and all primary 
and secondary equipment will be removed from the site including steel structures and 
foundations.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
The Union Falls substation is in a high risk flood zone located  2.5 feet below the base 
flood elevation and directly adjacent to the Saranac River and due to the amount of 
seasonal flooding that occurs each spring with snow melt and rainfall , a mitigation plan 
is needed to prevent damage to essential equipment located at the site. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Relocating the substation eliminates risk of substation flooding improving customer 
reliability

C078428 
Union Fall - Flood Mitigation 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative #2- This alternative rebuilds the substation as two separate pole top solution 
replacing the existing four (4) 46kV OCB breakers with four (4) 72.5kV reclosers 
mounted on a pole structure within the ROW about 1 mile to the east of the existing 
station and the a new distribution transformer consisting of 46/2.4kV 3-333kVA 
transformers mounted on a Utica-rack type platform South West the existing station 
serving Union Falls feeder#84461.   Alternative#3 - This options proposes constructing a 
new conventional substation similar to  the recommended option#1 on a ROW 
approximately 1 miles east of the existing Union Falls substation at the same site 
proposed for pole top reclosers on Alternative#2 but may require National Grid purchase 
of land to build the substation and permitting rights.   
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The project need is to move substation assets outside flood zone due to high risk of 
flooding, which cannot be met with an NWA alternative. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

C078752 
Kenmore Station 22 Battery Storage 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Type: 
 
T SubT D 
 
X 
 
 
 
Spending Rationale: 
Asset Condition 
Communications/ 
Control Systems 
Customer 
Requests/ Public 
Requirements 
DER Electric 
System 
Access 
Damage/ Failure 
  
  
  
  
  
 
Non- 
Infrastructure 
Reliability 
Resiliency 
System 
Capacity 
Multi-Value 
Transmission 
(MVT) 
  
  
  
X 
  
 
 
Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
The existing sub transmission lines supplying the Kenmore area are projected to exceed 
summer normal operating conditions. An energy storage asset on the distribution system 
would provide capacity relief by discharging during peak hours to alleviate this operating 
constraint. In addition, the asset will provide capacity relief during zonal peak periods 
and services into the wholesale markets, providing supplemental system benefits during 
periods where capacity relief is not needed by the sub transmission lines. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Leveraging available, Company-owned space adjacent to Buffalo Station 22, install a 
single 2MW/3MWh energy storage system interconnected on the Station 22 distribution 
system. Similar to other non-wires alternative opportunities, the Company will issue a 
request for proposals from industry for a turn-key installation. The awarded proposal will 
include engineering services, permitting, interconnection, procurement of the energy 
storage system, protection, and controls, as well as construction and commissioning of 
the complete system into operational status.

Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 233 of 457 
C078752 
Kenmore Station 22 Battery Storage 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This project will ensure safe and reliable power is delivered to the Kenmore area.  
 
Alternatives: 
 
Distribution engineering has proposed a traditional (wires) solution involving installation 
of 2 new cables from Sawyer station through Stations 24 and 23 to Station 22 and then 
redistributing the 12 distribution power transformers about the six cables so that each 
cable supplies only two (2) transformers rather than three (3).   
 
DER/NWA Alternative:   
 
This project is a DER alternative to the wires solution (described above). 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally 
increases the threshold for certain higher cost interconnection upgrades (such as 3V0). 
This will enable PV and ESS to interconnect at higher volumes with a lower marginal 
interconnection cost.  
 
VVO directly contributes to system efficiency, contributing to EE targets, as well as 
reducing energy required from carbon heavy generation (by reducing the total energy 
procured from NYISO). Increasing efficiency also reduces peak loading, which may 
decrease the need for fossil fuel peaking plants.

C079532, C079534 & C083385. 
Temple Substation Rebuild 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Type: 
 
T SubT D 
 
 
X

Communications/

Customer

Transmission 
(MVT) 
  
  
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name:  
None 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  C079534 is for the two new stations. C083385 is for 
the new control house. 
 
Description: 
 
C079532 is the underground work required for the rebuild of Temple Street Substation.  
There will be 3,000 feet of new conduit, 6 new manholes and 14,500 feet of new cable.  
This underground work will reconnect the existing 20 feeders to the two new “open bus” 
stations at Temple Street Station. 
 
C079534 is for the two new stations.  The two new stations will reuse the existing three 
station transformers and will have a fourth transformer; so, each of the new stations will 
be supplied with two station transformers.  The south station will be of “open air” 
construction and configured with a “breaker and a half” bus.  The south station (i.e. 
“breaker and a half” portion of Temple Street station) will supply the network feeders and 
the critical (hospitals) all underground feeders.  The north station will be of “open air” 
construction and configured with a “straight” bus.  The north station will supply the non-
critical loads.   
 
C083385 is for the new control house.

--- Page 237 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 236 of 457 
C079532, C079534 & C083385. 
Temple Substation Rebuild 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification: 
 
In March 2017, the existing “breaker and a half” metalclad was de-energized due to a 
switch failure.  The switch that failed fed one of the seven network cables and the 
network protector failed to operate, suppling fault current to the entire medal clad bus.  
This resulted in the need to open the breakers that supplied the metalclad switchgear 
dropping the entire Temple Street network along with the radial underground circuits that 
supplied several hospitals.  The resulting damage was over $1.3 million to repair.  Both 
metalclads (i.e. “breaker and a half” metalclad  & the “straight” bus metalclad) are in 
very poor condition and in need of replacement per the Asset Condition Report (along 
with some other equipment within the substation). 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacement of the metalclads will reduce the probability of future interruptions to the 
Temple Network and other critical and high-profile customers fed from this substation. 
 
Alternatives:  
 
 
None 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
Since the justification for this project is Asset Replacement in nature, it was determined 
that non-wires alternatives are not feasible. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA): N/A 
 
Studies/References: 
 
Study Report Name (s):  
Temple Asset Condition Report
