Program Name: 
Fault Location Isolation and Service Restoration (FLISR) 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: 
 
C080088 - NY FLISR Central - D-line, 
C080090 - NY FLISR West- D-line, 
C080089 - NY FLISR East - D-line 
 
Description: 
 
The Company has been implementing automation schemes on its sub-transmission 
circuits for the past several years in its upstate New York service territory.  This 
distribution level program will begin the automation of distribution circuits for reliability 
enhancement. 
 
Distribution Automation, commonly referred to as Fault Location Isolation and Service 
Restoration (“FLISR”), is a control scheme that incorporates telecommunications and 
advanced control of key switching devices. This scheme provides remote monitoring and 
operator control of field devices for normal operations and maintenance, while at the 
same time providing an automated response to system contingencies. The automated 
functionality is expected to minimize the number of customer interruptions and expedite 
system reconfiguration to facilitate service restoration.   
 
This automation scheme positively impacts the resulting customers interrupted and 
customer minutes interrupted (“CMI”) performance from a fault event that occurs within 
the zone of protection.  As part of a FLISR system, manual switches and feeder ties 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 240 of 457


--- Page 242 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 241 of 457 
C080088, C080090, C080089 
NY FLISR (West, Central, and East 
Divisions) 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
would be upgraded to Company standard reclosers at selected three phase mainline 
locations to allow for automated switching in the case of a contingency event.  In 
addition, these devices would be integrated with the Company’s SCADA system and any 
future Advanced Distribution Management System. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
National Grid anticipates improved main line CMI performance on the feeders targeted 
for FLISR deployment.  The additional operational data collected by the automated 
switches also will support the improved management of the distribution system, assisting 
in demand optimization, DER integration, and operational efficiency. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Reduced duration of interruptions on targeted circuits will have a positive impact on 
customers served by those circuits. The FLISR scheme is also expected to reduce the 
number of customers that experience an outage for an event by utilizing the feeder tie 
capability to restore a portion of the affected circuit. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Do Nothing:  The company would continue to deploy local device settings on reclosers 
without a centralized control scheme which would not realize the system benefits of the 
coordination technology. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.  
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
Prospect Hill substation is a 115-13.2kV, 22.4MVA single transformer substation with 
three (3) distribution feeders. The substation was constructed in 1974 and much of the 
equipment is from the original installation. This project will replace the 15kV Metalclad 
Switchgear and 115kV circuit switcher at Prospect Hill substation due to asset condition 
concerns. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The Metalclad switchgear at Prospect Hill substation is in poor condition. There are 
through holes at several locations on the unit along with signs of water ingress and 
animal intrusion. This unit requires increased maintenance in order to continue safe, 
reliable operation. The existing 115kV circuit switcher is not capable of interrupting the 
available fault current and requires the remote end breakers to trip in order to isolate a 
transformer fault. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacement of this equipment in a controlled manner mitigates the risk of in-service 
failure of deteriorated assets which can lead to long-term interruptions and unplanned 
customer outages. The additional benefit of the installation of modern protection, control

and monitoring capabilities have the potential to improve system reliability through 
increased communication and real time system information in Regional Control Centers 
and help to promote the increased penetration of Distributed Energy Resources. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Retire Prospect Hill Substation 
This alternative would remove all existing equipment at Prospect Hill substation. This 
alternative is not recommended as it has the potential to decrease reliability in the area 
through the removal of additional transmission sources, and cause overloads on the assets 
that would supply the customer load in place of Prospect Hill. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
This project was reviewed for potential NWA opportunities and deemed unsuitable for 
NWA due to it being an in-kind replacement of structural and switching equipment 
driven by asset condition and without need for additional capacity.

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  C080475 
 
 
Description: 
 
C080474 - Seventh Ave 13.2kV Transformer 
Install a new 34.5/13.8kV 20MVA transformer with LTC.  This will also include the 
installation of a new 1200A 34.5kV circuit breaker and the replacement of two 1200A 
34.5kV oil circuit breakers. 
 
C080476 - Seventh Ave North Feeder Conversion 
Creation of one new 13.2kV distribution circuits through conversion of two existing 
4.16kV circuits running along 2nd Ave. and 6th Ave. in Troy, NY.  Includes 
reconductoring of #6 Cu with 336.4 AAC, significantly increasing capacity, and 
replacement of poles to achieve necessary clearances and address pole deterioration. 
After this work is complete, retirement of distribution assets at the Lansingburgh 
substation will begin. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The transformer at the Seventh Ave substation has exceeded nameplate rating multiple 
times. Due to rapid load growth and limited ties in the area, a larger transformer is 
required to prevent overloading and low voltage conditions.

C080474, C080476 
Troy 4.16kV Area Study-Seventh Ave Station 
Replacement 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The Seventh Ave Station is located within the City of Troy, NY and supplies customers 
in and around the Lansingburgh area.  The expansion of the Seventh Ave substation and 
subsequent retirement of the 34.5/4.16kV transformer will ensure that this portion of the 
City of Troy continues to be served safely and reliably via three new distribution circuits. 
Additionally, the conversion of the four (4) 4.16kV distribution circuits currently served 
by the Seventh Ave TB#1 to 13.2kV will increase capacity to accommodate future load 
growth in the area while increasing reliability through the creation of 13.2kV feeder ties.  
 
Alternatives: 
 
Rebuild Seventh Ave with a new 34.5/13.8kV 9.375MVA transformer with LTC. 
Reconductor the North Troy-Lansingburgh 1 Line to increase 34.5kV capacity 
 
• The sub-transmission line work required for this project is significant and requires 
replacement of conduit through a large portion of Lansingburgh 
 
• The location of the Lansingburgh station places limits on the number of customers 
that can be served from Corliss Park. This reduces the effectiveness of the new 
20MVA transformer at Corliss Park. 
 
Retire the Seventh Ave Substation, rebuild the Lansingburgh substation and supply the 
customers from the Lansingburgh substation and the Sycaway Substation 
 
• The distribution line work required for this project is significant and exposes 
customers to a higher risk of an outage 
 
• The location of the Lansingburgh station places limits on the number of customers 
that can be served from Corliss Park. This reduces the effectiveness of the new 
20MVA transformer at Corliss Park. 
 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
NWA Alternatives are being considered as part of a hybrid solution to meet the needs 
identified by the Troy Area Study (including those being met by this project).

Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally 
increases the threshold for certain higher cost interconnection upgrades (such as 3V0). 
This will enable PV and ESS to interconnect at higher volumes with a lower marginal 
interconnection cost.  
 
VVO directly contributes to system efficiency, contributing to EE targets, as well as 
reducing energy required from carbon heavy generation (by reducing the total energy 
procured from NYISO). Increasing efficiency also reduces peak loading, which may 
decrease the need for fossil fuel peaking plants. 
 
Studies/References:

Program Name:  
Asset Condition 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C081399 - Mohican Distribution 
 
Description: 
 
A new 15/20/25 MVA, 115/13.2kV LTC transformer with a four-feeder metal-clad 
switchgear is being installed under funding projects C053133 and C080755 while those 
projects are rebuilding the Mohican substation to address transmission asset condition 
issues. This project consists of the construction of four (4) complete distribution feeders 
from that new distribution transformer including reconductoring and/or conversion and 
reconfiguration of the area distribution feeders, to allow for the retirement of the Henry 
Street and McCrea Street substation which have significant asset conditions issues as well 
as the Farnan Road and Hudson Falls substations. The drivers of this project are asset 
condition at Henry and McCrea Streets and load relief at Hudson Falls. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The Henry Street and McCrea Street substations are 56 and 70 years old respectively and 
both have significant asset conditions issues which require the replacement of over 75% 
of the equipment within the substation.  In addition, Henry Street is built on a site of a 
former manufactured gas plant which needs to be remediated.  Finally, the Hudson Falls 
station transformer is projected to be overloaded by 2022.

Mohican Getaways & Distribution Feeders 
Version 1 –

Customer Benefit: 
 
This project directly improves reliability for customers of Farnan Road, Henry Street, 
Hudson Falls and McCrea Street substations.  
 
Farnan Road station serves 1,219 customers 
 
Henry Street station serves 3,413 customers 
 
Hudson Falls station serves 1,785 customers 
 
McCrea Street station serves 1,061 customers 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Option 1 is to address the issues at each substation individually by rebuilding the Henry 
and McCrea Street substations with new substations in kind in place while also replacing 
the Hudson Falls station transformer with a new larger transformer. 
 
Option 2 would rebuild the Henry Street substation in place in kind and would replace 
Hudson Falls and McCrea Street with a new 115/13.2 kV substation at McCrea Street 
with a 25 MVA station transformer and 3 distribution feeders while retiring Hudson 
Falls. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA. Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

Description: 
 
C081418 - Smith Bridge Transformer & Metalclad 
Install a new 115/13.8kV 25MVA transformer with LTC and a new 15kV Insulated 
Metalclad Switchgear with four (4) 1200A feeder breakers, one (1) 2000A main breaker 
and one (1) 2000A tie breaker.  This will also include the installation of one new 1200A 
115kV circuit switcher. 
 
C083483 - Smith Bridge Distribution Getaways 
Construction of underground distribution feeder getaways for three (3) new 13.2kV 
distribution circuits.  Includes the installation of four new manholes and approximately 
0.4 miles of duct system. 
 
C083485 - Smith Bridge 46456 & 46457 Construction 
Creation of two new 13.2kV distribution circuits through conversion of two existing 
34.5kV circuits running from the vicinity of the Smith Bridge station to the Saratoga

C081418, C083483, C083485, C083484, 
C083487 
Saratoga Station Replacement 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Station.  Includes installation of 1/0 AAAC ground wire and replacement of 19 structures 
to achieve necessary clearances and address pole deterioration.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
The primary drivers for these projects are related to the safety, reliability and 
maintenance concerns of the existing equipment at the Saratoga Station.  This substation 
equipment is at a higher probability of failure due to its condition while it's age makes 
repairs and acquisition of spare parts difficult or impossible.  The Saratoga station is a 
two-transformer station with TB #2 placed in service in the late 1930’s.  Due to its 
degraded condition, TB#2 requires replacement by the end of 2023.  In addition to the 
transformer, the metalclad switchgear, 34.5kV bus supports, potential transformers, 
switches, insulators and lightning protection require replacement by 2023 due to 
deterioration.  Furthermore, the Saratoga TB #1, 5kV circuit breakers and control 
building require remediation activities within 5-10 years.  These projects enable the 
retirement of the Saratoga Station through expansion of the existing Smith Bridge station 
to the north.  Photos depicting some of the visible asset condition issues at the Saratoga 
Station can be found in Appendix A. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The Saratoga Station is located within the City of Saratoga Springs, NY and supplies 
customers in and around the downtown area.  The expansion of the Smith Bridge 
substation and subsequent retirement of the Saratoga station will ensure that this portion 
of the City of Saratoga continues to be served safely and reliably via one of the three new 
distribution circuits. Additionally, the conversion of the three (3) 4.16kV distribution 
circuits currently served by the Saratoga TB#2 to 13.2kV will increase capacity to 
accommodate future load growth in the area while increasing resiliency and reliability 
through the creation of 13.2kV feeder ties.  
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1 - Rebuild Saratoga Substation In-Place 
This option consists of rebuilding the existing Saratoga station at the existing site through 
the installation of three (3) new 38 kV, 1200 Amp vacuum breakers, one (1) new 
12/16/20 MVA 34.5/13.8 kV transformer with LTC, one (1) new four feeder metal-clad 
switchgear and construction of new feeder getaways for three (3) new 13.2 kV 
distribution feeders.  This alternative is not recommended due to it's comparatively high 
cost, potentially extensive environmental remediation requirements due to the site's 
former use as a Manufactured Gas Plant, the listing of the Control Building on the 
National Parks National Historic Registry, and it's location in close proximity to valuable 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10)

C081418, C083483, C083485, C083484,

Saratoga Station Replacement 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
commercial & residential real estate located within the City of Saratoga Springs, NY. 
 
Alternative 2 – Build New Saratoga Substation Nearby 
This option consists of  the construction of a new Saratoga Substation on National Grid 
owned former Ice Rink property across the street to the southeast of the existing site with 
the same equipment noted in Alternative 1.  This alternative would also require an 
approximately 1,000’ extension of two 34.5kV overhead sub-transmission circuits to the 
new substation.  This option is not recommended due to it's comparatively high cost and 
it's location in close proximity to valuable commercial & residential real estate located 
within the City of Saratoga Springs, NY. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
This project addresses asset condition concerns at the existing Saratoga Station and does 
not increase the overall capacity of the area beyond that achieved through the use of 
standard equipment.  As a result, it does not meet the NWA suitability criteria.

Program Name:

Associated Funding Numbers: C083844, C083849, C083850 
 
Description: 
 
C081420 - Liberty St TB5 Install 34.5/13.8kV 
Install a new 34.5/13.8kV 12.5MVA transformer with LTC and a new 15kV Insulated 
Metalclad Switchgear with two (2) 1200A feeder breakers and one (1) 2000A main 
breaker.  This will also include the installation of one new 1200A 34.5kV circuit breaker 
and one new 1200A 34.5kV tie circuit breaker. 
 
C081421 - Liberty St 13.2kV Getaways 
This project is to install new 500MCM Cu getaways for out of the new 13.2kV metal-
clad switchgear that will be installed as part of the Liberty St. Station Rebuild. 
 
C083844 - Liberty St D-Line Overhead Rebuild 
This project is to convert 4.16kV overhead distribution to 13.2kV in support of the 
Liberty St Substation Rebuild. This will create feeder ties with the surrounding area. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The #1 transformer at Liberty St. substation has severe oil leaks. The tank is not holding 
pressure and there is a high oxygen content contributing to higher moisture absorption, as 
well as deterioration of the oil. The oil containment is badly damaged and not providing 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 262 of 457


--- Page 264 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs

Page 263 of 457 
C081420, C081421, C083852 
Troy 4.16kV Area Study-Liberty Street 
Station Replacement 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
oil spill protection. This is an environmental risk that should be mitigated. It is 
recommended to upgrade the transformer to a new unit. New spill containment would be 
installed. The transformer bay would need to be re-worked as well since all its equipment 
is in poor shape. 
 
The overhead distribution circuits at the Corliss Park substation show signs of aging 
getaway cables and load growth causing circuit loading approach the cable ratings. There 
is currently no operational concern, but portions of the getaway cables are suspected to be 
over 100 years old based on failed sections of other getaway cables at this station. 
Additionally, 4 of the 5 circuits are expected to be overloaded within the next 10 years. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The Liberty Street Station is located within the City of Troy, NY and supplies customers 
in and around the downtown area.  The expansion of the Liberty Street substation and 
subsequent retirement of the 34.5/4.16kV transformer will ensure that this portion of the 
City of Troy continues to be served safely and reliably via two new distribution circuits. 
Additionally, the conversion of the five (5) 4.16kV overhead distribution circuits 
currently served by the Liberty Street TB#1, TB#2, and TB#3 to 13.2kV will increase 
capacity to accommodate future load growth in the area while increasing reliability 
through the creation of 13.2kV feeder ties. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Retire Transformer Bank #1 and replace with a 34.5kV/13.2kV Transformer and open-air 
breakers 
 
• The substation work required for this project requires a larger footprint, meaning 
more land would have to be bought, and is more expensive for the same outcome. 
 
Replace Transformer Bank #1 in kind (with a 34.5kV/4.16k transformer) 
 
• The distribution line work required for this project is significant and will not 
provide the reliability of 13.2kV ties and capacity for the rapid load growth 
currently being witnessed in Troy, NY 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
NWA Alternatives are being considered as part of a hybrid solution to meet the needs 
identified by the Troy Area Study (though this project cannot be deferred by an NWA 
since it is driven by Asset Condition).

Program Name:  
Asset Replacement 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
This project is to rebuild Grand Island station due to the asset condition issues present at 
the station. The aging equipment includes both transformers, two circuit switchers, two 
circuit breakers, two 115kV voltage transformers, and both the high side and low side 
transformer silicon carbide arrestors. All aging equipment issues at this station will be 
addressed by being replaced. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
There is an asset condition issue present at Grand Island station due to it aging 
equipment. The aging equipment includes both transformers, two circuit switchers, two 
circuit breakers, two 115kV voltage transformers, and both the high side and low side 
transformer silicon carbide arrestors. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This project will create a more reliable station due to the replacement of its aging 
infrastructure. In addition to reliability, larger transformers are being considered to 
increase the loading capacity for new customers to be served by this station.

Alternatives: 
 
Do nothing and replace units upon failure. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since the 
justification for this project is Asset Condition in nature, it was determined that this 
project will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
Blue Stores Substation is a 115-13.2kV distribution station with a single transformer 
rated at 17.92MVA and four (4) distribution feeders. This project is for the replacement 
of the 15kVMetalclad switchgear (mfg. 1971), 115kV circuit switcher, and installation of 
115kV CCVTs at Blues Stores Substation due to asset condition concerns. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The Metalclad switchgear at Blue Stores substation is in poor condition, showing signs of 
water intrusion and animal ingress. The 115kV circuit switcher is targeted for 
replacement due to published manufacturer warnings, and lack of support.  Asset 
condition shows that likelihood of failure if not replaced or refurbished within less than 5 
years.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacement of this equipment in a controlled manner mitigates the risk of in-service 
failure of deteriorated assets which can lead to long-term interruptions and unplanned 
customer outages with possible 4854 customers impacted. The additional benefit of the 
installation of modern protection, control and monitoring capabilities have the potential 
to improve system reliability through increased communication and real time system

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
Sycaway Substation is a 115-13.2kV distribution station originally commissioned as a 
single bank station in the 1970's. There are currently two (2) transformers rated at 
22.4MVA and 25MVA respectively. Two (2) metalclad switchgear units are supplying 
six (6) distribution feeders. This project is for the in-kind replacement of the 15kV 
Metalclad switchgear #1 at Sycaway Station. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The 15kV Metalclad Switchgear #1 at Sycaway station is in need of replacement due to 
asset condition (mfg. 1971). The 15kV bus has been damaged due to faults and subjected 
to tracking on the bus. The 15kV circuit breakers have issues that are prone to causing 
mis-operations at times, and lack of original equipment manufacturer support. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacement of this equipment in a controlled manner mitigates the risk of in-service 
failure of deteriorated assets which can lead to long-term interruptions and unplanned 
customer outages with a possible 7282 customers impacted. The additional benefit of the 
installation of modern protection, control and monitoring capabilities have the potential 
to improve system reliability through increased communication and real time system

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C081262, C081415, C083661 
 
Description: 
 
C081991 - Corliss Park XFMR 2 & Bus Install 
Install a new 34.5/13.8kV 20MVA transformer with LTC and a new 15kV Insulated 
Metalclad Switchgear with three (3) 1200A feeder breakers and one (1) 2000A main 
breaker.  This will also include the installation of one new 1200A 34.5kV circuit 
switcher. 
 
C081385 - Corliss Park West Feeder Conversions 
Creation of a new 13.2kV distribution feeder through the conversion of two existing 
4.16kV circuits running along 2nd Ave. in Troy and River Rd. in Schaghticoke.  This 
includes the reconductoring of #6 Cu with 336.4 AAC, significantly increasing the 
feeders capacity, and replacement of poles to achieve necessary clearances and address 
pole deterioration. 
 
C081414 - Corliss Park South Feeder Conversion 
Creation of a new 13.2kV distribution circuit through conversion of three existing 4.16kV 
circuits running along 6th Ave. in Troy. This includes reconductoring of #6 Cu with 
336.4 AAC, significantly increasing the feeders capacity, and replacement of poles to 
achieve necessary clearances and address pole deterioration.

          Page 279 of 457


--- Page 281 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 280 of 457 
C082047 
Harris 54 Relief 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The transformer at the Corliss Park substation shows evidence of previous leaks as well 
as some current leaking from the drain valves. There is oil in the base of the LTC control 
cabinet and the oil pad is saturated. The transformer has seen some through-faults due to 
faults on the feeder. 
 
The metal-clad switchgear at the Corliss Park substation shows rusting throughout. There 
is currently no operational concern, but the breakers in the metal-clad are prone to 
operational problems from moisture absorption due to the design of the bushings and arc 
chutes. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The Corliss Park Station is located within the City of Troy, NY and supplies customers in 
and around the Lansingburgh area.  The expansion of the Corliss Park substation and 
subsequent retirement of the 34.5/4.16kV transformer will ensure that this portion of the 
City of Troy continues to be served safely and reliably via three new distribution circuits. 
Additionally, the conversion of the three (3) 4.16kV distribution circuits currently served 
by the Corliss Park TB#1 to 13.2kV will increase capacity to accommodate future load 
growth in the area while increasing resiliency through the creation of 13.2kV feeder ties.  
 
Alternatives: 
 
Retire the Corliss Park Substation and supply the customers from the North Troy 
Substation and the Prospect Hill Substation 
 
• The distribution line work required for this project was significant, required a new 
river crossing, and exposes customers to a higher risk of a sustained outage. 
 
Retire Corliss Park and Rebuild North Troy with 2-24/32/40MVA banks 
 
• The distribution line work required for this project is significant, required double 
circuiting to run express feeders to serve the load currently supplied by Corliss 
Park, and exposes customers to a higher risk of a sustained outage 
 
• The substation work required for this project is significant, requiring the 
installation of two transformers instead of one, and there is no spare breaker for a 
low side bus tie to address contingency concerns.

MSH Create Tie F5151 to F5052

Program Name: 
Minor Storm Hardening 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  
C049885 
 
Description:   
 
This project is to rebuild, extend and convert approximately 11,900’ of #4 CCW to 336 
AAC to create a full 13.2 kV feeder tie between Mumford F5052 and East Golah F5151 
on Caledonia-Avon Rd in Caledonia, NY. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The primary driver for this project is resiliency.  Rebuilding 1940 Circa small #4 wire 
with 336 wire will make this portion of the feeder more resilient and allow for the 
creation of a full 13.2 kV tie between Mumford feeder 5052 and East Golah feeder 5151.  
The new feeder tie will also improve reliability and service by providing the ability for 
load transfers and operational flexibility during maintenance and outage restoration. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Customers will benefit from a more resilient feeder and improved reliability and service 
with the ability for load transfers and operational flexibility during maintenance and 
outage restoration with the new feeder tie.  
 
 
T SubT D

C082103  
MSH Create Tie F5151 to F5052 
Version 1 –

Alternatives: 
 
None 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):   
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation. 
 
Studies/References: 
 
Study Report Name (s): None 
Sanction Paper No: None 
Strategy No: None

C082232, C082233, C082234 
D5D Porcelain Replacement NY 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020

(MVT) 
  
X 
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name: 
D5D Porcelain Replacement 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C082232 (West), C082234 (East), C082233 
(Central) 
 
Description: 
 
This program will replace on average approximately (75) locations per year having 
porcelain D5D style switches replaced with gang operated hook switches or new D5D 
switches.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
Prior to 2006 mainline 600A feeder switches, known as D5D switches, were made of 
porcelain.  In recent years a trend was identified that many of these units failed under 
operation due to breakdown and small fractures in the porcelain material.  This project 
will begin to replace units of that vintage at critical locations such as normal open points 
and circuits with high customer counts. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Use of new switches can avoid unexpected customer outages that can occur when switch 
fails unexpectedly during routine operation.  If these switches fail during use as part of 
restoration effort during an outage response they cause delays and extended outage time 
because added repair work must be done along with resolving original issue. 
 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 286 of 457

Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan

Page 287 of 457 
C082232, C082233, C082234 
D5D Porcelain Replacement NY 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Do nothing and replace units upon failure. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
This program replaces existing porcelain D5D switches with a safer alternative. An NWA 
is not applicable to this program.  
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

Malone 2nd Bank Feeders (D-Line)

Program Name:  
Specific 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C059673, C082668, C046418 
 
Description: 
 
The Malone Area Study looked at solving Asset Condition issues and all MWhr 
violations at Malone Substation and the surrounding stations.  To eliminate the MWhr 
violations and all asset condition issues it is necessary to retire Elm Street and North 
Bangor substations, install a second bank at Malone substation and transfer the load from 
Elm St. and N. Bangor onto the second bank.  In order to retire Elm St. and N. Bangor 
substations three new 13.2kV distribution feeders need to be constructed. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The Malone Area Study looked at solving Asset Condition issues and all MWhr 
violations at Malone Substation and the surrounding stations.  Currently upon loss of the 
bank at Malone there would be approximately 400 MWhrs at risk, violating the 240 
MWhr criteria.  Malone is a 13.2kV island with no adequate neighboring feeder ties. The 
Malone station feeds 3,791 customers. In 2018 an Inspection report that was completed 
on Elm St., North Bangor, and Malone Stations.  This inspection report  identified the 3 
phase regulator at Elm Street , the Metal Clad at Malone, and the North Bangor TB1 and 
breakers needing replacement. 
 
 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 289 of 457


--- Page 291 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 290 of 457 
C082332 
Malone 2nd Bank Feeders (D-Line) 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This will eliminate all MWHr violations at Malone Substation, all asset condition issues 
at Malone, Elm St., and N. Bangor substations, eliminate the maintenance costs from Elm 
St and N Bangor substations, and adds distributed generation hosting capacity to the 
Malone area. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
The second option is to fix the asset condition issues at Elm St. substation, Convert N. 
Bangor substation to 13.2kV, and replace the Metalclad at Malone.  This option 
eliminates all the asset condition issues but leaves 192MWhr's at risk which is 
approximately 3,500 customers out of power upon loss the Malone substation. The third 
option is to convert Elm St. substation to 13.2kV, convert N. Bangor substation to 
13.2kV and replace the Metalclad at Malone substation.  This option eliminates all asset 
condition issues but leaves 120MWhr's at risk which is approximately 2,200 customers 
out of power upon loss of Malone substation. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since this 
project is partially driven Asset Condition in nature (retirement of Elm St. and N. Bangor 
stations), it was determined that this project will not be evaluated for a potential 
NWA.      
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally 
increases the threshold for certain higher cost interconnection upgrades (such as 3V0). 
This will enable PV and ESS to interconnect at higher volumes with a lower marginal 
interconnection cost.  
 
VVO directly contributes to system efficiency, contributing to EE targets, as well as 
reducing energy required from carbon heavy generation (by reducing the total energy 
procured from NYISO). Increasing efficiency also reduces peak loading, which may 
decrease the need for fossil fuel peaking plants.

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
Station 79 is a 23-4.16kV three bank distribution station constructed in the 1950s and a 
majority of the equipment is from the original installation. This project is for the rebuild 
of Station 79 on an unused piece of adjacent land. This includes the installation of (2) 23-
4.16kV, 9.375MVA transformers, 23kV circuit breakers, and new outdoor metalclad 
switchgear. The existing station will be retired and removed upon commissioning and 
energization of the new substation in an adjacent location. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The equipment at Station 79 has asset condition issues and is built in a configuration that 
no longer meets the Company's standards. The existing equipment requires increased 
maintenance due to deterioration, no spare parts, and lack of original equipment 
manufacturer support. Most of the equipment was identified for highly probable of failure 
if not replaced or refurbished within the next 5 years.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacement of this equipment in a controlled manner mitigates the risk of in-service 
failure of deteriorated assets which can lead to long-term interruptions and unplanned 
customer outages with possibilities of 2556 customers impacted. The additional benefit of 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10)

Station 79 Rebuild 
Version 1 –

the installation of modern protection, control and monitoring capabilities have the 
potential to improve system reliability through increased communication and real time 
system information in Regional Control Centers and help to promote the increased 
penetration of Distributed Energy Resources. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Rebuild Station 79 in place 
This option would replace the three (3) 23-4.16kV, 2.5MVA transformers and regulators 
with three (3) standard 23-4.16kV 3.75/4.675MVA LTC equipped transformers. Replace 
(6) 5kV oil-filled circuit breakers with 15kV vacuum circuit breakers and upgraded 
protective relaying and replace associated disconnect switches. The five (5) 23kV motor 
operated disconnects will be replaced in kind. This alternative is not recommended as the 
trimline and complexity are increased due to the construction and designs that would 
interface with existing equipment for the duration of the project. The existing 5kV bus 
would also remain in place, failing to mitigate safety concerns associated with 
construction using outdated clearance standards. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
This project was reviewed for potential NWA opportunities and deemed unsuitable for 
NWA due to it being an in-kind replacement of structural and switching equipment 
driven by asset condition and without need for additional capacity.

Description: 
 
C083235 is to install approximately 44,000 feet of new cable in the new duct line system 
(installed under Funding Projects C054834 & C081071) and remove approximately 
44,000 feet of existing cable what is presently contained in the failed duct line. 
 
The project is located in Syracuse, NY on State Street from Ash Street to East Adams 
Street and impacts 18 separate systems or circuits that were installed in the 1950’s, 
1960’s and the 1970”: 
 
• portions of three 120/208V low voltage distribution systems,  
• portions of three 4.16kV primary radial circuits,  
• portions of two 13.2kV primary radial circuits,  
• portions of six 11.5kV primary network circuits,  
• portions of four 13.2kV primary network circuits.  
 
The majority of the cables are original to their systems and some are of an undesirable 
cable construction (i.e. Paper insulate-Lead covered) which are to be removed from

The cable is planned to be installed and removed over 3 years: 
 
Year #1 will install the LVAC cables, first, to limit the field forces time in the manholes 
to newly spliced primary cable.  The LVAC cables are comprised of three electrical 
systems – Ash Street Radial Net, Ash Street Network, & Temple Street Network. 
 
Year #2 will install five radial primary circuits.  The radial primary circuits are comprised

Year #3 will install ten network circuits.  The network circuits are comprised of six 
11.5kV Ash Street circuits and four Temple Street circuits.         
 
Project Justification: 
 
The primary drivers for this project are related to reliability and asset condition. 
 
The need to replace the State Street Cables was found during two separate activities: 
   
• During the replacement of Temple Street 13.2kV Network Feeder 24358 under 
the Company’s Cable Replacement Program, the duct line system along South 
State Street was found to be unusable for cable replacement. 
 
• During the field investigation of Ash Street 11.5kV Network Feeder 22347 cable 
fault, the duct line system along North State Street was found to be unusable for 
cable replacement. 
 
The existing cables cannot be replaced (i.e. existing cables can't be removed, and/or new 
cables can't be installed) due to the manhole walls being in a deteriorated condition to 
structurally support the pressures required for pulling-in/pulling-out of cables as well as 
cables cannot be replaced due to the tile ducts that comprise the duct line system between 
manholes are compromised or collapsed. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The benefit to the Customer's is that they will not experience interruptions as well as 
prolonged outages.

Program Name:

Description: 
 
Rock Cut Substation is a two bank distribution station with two (2)  115-13.2kV, 
33.6MVA and 40MVA transformers and eight (8) distribution circuits. This project is for 
the replacement of the 15kV Metalclad switchgear at Rock Cut Substation. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The Metalclad switchgear at Rock Cut substation has been identified for replacement due 
to asset condition issues. There are signs of rusting and water intrusion in the unit 
increasing the likelihood of in service failure and unplanned outages. This metalclad has 
been identified that it will have a high probability of failure if not replaced or refurbished 
within 5 years.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacement of this equipment in a controlled manner mitigates the risk of in-service 
failure of deteriorated assets which can lead to long-term interruptions and unplanned 
customer outages with the possibility of impacting 11,537 customers. The additional 
benefit of the installation of modern protection, control and monitoring capabilities have 
the potential to improve system reliability through increased communication and real

Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Do nothing / Replace upon failure 
This alternative is not recommended as it presents a higher potential for in service failure,

DER/NWA Alternative:

DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
Since this justification for this project is Communications / Control Systems in nature, it

Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Communication and Control programs directly improve the Company's ability to 
efficiently interconnect and operate DER. These technologies enable lower cost 
interconnections by allowing the Company to explore techniques such as flexible 
interconnection and advanced dispatch for programs like NWA. Telecom and Control 
(consistency in name of program) will enable more PV and ESS to interconnect to the 
system more efficiently. 
 
EMS / SCADA gives the company higher visibility into system conditions, allowing 
planners to make fewer assumptions, and be less conservative when interconnecting DG. 
This may lead to higher penetration of DG at lower costs

North Genesee Area Study 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020

Multi-Value 
Transmission 
(MVT) 
  
  
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name:  
Asset Condition 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
Barker Station 78 is located in the Northern Genesee Area. There are two feeders 
supplied by Barker Station which supplies mostly residential and agricultural customers. 
This station was studied as part of the Northern Genesee area study. The output of the 
study was that there is primarily an asset condition need and secondarily a load relief 
need. The 2018 ACR of Barker Substation 78 highlighted that the 1960’s equipment is  
deteriorating. The transformer and most of the main equipment, is deteriorating with 
expectation of failure. 
 
The scope of this job will include building a new station near the existing Barker Station 
78 and the retire the existing station. Feeder work will be necessary to create stronger 
feeder ties with Lyndonville Station 95 to provide operational flexibility and resiliency to 
the area. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Barker Station 78 has deteriorating equipment that has failed or could fail within the next 
couple of years. In addition, the station is above capacity during peak times with a 
transformer that causes issues for operations.  
 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 307 of 457


--- Page 309 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 308 of 457 
C083749 
North Genesee Area Study 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacing Barker Station would provide more reliable and safe power for all of the 
customers that is serves.  
Alternatives: 
 
The alternative explored was an option to rebuild Barker Station in place while installing 
a larger 9.375 MVA 34.5 kV/4.8kV bank and expanding the fence to allow a mobile to be 
parked inside the fence. The current 3.75 MVA 34.5kV/4.8kV Delta/Delta transformer is 
protected by fuses but the fuses will have to be replaced with a high side recloser for the 
9.375 MVA 34.5 kV/4.8kV transformer. The existing breakers and bus PTs will have to 
be replaced per the Asset Condition Report. In addition, the existing regulators will be 
replaced with an LTC.  
This option was found to be not feasible due to reliability and safety concerns. Even with 
the expansion of the station fence, it would still not meet National Grid clearance 
standards. In addition, using a mobile long term during the construction of the station is a 
reliability concern.  
DER/NWA Alternative:   
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

Description: 
 
This project calls to construct a second bank at New Krumkill station, with existing 
4.16kV feeders being converted and replaced and breakers bring converted and replaced.  
 
Delmar TB1 and TB2 (4.8kV) will also be replaced with a single 34.5/13.2kV, 
12/16/20MVA bank. Approximately 12 miles of overhead distribution will be 
reconductored or converted to 13.2kV operation.

C083927, C083911, C083928, C083929, 
C083916, C083917, C083920, C083926, 
C050245, C049685 
Delmar Substation Work 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The primary driver for this project is asset condition. Delmar Station is a two-bank, 
34.5/4.8kV station with 6 feeders. Both transformer banks, TB1 and TB2, are on the five-
year replacement list.  Delmar TB1 is 65 years old and shows signs of deteriorated paper 
insulation and contaminated oil.  Delmar TB2 was gassing and being monitored on a 
monthly basis but is presently stable.  Also, this transformer has contaminated oil, 
elevated levels of combustible gases, high-energy arcing gases, and the LTC 
malfunctions. TB2 is 57 years of age and there is no spare. In addition to the transformer 
issues, this station has no room for expansion and the existing metalclad is in poor 
condition. 
 
 In addition, this will resolve a MWh criteria violation at the New Krumkill substation 
due to an outage of the existing Transformer Bank 2. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This project allows for future load growth within the Vista Tech Park and the Delmar 
area. This project increases reliability by converting Delmar station and retiring Juniper 
station, solving asset condition issues and reducing the size of a 4.8kV island.  
 
Alternatives: 
 
Construct a new substation at the intersection of Delmar Bypass and Van Dyke Road in 
the Town of Bethlehem called Van Dyke Substation. This alternative has faced 
insurmountable zoning and permitting challenges. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
An NWA solicitation found that the traditional solution was a better option for customers. 
An RFP was released in 2017 and solicitation was closed in 2019 because no NWA 
projects were able to pass a benefit cost analysis. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA): N/A

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
Mill Street substation has number of deteriorated assets identified by an asset condition 
report.  It is necessary to rebuild Mill Street substation.  During the rebuild the three 
transformers, the control house, and the lightning protection will be replaced. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Mill Street substation serves six underground network feeders and four overhead 
distribution feeders in the City of Watertown.  Mill Street Substation has number of 
deteriorated assets identified by an inspection report.  The three transformers have 
internal deterioration and high levels of combustible gases and need to be replaced.  The 
control house in the substation has no security and the foundation is crumbling.  It is 
necessary to rebuilt Mill Street substation to eliminate the asset condition issues. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Mill Street substation serves six underground network feeders and four overhead 
distribution feeders in the City of Watertown.  Maintenance on this substation will 
improve customer reliability by preventing a station outage upon failure of a deteriorated 
asset.  Mill Street substation serves 9,534 customers and repairing the substation will help 
prevent the loss of power for these customers. 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 316 of 457

Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 317 of 457 
C084102 
MILL ST TB Replacements 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Alternatives: 
 
The alternative is to repair the assets upon failure which would result in 9,534 customers 
without power. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
As the project need is driven by asset condition, it does not meet the NWA suitability 
criteria thresholds. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

Program Name: 
 
Communications for Voltage Regulators and Switched Capacitors 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: 
 
C084103 - COMMUNICATIONS FOR REGS & CAPS-NYW, 
C084104 - COMMUNICATIONS FOR REGS & CAPS-NYC, 
C084105 - COMMUNICATIONS FOR REGS & CAPS-NYE 
 
Description: 
 
This program will deploy communications via a cellular radio to existing switched 
capacitors and voltage regulators throughout the Distribution electric system in Upstate 
NY. The added communications will be integrated with the Company’s SCADA system 
and be used in providing near real-time measurements of system performance to enable 
engineering and operations personnel to better manage the electric distribution system. 
 
The data will be provided through the use of existing sensor measurements local to each 
device and will yield interval feeder loading, voltage information, and real-time status of 
the device (i.e.: closed/open for switched capacitors) that will foster more granular 
evaluations of system performance in support of distribution system planning, hosting 
capacity analysis and consideration of non-wires alternative solutions.  The added 
communications will provide real-time information at locations previously not monitored 
along the feeder downstream from the substation breaker.  
 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 331 of 457


--- Page 333 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 332 of 457 
C084103, C084104, C084105 
Communications for Regs & Caps NY  - 
West, Central, and East Divisions 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The driver of this program is to provide reliable and high quality power at an overall 
lower cost to our customers by enabling engineering and operational personnel to have a 
greater degree of insight into the electric delivery system with increased granularity 
throughout the distribution system. 
 
Achieving the ability to monitor the loading and voltage along various points of 
distribution feeders in near real time will provide the necessary data to evaluate and 
initiate system improvement opportunities that will reduce premature failures, mitigate 
voltage irregularities, reduce emergency manpower response, increase customer 
satisfaction and improve reliability indices. The increase in data points along the feeders 
through these devices would enable greater visibility into the system for both control 
center operations and system planning studies. This additional monitoring can be 
integrated into Advanced Distribution Management System applications as well. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The added cellular radio communication will provide operational benefits to allow the 
distribution system to be operated in a more efficient manner resulting in lower costs to 
customers.  The available data will enable better decisions regarding diagnosing and 
locating a fault or load transferring during peak load conditions. Bringing the data into 
the SCADA system allows for more granular information on an 8760 hourly basis across 
the entire year which will allow actual system data to be used for DER interconnection 
studies in locations where engineering model assumptions are required today. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Do Nothing:  The company would continue to utilize the data available to plan and 
operate the system without the increased granularity on devices along the distribution 
circuits, resulting in continued assumptions in specific areas along the feeders. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  None 
 
Description: 
 
This project calls for the installation of voltage regulators on several feeders in the 
Watertown area amongst several substations. The purpose of this project is to increase the 
feeder tie capability in the area in the event of an interruption at one of the surrounding 
13.2kV substations.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
Currently the feeder ties in the Watertown area are limited in the load they can carry due 
to the voltage profile.  Multiple feeder upgrades are required to beef up circuit ties so that 
they can carry load upon circuit or substation loss.  The following regulator changes are 
required:  
Upgrading the mainline 167KVA voltage regulator to 333KVA regulator on West Adams 
87753 (Pole 29, TD 6230, LN 10)  
 
Upgrade two sets of 167KVA voltage regulators on Coffeen 76053 to two sets of 333 
KVA voltage regulators (Pole 14, TD 6211, LN 189) and (Pole 2, TD 6211, LN 146)   
 
Addition of 3-167KVA voltage regulators on West Adams 87753 (Pole 128, TD 6230, 
LN 10)  
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 335 of 457


--- Page 337 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 336 of 457 
C084106 
Coffeen Dist Line Work 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Addition of 3-167KVA voltage regulators on East Watertown 81756 (Pole 29, TD 6211, 
LN 6) 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
There are over 10,000 customers in the Watertown area.  Having available feeder ties that 
can handle load for loss of a substation or distribution circuit improves reliability.   
 
Alternatives: 
 
The alternative is to not install the feeder upgrades and to risk the loss of up to 10,000 
customers upon loss of a substation transformer. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
An NWA is being pursued to eliminate the load at risk (currently in evaluation phase of 
solicitation process). The feeder upgrades will enhance the tying capabilities with 
neighboring substations and allow ties to pick up load. This project complements the 
NWA being procured as part of a hybrid solution. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

C084107

Multi-Value

X 
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  None 
 
Description: 
 
Dexter substation has a number of deteriorated assets identified by an asset condition 
report.  It is necessary to replace the three transformers and install a high side vacuum 
breaker for the transformer protection.  The high side lightning protection also needs to 
be replaced. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Dexter substation serves one overhead distribution feeder and has several deteriorated 
assets identified by an inspection report.  The three single phase transformers have all 
exceeded their in service designed lifetime.  All the transformer accessories are original 
to the units.  The transformers have minimal protection by the fuses and are subject to 
through-fault in case of a bus fault.  It is necessary to rebuilt Dexter substation to 
eliminate asset condition issues.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Maintenance on this substation will improve customer reliability by preventing a station 
outage upon failure of a deteriorated asset.  Dexter substation serves 112 customers (one 
of which is the Watertown Airport) and repairing the substation will help prevent the loss 
of power for these customers.

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  None 
 
Description: 
 
Leray substation has several deteriorated assets identified by an asset condition report.  It 
is necessary to replace the transformer and install a high side vacuum breaker for the 
transformer protection.  The distribution feeder protection and the high side lightning 
protection also needs to be replaced. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Leray substation serves one overhead distribution feeder and has number of deteriorated 
assets identified by an asset condition report.  The three-phase transformer has exceeded 
the in service designed lifetime.  All the transformer accessories are original to the unit.  
The transformer has minimal protection by the fuses and are subject to through-fault in 
case of a bus fault. The distribution protection is a recloser that has also exceeded its in 
service designed lifetime.  It is necessary to rebuilt Leray substation to eliminate asset 
condition issues.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Maintenance on this substation will improve customer reliability by preventing a station 
outage upon failure of a deteriorated asset.  Leray substation serves 633 customers and 
repairing the substation will help prevent the loss of power for these customers.

Coffeen replace TB3

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  None 
 
Description: 
 
Coffeen TB3 serves three overhead distribution feeders and currently tested positive for 
high levels of gases in the transformer.   As a result, the transformer has been de-rated 
and needs to be replaced.  Coffeen TB3 will be replaced to match the Coffeen TB4 
transformer.  The new transformer will be 25MVA 115/13.2kV. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Coffeen TB3 has been de-rated and needs to be replaced.  Coffeen TB3 is a 20MVA and 
Coffeen TB4 is a 25MVA transformer. Coffeen TB3 is currently unable to carry the 
entire substation upon loss of TB4.  TB3 needs to be replaced to enable the transformer to 
carry more peak load upon loss of TB4.   
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Coffeen TB3 serves 3,571 customers and Coffeen TB4 serves 3,539 customers.  Failure 
of TB3 during summer peak will put 3,571 customers at risk for a power outage.  Loss of 
TB4 will result in 3,539 customers without power because TB3 cannot handle the 
additional load.  There are approximately 264MWh’s at risk upon the loss of TB3.  
Replacing TB3 will prevent the risk for the customers on TB3 and TB4. 
 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 352 of 457


--- Page 354 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan

Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 353 of 457 
C084109 
Coffeen replace TB3 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Alternatives: 
 
The alternative is to repair the assets upon failure which would result in 3,571 customers 
without power. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
This project will complement an NWA solution. This project is part of a hybrid solution 
to meet area needs, and an NWA is currently under evaluation to meet the capacity needs 
in this area. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

Program Name:  
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  C084111 
 
Description: 
 
West Adams substation serves four overhead distribution feeders.  The West Adams 52 
circuit is expected to add 6.2MVA of new load for the customer Agbotic.  In order to 
accommodate this new load the construction of two new distribution feeders is 
needed.  The West Adams 52 circuit will be split into two new feeders. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Loss of TB1 at West Adams substation will result in approximately 528MWhrs at risk, 
which is above the 240MWhr criteria.  Load is expected to grow because of new 
customers.  The projected additional load is expected to exceed the West Adams TB1 
summer normal rating and exceed the West Adams circuit 52's summer normal rating.  
The installation of a second bank a West Adams eliminates the MWhr violations.  The 
construction of 2 new distribution feeders will accommodate new load growth. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The construction of two new distribution feeders is to accommodate load growth on the 
West Adams 52 circuit.  The two new circuits can facilitate new load and create 
additional feeder ties.  The additional feeder ties will improve distribution reliability upon 
a power outage.

C084110 
West Adams New Feeders for TB2 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Install a larger transformer at West Adams substation and install new feeder getaway 
cables to accommodate the new load. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
As the project is driven by reliability, it does not meet the NWA suitability criteria 
thresholds. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally 
increases the threshold for certain higher cost interconnection upgrades (such as 3V0). 
This will enable PV and ESS to interconnect at higher volumes with a lower marginal 
interconnection cost.  
 
VVO directly contributes to system efficiency, contributing to EE targets, as well as 
reducing energy required from carbon heavy generation (by reducing the total energy 
procured from NYISO). Increasing efficiency also reduces peak loading, which may 
decrease the need for fossil fuel peaking plants.

Program Name:  
Feeder Resiliency 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C084876 (West), C084879 (East), C084878 
(Central) 
 
Description: 
 
This program is intended to create high value feeder ties to enhance distribution system 
resiliency. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Resiliency projects are intended to ensure the electric power system has the ability to 
recover quickly following a disaster or, more generally, the ability of preparing for 
extraordinary and high-impact, low-probability events and rapidly recovering from these 
disruptive events. Projects identified as part of this program will ensure that recovery 
from an outage in targeted areas will be quicker though the generation of new feeder ties. 
Additionally, this program will be used to build connections to adjacent substations 
eliminating the need for the installation of a mobile transformer in the event of a 
substation transformer failure.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The use of new feeder ties will assist with restoration times when an outage occurs. These 
new ties also offer improved flexibility for system operation by allowing the system to be 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 361 of 457


--- Page 363 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 362 of 457 
C084876, C084879, C084878  
Feeder Resiliency 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
reconfigured in new ways. The customer experience will improve in the areas where 
these projects are implemented.  
 
Alternatives: 
 
Do nothing and leave the system in its current state.  
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Since the 
justification for this project is Resiliency in nature, it was determined that this project will 
not be evaluated for a potential NWA.      
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Resiliency spends improve the system's ability to re-energize portions of the system post 
contingency. These projects will generally reduce the duration of outages for load and 
generation customers. This means clean generation (such as solar PV) will have increased 
deliverability, increasing the average carbon emissions per MWh in NY with a cleaner, 
more deliverable, supply.

Program Name: 
NWA Interconnection 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C084925 
 
Description: 
 
This project will enable the interconnection of an anticipated NWA at the Coffeen 
Substation. These costs cover required upgrades for interconnection as outlined in the 
latest Energy Service Bulletin, as well as some costs to enable the dispatch and operation 
of this NWA during times of grid need. These costs, which normally would be directly 
attributable to the DER developer/owner, have been included in our capital plan (and the 
benefit cost analysis) for the NWA in accordance with the parameters set forth in the 
Commission’s December 13, 2018 Order Establishing Energy Storage Goal and 
Deployment Policy (“Storage Order”) in Case 18-E-0130. The required upgrades will 
depend on the technical details of the winning bidder(s), most importantly the exact 
location, size, and technology of the DER. The Company is currently finalizing bid 
evaluation and has a grasp on the potential upgrades needed to interconnect the potential 
winning bidder(s). These upgrades will include substation protection upgrades (such as 
3V0) and the standard PCC interconnection package, and will likely include additional 
distribution upgrades such as 3 phase extension and/or reconductor or voltage regulator 
upgrades.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
These upgrades are required to enable the 3rd party NWA to meet its designed and 
contractually obligated use case, providing post contingency capacity support (i.e. 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 364 of 457


--- Page 366 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 365 of 457 
C084925 
NWA Interconnection 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
reliability support). Without these upgrades, the NWA would not be able to meet the need 
defined in the Request for Proposal (RFP) or Energy Services Agreement (currently 
being drafted).   
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Customers served by Coffeen Station would be at risk of experiencing extended 
interruptions of service given a single station contingency. This NWA will ensure the 
load at risk (given a contingency) is within planning criteria limits. Costs for the 
interconnection have been included in a benefit cost analysis that has been reviewed with 
DPS Staff and will be filed with the PSC prior to project implementation. This benefit 
cost analysis confirmed pursuing this NWA opportunity is beneficial for society 
(customers).  
 
Alternatives: 
 
There are no cost-effective alternative means of interconnecting this DER to the 
distribution system. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
This project is required to enable the NWA to meet it’s intended use case, improving grid 
reliability. This cannot be deferred / eliminated with NWAs.  
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
This project will directly increase DER capacity by at least 6MW in NY State by 
enabling the procurement of an NWA.

Program Name:  
OpTel 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:   
 
C084927 - DMX Replacement, C084525 - DMX Projects 
C084929 - Critical and Key Facilities 
C084926, C085950 - SCADA Analog Replacement 
C083767, C083766, C083768 - Telecomm Migration East, Central and West 
C081809 - EMS/RTU for DSCADA TRANS 
C085421 - Batavia Ring 
 
Description: 
 
The OpTel program. 
 
DMX Replacement/ DMX Projects: The purpose of this project is to identify, design, 
procure, and implement packet-based fiber equipment to replace the existing end-of-life 
Nokia (formerly Alcatel-Lucent) Data Multiplexer (DMX) Synchronous Optical 
NETwork (SONET) equipment. The SONET provides a redundant ring communication 
topology network linking critical transmission substations and corporate facilities over 
both private and leased fiber with microwave linkage as well.  The replacement 
technology and equipment will provide higher bandwidth and additional capability for 
future telecom network expansion, such as the Critical and Key facilities project.

Operational Telecommunications (OpTel)

The Critical and Key Facilities: The purpose is to upgrade substations and critical NG-US 
locations from antiquated leased circuits to a modern private telecom network.   The 
upgraded private telecom network will provide enhanced backhaul capabilities for current 
operational needs.  This will also provide communications for digital substations, 
backhaul capabilities for Tier 3 operations in support of grid modernization efforts 
proposed as well as DERs.    
 
The SCADA Analog Replacement: The purpose is to replace leased telecommunications 
circuits across New York, for both distribution and transmission.   These circuits will be 
migrated to our private telecom network or alternative modernization technology, not 
covered under other projects. 500 of these circuits are part of an expired contract. Verizon 
has stated that these contract circuits will have significant monthly price hikes beginning 
in May 2021 and are targeted for disconnect in the next three years.  These circuit 
migrations will be prioritized based on need and align with other substation projects 
where possible.  This will enhance our current SCADA communications and provide 
future communication options for digital substations, backhaul capabilities and Tier 3 
operations in support of grid modernization efforts proposed as well as DERs.   It will 
also prevent circuit outages caused by Verizon disconnects which would negatively 
impact the stability of the power grid.  
 
Telecomm Migration East, Central and West: The replacement of existing leased line 
communication circuits currently used for teleprotection with National Grid owned 
fiber/microwave and shared fiber communications.  These are critical circuits used to 
protect our electric transmission lines and help ensure the stability and reliability of our 
electric transmission network.  Upon completion, National Grid will have complete 
maintenance & operational control of these critical circuits.   This is a critical since 
Verizon has stated that have no technology option in their future network for the 
teleprotection circuit type.  
 
EMS/RTU and DSCADA Trans Projects: The purpose is to enhance Regional Control 
Center Operations through the implementation of a dedicated Supervisory Control and 
Data Acquisition (SCADA) system along with a Distribution Management System 
(DMS). This requires the expansion of Remote Terminal Units (RTUs) in the Company’s 
substations and the reconfiguration of some RTUs that are managing both transmission 
and distribution assets.  This project supports control center capabilities by installing and 
reconfiguring RTUs in substations in which the control and monitoring of T&D assets 
utilize a common RTU.
