Project Justification: 
 
DMX Replacement/DMX Projects  
The critical driver for this project is that Nokia ceased manufacturing the DMX fiber 
equipment in 2018 and the "standard" technical support will be discontinued by the end 
of 2021. To bridge the timeframe from the current End-of-Life (EoL) to the 
implementation of replacement technology / equipment, NG purchased an inventory of 
spare equipment in June 2018 that is estimated to cover a three to five-year timeframe if 
the equipment does not experience any increase in typical failure rates.  This equipment 
will also be used for further expansion of NG private telecom network and infrastructure. 
 
Critical and Key Facilities  
The company (NG-US) needs to expand the current private telecom network to support 
growth in line with multiple grid modernization initiatives.  This is a multiyear 
deployment and as expected growth continues for a reliable, resilient, scalable network, 
the network expansion must support this.  Doing so will allow for improvement of 
current needs and for future needs as well.  The replacement of 3rd party analog circuits 
along with the growing need to transport more data at lower latencies in a robust and 
secure manner will continue for the next several years.  This same infrastructure will also 
provide capabilities to support the backhaul of field area network devices, such as Volt 
Var Optimization (VVO), capacitor banks, line reclosers, along with Distributed Energy 
Resources (DERs), reducing costs and dependencies of phone companies.    
 
The SCADA Analog Replacement  
The driver for these changes is the phone companies’ position that these circuits are at an 
end of life condition.  This is a multiyear deployment and as expected growth continues 
for a reliable, resilient, scalable network, the company needs to upgrade obsolete DS-0 
circuits to newer technology and the solutions will allow for the expansion of our current 
private telecom network to support all growth in line with grid modernization.    Doing so 
will allow for improvement of current needs but for future needs as well.  The 
replacement of over 1,000 obsolete leased DS-0 circuits, of which over 500 are part of an 
expired contract, along with the growing need to transport more data at lower latencies in 
a robust and secure manner will continue for the next several years.  This same 
infrastructure will also provide capabilities to support the backhaul of field area network 
devices, such as Volt Var Optimization (VVO), capacitor banks, line reclosers, along 
with Distributed Energy Resources (DERs), reducing costs and dependencies of 3rd party 
communication carriers.    
 
Telecomm Migration East, Central and West  
National Grid currently relies on a mixture of communication systems across its service 
territory to protect its transmission lines and to monitor and control substation assets. 
These protection systems must remain operable before, during, and after a transmission 
fault, which is referred to as Class A service. National Grid networks are engineered for 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 369 of 457


--- Page 371 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 370 of 457 
C084927  
Operational Telecommunications (OpTel) 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
99.999% availability. Without this requirement, the protection schemes could fail in the 
event of a fault and be incapable of performing their stated purpose. Many of the circuits 
that comprise the communication network between stations are not owned by National 
Grid but are instead leased from external telecommunications providers. National Grid 
leases approximately 200 teleprotection communication circuits, 115kV and above, from 
Foreign entities in New York, with 173 circuits being owned by Verizon, its primary 
telecom provider across its entire service territory. These 200 circuits encompass 150 
separate substations across National Grid’s service territory. Verizon has told us that they 
will no longer offer DS0 circuits and that T1(DS1) circuits may be phased out beginning 
in the next few years. This process will begin in stages as various types of 
communication are retired over a multiple year period. To guarantee greater control and 
performance of the communication network and to avoid the significant yearly OPEX 
costs of leasing communications, National Grid is planning a private communication 
network for its transmission system. This purpose-built network will provide more 
reliable service for these critical circuits at a lower operational cost. The private telecom 
network will incorporate a combination of solutions including private fiber optic cables 
(preferred option), microwave links, or limited use of shared fiber routes. 
 
EMS/RTU and DSCADA Trans Projects 
Increasing volumes of distribution assets to be monitored and controlled, both Company 
assets and those of Distribution Energy Resources (DERs) providers, is driving the need 
to expand the capabilities of the SCADA system. This will also include RTU 
communications to substations that currently do not have it.  The proliferation of 
distributed intelligent devices in support of distribution system operations also increases 
cyber security risks. As part of this expansion the Company proposes to separate 
Transmission and & Distribution (T&D) SCADA systems to improve database 
performance and enhance cyber security and NERC CIP protections related to the Bulk 
Electric System (BES). 
  
Customer Benefit: 
 
Operational telecommunications remain foundational elements to grid modernization 
activities as these provide the media over which the devices interface in order to be 
functional.  Upgrade of analog communication circuits is consistent with the Company’s 
goal of improving reliability across the system.  Private, dedicated circuits that provide 
time certainty in signal speeds are necessary to ensure the reliability and security of the 
electric grid.  Multiple tiers of modernizations will affect improved communications 
performance, which will enable enhanced system reliability, quicker restoration, and 
provide customers with more timely information, allowing for more energy choices.  A 
private network solution to replace leased communications avoids the uncertainty of 
third-party service.

There is an increasing volume of assets that need to be monitored and controlled, both 
Company assets and those of Distribution Energy Resources (DERs) providers, is driving 
the need to expand the capabilities of the SCADA system. The proliferation of distributed 
intelligent devices in support of distribution system operations also increases cyber 
security risks. As part of this expansion the Company proposes to separate T&D SCADA 
systems to improve database performance and enhance cyber security and NERC CIP 
protections related to the Bulk Electric System (BES). 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Due to EoL of the current Nokia DMX equipment, this puts NG-US on a critical path to 
identify and source a replacement technology and associated equipment to prevent the 
degradation or loss of key components of the operational telecom network, which could 
have significant impacts on safety and operational awareness, including substation 
protection, and operational control of the grid. The DMX replacement will serve as the 
primary backhaul and communication path for all NG-US operational equipment 
including Tiers 1, 2 & 3. 
 
Due to the expiration of our current contract with Verizon for over 500 DS0 circuits and 
the obsolescence of this technology affecting more than 500 additional DS0 circuits, 
which could have significant impacts on safety and operational awareness, including 
substation protection, and operational control of the grid.  The replacement of these 
circuits with new private/hybrid solutions will provide replacement of current technology 
and future growth capabilities. 
 
Verizon provides the majority of 3rd party carrier communications circuits to NG-US.  A 
solution proposed by Verizon to convert the existing analog services being 
decommissioned to digital circuits via Verizon fiber does not meet the entire suite of 
communications requirements outlined by the Company’s Protection Engineering 
department. Furthermore, Verizon fiber is not available everywhere.  This also, drives 
continuous OPEX spend and does not prepare NG-US adequately for grid modernization.   
 
A private telecom network solution to replace leased communications would provide the 
company with control of our communications systems and allow us to prioritize service 
restoration.   In addition, it would improve reliability and eliminate the risk of changes in 
the service provided by the phone companies.

project based on a societal cost test and a future looking scenario and predicts the Clean 
Innovation portfolio of projects will have a BCA ratio of at least a 1.0. 
 
Alternatives:

state’s goals without negatively impacting the grids safety and reliability.

(MVT) 
  
  
  
  
  
 
 
Program Name: 
3V0 Cost Share Program 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
A significant amount of solar and wind power distributed generation (DG) capacity is 
being installed at the distribution level in NY and is expected to increase more to meet 
renewable energy mandates. As the DG sites begin to saturate distribution circuits at 
increasingly high levels, depending on the protection schemes in place at the given 
substation, zero sequence overvoltage (“3Vo”) protection equipment may be required.  
 
 DGs on Delta-Wye (or Ungrounded Wye-Wye) connected transformers cannot 
contribute zero sequence ground fault current during single line to ground faults on a 
transmission line. This causes the voltages on the unfaulted phases to rise significantly 
and rapidly. These overvoltage’s have the potential to exceed insulation levels of the 
substation and transmission line equipment, and the maximum continuous operating 
voltage of surge arresters.  In order to detect these overvoltage conditions, 3Vo protection 
on the primary side of the transformer is a standard method employed by National Grid. 
This 3Vo protection will open all feeder protective devices in the substation to disconnect 
all possible DG sources from the bus, thereby stopping the DG from contributing to the 
transmission-side fault condition.  
 
As DG penetration levels continue to increase, the need for 3Vo is becoming more 
frequent.  From the industry perspective, developers are continually seeking to 
interconnect in the most time efficient fashion for their projects to begin generating 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 380 of 457


--- Page 382 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 381 of 457 
C084930 
3V0 and LTC Upgrades 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
revenue.  As such, the development community has a vested interest in shortening 
construction timelines and reducing the cost impact wherever possible.  In response to 
this societal and developmental need, National Grid is developing a proactive 3Vo 
program for the state of New York (NY). This program will cover the installation of 3Vo 
protective devices at 22 NY substations for 26 transformers over a 4-year period.    
 
To recoup the investment costs, NMPC will charge a pro-rated fee to all applicants with 
DG systems above 50 kW who connect to the upgraded substation transformer banks. 
The fee will be based on the estimated common system upgrade costs divided by a factor 
that represents the substation transformer bank’s capacity. The factor is projected to be 80 
percent of the smaller of the respective substation transformer bank’s capacity. This 
assumption ensures each bank is protected in case of a bank failure or outage. This also 
allows for up to 20 percent of the substation transformer bank’s capacity to be used by 
DG projects under 50 kW, which are primarily residential and small commercial projects.  
For accounting purposes, the Company will place the cost of the common system 
upgrades as well as fees received from the DG applicants in a regulatory asset. To the 
extent the fees do not equal the costs, the Company will recover or pass back the residual 
balance in the regulatory asset in a future proceeding. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Expanding success from the REV DG Interconnection Demonstration project to 
substations with high levels of DG in the queue. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Proactive 3Vo installation will make substations DG ready and reduce construction 
timelines for DG developers. Reducing DG interconnection timelines will help accelerate 
New York’s renewable energy goals by allowing DG’s to generate renewable energy 
faster than current interconnection timelines. The cost is also only for systems 50 kW and 
greater. Typical residential installation is under the 50 kW system threshold. This cost 
will not be passed onto residential DG customers with up to 20% of the transformer 
banks nameplate being used by DG projects under 50 kW. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
The alternative is to have any single project that exceeds the 3V0 trigger limits and the 
associated developer pay the full cost of the 3V0 upgrade.

Program Name:  
Metalclad Switchgear 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
This program is to replace metal clad switchgear that is in poor condition. Individual 
units will be evaluated based on original manufacturer support, maintenance history, 
visual inspection, InfraRed, or acoustic testing that can determine failing insulation 
components on live equipment. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Metalclad switchgear units manufactured prior to the 1970's are being targeted for 
replacement due to their asset condition. The enclosure is not of a robust design; the 
gaskets are thin and deteriorated allowing moisture ingress. These types of enclosures 
have a history of failure and are being addressed under the Metalclad Switchgear 
Strategy. Due to the design of metal clad switchgear equipment failure has the potential 
to result in sustained customer outages. The Metal Clad Switchgear program will allow 
National Grid to assess its Metal Clad fleet health, importance factor, and rank units in 
terms of risk so that they are properly prioritized for asset replacement. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Replacement of this equipment in a controlled manner mitigates the risk of in-service 
failure of deteriorated assets which can lead to long-term interruptions and unplanned

Program Name:  
Microgrid 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:   
 
N/A 
 
Description: 
 
A storage solution totaling 5 MW and 15 MWh to mitigate the impact for the loss sub-
transmission 23 kV supply lines that supply the Gilman Town area. In addition to 
providing reliability support the storage asset will participate in the NYISO to increase 
the benefits but be optimized to fulfill the #1 priority of reliability. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
Radial 23 kV kV sub-transmission line from Northville to Gilmantown that supplies ~5 
MVA of coincident peak load. The feeders supplied from these stations are typically on 
the WPF (worst performing feeder) list. Outages experienced along this corridor impacts 
all substations and customers fed by the circuit (Wells, Charley Lake, Gilmantown 
Road). There is limited feeder ties and capacity and the traditional” reliability solution is 
predicted to be expensive. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Reduce long duration outages for all customers served from Wells, Charley Lake, Gilman 
Town Road Substations.

Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

Program Name:  
Switched Capacitor Program 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  N/A 
 
Description: 
 
A 4-year program to install new switched shunt 3 phase capacitor banks across NY 13.2 
kV systems in accordance to proposed planning criteria changes that set a reactive power 
threshold any time a feeder VAR load is greater than 450kVAR (3 phase) and has a 
loading of above 80% of normal feeder head rating. The switched shunt capacitors would 
all have communications and tie into the ADMS system for optimal control and operation 
once ADMS Is deployed. This program is for feeders we are not planning to deploy 
VVO-CVR on over the period of the rate case. If VVO-CVR is deployed to the feeders 
impacted by this program the capacitor banks will be integrated into the VVO-CVR 
scheme at that time. 
 
Deployment of ~175 3 phase capacitor banks on targeted 13.2 kV feeders.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
Analysis of all 13.2 kV feeders in NY revealed low power factor on many feeders that are 
managed by voltage regulator devices for voltage but have high losses and subsequently 
unnecessarily increase loading on those feeders. Following a Benefit Cost Analysis 
(BCA), it was determined the benefits based on a reduction in loading, bulk system VAR 
needs and losses out way the costs of adding the capacitor banks for feeders with greater 
than 80% loading. 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 388 of 457


--- Page 390 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 389 of 457 
C084938 
Switched Capacitor Program 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
 
 10 year BCA NPV of +$1.967M and a ratio of 1.37 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The BCA conducted is a societal cost test therefore providing benefits to the customers 
based on a reduction in customer bills over time via reduction wholesale VAR payments, 
decreased capital wire-based investments for highly loaded feeders and reduction in 
losses and therefore energy component of the customer bill.  
 
Alternatives: 
 
Solar PV smart inverters will likely be able to play a role in the future to help support 
control of optimal voltage and VAR on distribution feeders. However, their ability to 
regulate primary feeder voltage can be challenging (from MA solar Phase 2&3 and JU 
Smart Inverter WG). While autonomous localized control can certainly provide initial 
support, as penetration of solar PV increases communication back to an DERMS system 
will likely be required to help coordinate settings with other feeder voltage regulating 
equipment therefore increasing the costs. Also, smart inverters don’t commonly provide 
voltage support when the sun isn’t shinning. To conclude, it is thought solar PV smart 
inverters will likely become an addition to voltage regulators and switchable capacitors in 
the future. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
As descried above, solar PV smart inverters can certainly help with VAR and voltage 
control but will likely end up becoming integrated with the more traditional wire-based 
solutions. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Load relief upgrades directly increase the maximum hosting capacity of the local EPS 
(electric power system) by two mechanisms. Increased asset capacity allows for a higher 
reverse power flow. Additionally, it allows more load to interconnect, which generally 
increases the threshold for certain higher cost interconnection upgrades (such as 3V0). 
This will enable PV and ESS to interconnect at higher volumes with a lower marginal 
interconnection cost.  
 
VVO directly contributes to system efficiency, contributing to EE targets, as well as 
reducing energy required from carbon heavy generation (by reducing the total energy

Program Name:  
NY LTC Controller 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  There is a funding project for FERC D (C085942) 
and FERC T (C085943) substations. 
 
Description:  
 
The NY LTC Controller upgrade program is a distribution system program where Load 
Tap Changer (LTC) controllers are updated to enable new functionality and assist with 
Grid Modernization initiatives. This program is designed to update existing controllers to 
enable remote operation and centralized control (i.e.: ADMS) via up to date 
communications protocols. 
 
The installation of new LTC controllers helps to modernize National Grid’s distribution 
system by replacing outdated controls that may not have as many features or setpoints. 
The new controllers at substations across NY state will enable advanced functionality 
through remote operation. Once the Advanced Distribution Management System 
(ADMS) is fully deployed, an application will be able to link the LTC into its monitoring 
functions to optimize the setpoints of the controller. As Advanced Metering Infrastructure 
(AMI) is rolled out in NY, the data can create a customer meter point in the ADMS 
power-flow which can then be utilized to perform active control of the tap changer 
position. This will enable a statewide Conservation Voltage Reduction (CVR) program 
where these controls are deployed. The CVR application in ADMS will monitor customer 
voltage to maintain the lower end of ANSI c84.1 Range A (114V) across the substation’s 
feeders and actively adjust (in real time) based on the AMI data. This allows for 
reductions in energy consumption throughout the year, expected to be on average 
approximately 1% lower than if the Company were to use traditional control methods. 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 391 of 457


--- Page 393 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 392 of 457 
C085942, C085943 
NY LTC Controller 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
 
The Company has historically managed feeder head voltage primarily with the use of 
autonomously controlled LTC Transformers.  When installed, controls are typically 
programmed to maintain a specific voltage at its location as specified by a distribution 
planning engineer.  The new controllers enable more data to be visible in National Grid’s 
ADMS as the new controller would be integrated with Supervisory Control and Data 
Acquisition (SCADA). This gives operators real-time information on things such as tap 
position, raise/lower status, and control of the LTC itself. This will allow for quicker 
mitigation if an issue were to arise and allow for remote operation for any reason. 
 
The primary driver of this project is to reduce customer bills via a reduction in energy 
consumed through the operation of CVR, however in addition   will provide  improved 
power quality by monitoring and adjusting the voltage performance across the system in 
real time and may also help increase hosting capacity for DG (Distributed Generation) 
This proposed program adds a layer of control, via communication and increased data to 
respond to system dynamics in real time through ADMS or control center operator 
control. 
 
The proposal is to install new, updated LTC controllers on approximately 59 substations 
(86 LTCs) over the course of FY22-25. The stations that are targeted are expected to 
align with AMI and AMDS rollout plans and have a Net Present Value (NPV) of 
+$10.10M based on the expected 1% energy savings. 
 
Project Justification:  
 
There are several anticipated benefits of updating LTC controllers: 
• The addition of CVR functionality (once the ADMS application is enabled and 
AMI is deployed), via updated LTC controllers, in NY is expected to achieve 
around a 1% energy savings where deployed, but individual feeder benefits may 
vary based on characteristics.  
• The increased near real time operational data made available to the regional 
control centers, via data collected from the updated controllers, will support the 
improved management of the distribution system and assist in the integration of 
future distributed resources. 
• Actively maintaining proper voltage via an ADMS application will improve 
substation voltage control, keeping the voltage low, potentially allowing for 
higher DER penetration on its associated feeders. 
• Linking LTC Controls with ADMS enables the use of AMI data to help ensure 
voltage is maintained within ANSI c84.1 Range A criteria where the controllers 
are deployed.

          Page 392 of 457

C085942, C085943 
NY LTC Controller 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
• Updated controllers are expected to aide in the deployment of VVO/CVR across 
the distribution system. Since the VVO/CVR program requires updated LTC 
controllers, this can help to offset substation work in the future. 
 
Customer Benefit:  
 
Many customer load technologies in particular residential loads such as electric heating, 
air conditioning, refrigeration and other appliances operate most efficiently at 114V.  
Delivering voltages at the optimal levels reduces energy consumed behind the meter,  
T&D losses on the grid, improves service quality all resulting in lowers costs for 
customers. 
 
A benefit/cost 7-year analysis was completed that reveals a preliminary energy savings of 
approximately 1% and an NPV of +$10.10M. 
 
Alternatives:  
 
Continue with autonomous local control of Load Tap Changers. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative:  
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
The energy reductions will help contribute to the energy efficiency CLCPA state targets 
and may help increase greater levels. increased near real time operational data made 
available to the regional control centers, via data collected from updated LTC controllers, 
will support the improved management of the distribution system and assist in the 
integration of future distributed resources.

Program Name: 
Energy Storage Order Upgrades 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: C085964 
 
Description: 
 
This project will allow the radial supply line out of Booneville Station to island given an 
outage on that supply line, islanding up to 5 distribution stations near or in the 
Adirondack Park in Central NY. The island will be supported by a 20MW Energy 
Storage System (“ESS”) connected to the supply line. The ESS will be interconnected 
and operational by the beginning of 2023, in accordance with the parameters set forth in 
the Commission’s December 13, 2018 Order Establishing Energy Storage Goal and 
Deployment Policy (“Storage Order”) in Case 18-E-0130. The system is not currently 
capable of disconnecting from the bulk system and maintaining safe and reliable supply 
from only the 20MW ESS source (i.e. islanding). The system requires enhanced 
sectionalizing, communication, and protection capabilities to operate as an ESS sourced 
island, which will be provided with the following upgrades: 
 
• Additional high side station breakers for sectionalizing 
• Distribution breaker relay replacements 
• Station to Station communication upgrades 
 
Note, these costs do not include “interconnection costs” to fund upgrades to allow the 
ESS to safely interconnect and operate during normal system conditions (such as supply 
line extension and PCC recloser); those costs will be charged to the ESS 
owner/developer.  
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 395 of 457


--- Page 397 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 396 of 457 
C085964 
Energy Storage Order Upgrades 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Project Justification: 
 
These upgrades are required to enable the 3rd party 20MW ESS to meet its designed and 
contractually obligated use case, providing an island source following loss of the radial 
supply line to any or all of the 5 stations served by that line. Without these upgrades, the 
ESS would not be able to safely island the system post contingency.   
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
Customers in this region experience longer and more frequent outages than most 
customers in Upstate NY due to the radial nature of the line and challenges with 
maintaining reliability in and around the Adirondack Park. This alternative source will 
significantly reduce the duration of or totally eliminate most interruptions experienced by 
almost 7000 residential and about 700 commercial and industrial customers. The Energy 
Storage Order Upgrades cost has been included in a benefit cost analysis (reviewed with 
DPS Staff) for this Energy Storage Order procurement and confirmed to be beneficial to 
society (customers). 
 
Alternatives: 
 
There are few alternatives to meet the sectionalizing, communication, and protection 
needs to enable island operation. One of the alternatives considered was including other 
sources (such as diesel turbines or a flywheel) to supply the necessary fault current to 
operate existing protection, but this was found to be costlier and challenging from a 
permitting/land availability standpoint.  
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
This project is required to enable a DER (the 20MW ESS) to meet it’s intended use case, 
improving grid resiliency. This cannot be deferred / eliminated with NWAs.  
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
This project will directly increase Energy Storage capacity by 20MW in NY State by 
enabling the procurement of dispatch rights for a 3rd party system (per the Storage Order).  
 
Studies/References: 
 
Study Report Name (s):  N/A

Program Name:  
None 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: CD00782 Buffalo Station 122 Rebuild, CD00779 
Buffalo Station Rebuild D-Line, CD00780 Buffalo Station 122 23 kV 
 
Description: 
 
CD00782 Substation 
Install a two new 15kV Insulated Metalclad Switchgear (IMCS) each with a 2000A 
transformer secondary breaker, four 1200A feeder breakers, and one with a 2000A tie 
breaker and the other with a 2000A disconnect.  Two new 23-4.16kv 9.375 MVA 
Transformers  with LTC will be installed as well as 23kV breakers and disconnects with 
EMS control capability.  Retirement of 4-4.16kV transformers and all the equipment 
associated with the existing substation will take place after the new station is in service. 
 
CD00779 Distribution Line 
Install new 5" underground system along with new 750kcmil Al cables to supply the 
existing 4.16kV feeders from the new station.  Install two new feeders with 1000kcmil 
for line relief in the area.  Remove existing 4.16kV cables for Feeders 
12261,12262,12265 &12268 & 12272 from the substation to the first manhole outside of 
the station.  Rebuild approximately 2,100ft of overhead distribution to accommodate 
these two feeders.  It is anticipated that approximately 1500ft of new UG conduits, 3000 
feet of new UG cable and 5 new manholes will be needed for this project.  
 
CD00780 Sub Transmission line 
Install approximately 500ft of new duct bank and 3000 ft of cable in new UG conduit

Project Justification: 
 
The Primary Driver for the station rebuild at Buffalo 122 is asset condition.  This leads to 
safety / reliability risks inherent from operating aging equipment related to the following 
specific issues: 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The recommended option will resolve all asset condition issues associated with the 
equipment at Buffalo Station 122 and help mitigate risks to reliability due to extended 
outages caused by equipment failure.  the recommended plan provides capacity to 
support economic development in the City of North Tonawanda and set the stage for 
future infrastructure development in the area. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Rebuild existing substation in place.  This alternative provides for the 
construction of a 15kv eight (8) feeder, metal enclosed switchgear placed at the existing 
station 122 site.  The scope of this option is similar to the preferred alternative.  the basic 
difference between the preferred alternative and alternative 1 is that the preferred 
alternative is a complete new installation while option 1 rebuilds  the existing equipment 
in place.  Replacing equipment in kind was attempted in the early 1990's and was 
abandoned due to high costs related to construction.  The station equipment is located in 
congested area and will be difficult to replace.  Personnel would need to be aware of 
added safety considerations with this alternative.  This option is not recommended due to 
constructability, added risk, additional outage requirements and incremental costs of 
replacing in kind as well as setting up Mobil in the Station 122 Yard. 
 
Alternative 2: Station rebuild with two 115/4.16kV Transformers.  This alternative uses 
two new 115:4.416kV, 10 MVA transformers with LTC, bank secondary breakers and 
bus tie breaker.  The amount of 115kV in the city is limited to a few corridors.  An 
approach to this alternative is extending 115kV lines 130/133 via underground duct 
system from Station 78 taps near Robinson St towards Sommer St.  However, this 
extension assumes the Company can receive timely approval of permits form city.  Also, 
this option would require construction of conduit which could be costly due to bedrock in 
the area 
 
Alternative 3: Install an open air station.  This alternative installs an open air substation in

Program Name:  
 
N/A 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:   
N/A 
 
Description: 
 
This program proposes to replace UG streetlight cable in targeted areas of high elevated 
voltage (EV) incidences, to significantly reduce future EV rates within the City of 
Buffalo. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
The main driver for replacing the UG streetlight cables in the City of Buffalo is to reduce 
the incidence of public exposure to elevated voltages due to deteriorated insulation on 
targeted streetlight cables. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The resolution of the EV conditions will reduce customer exposure to EV incidents 
within the specified geographic reporting boundary and is expected to result in an 
incident per mile rate consistent with other major metro areas.  This improvement would 
position the Company to petition the NY PSC to allow mobile inspections once per year 
rather the current mandated two inspections per year.  A reduced testing frequency could 
result in OPEX savings if approved by the NYS PSC. 
 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10)

Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 403 of 457 
CD00851 
UG SL Cable Replacement Program 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Alternative 1: Do Nothing will result in continuous Operation and Maintenance costs for 
mobile scanning twice a year and repairing on an ad hoc basis within the City of Buffalo.  
Continued degradation of the existing circuitry may cause an increase in the future EV 
incidents found during mobile testing.  The costs of these maintenance repairs are 
captured as expense funding under the divisional Operations OPEX budget.  Mobile 
scanning for Upstate NY costs $2.2 M (OPEX) and the current spend to scan the Buffalo 
area twice a year is approximately $1.98M.  This cost does not include the OPEX repair 
costs.  
 
This alternative is not recommended as the Company must respond to the public safety 
issue appropriately. 
 
Alternative 2: Replacement of all UG streetlight cable circuits that has experienced an 
EV incident would cost over $179M.  
 
This alternative is not recommended as a more targeted replacement program is more 
cost effective.  
  
Alternative 3: Replacement of the individual UG street light cable sections which have  
experienced an EV incident may be 20% less costly than the recommended option, 
however it is not recommended as it does not proactively address the public safety 
concerns of elevated voltages on the adjacent cable sections left in service. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA. Based on the 
Company’s evaluation, the need addressed by this project does not satisfy one or more 
element of the Suitability Criteria and will not be evaluated for a potential NWA.

CD00851 
UG SL Cable Replacement Program 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Background & Pictures 
 
The Underground (UG) Streetlight (SL) cable replacement program within the City of 
Buffalo replaces: 
• Leaded Cable 
• Damaged  
• Single Poly 
• Bare Neutral 
• Dated from 1900’s – 1960’s 
A fair amount of these replacements’ dates to the Pan-American Era; that brought 
electricity from Niagara Falls to Buffalo, NY.  During the Pan-American Exposition, the 
advent of the alternating current (AC) power transmission system in the US allowed 
designers to light the Exposition in Buffalo using power generated 25 miles away 
at Niagara Falls. 
 
Today, dated UG SL cable is replaced using directional bore methods, installing HDPE 
pipe, allowing more flexibility and less restoration. 
 
Please see some pictures from the City of Buffalo in the following pages. 
 
• Amherst & Peters Street

• Clinton & Hickory Street 
 
 
 
 
 
Pages 08-12 
• Heath & Cornell Street 
 
 
 
 
 
Pages 13-17 
• Kenmore Ave.

• Main & Best Street - 1115 Main at Best St.

Program Name:  
Reliability 
 
Associated Funding Numbers:  CD00964 
 
Description: 
 
This project is to add a second transformer & feeder at Long Road station in order to 
remove the MWHr violation due to a loss of transformer at the station as well as support 
additional load customers served by this station. The project will also include the 
construction of at least one new feeder to balance the load among the station and create 
additional feeder ties to increase resiliency. 
 
Project Justification: 
 
There is a MWHr violation at Long Road station when there is a loss of the single 
transformer. There has been significant interest on Grand Island over the last few years 
where there are also several large customers preparing to locate near Long Road Station 
including Project Olive The additional load projected in the area will only increase the 
need for a second transformer.  
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The addition of the second transformer will reduce the reliability impact of a transformer 
outage at the station. 
 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 444 of 457


--- Page 446 ---

Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 445 of 457 
CD00977 
Long Road 209 - Install TB2 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Increase the number of feeder ties to allow for load to switched away from the station 
during a transformer outage. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
The Company’s Non-Wires Alternative (NWA) Suitability Criteria considers the 
driver/spending rationale, timeframe, and potential costs to address a system need in 
determining whether that need can practicably be addressed with an NWA.  Due to the 
timing of the need, it was determined that this project will not be evaluated for a potential 
NWA.    
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for renewable energy resources DER (such as PV and ESS) to export 
power, thereby improving the economics of individual projects. Further, improved 
reliability mitigates the need for load customers to operate backup generation, which is 
typically natural gas or petroleum fueled generation.

Project / Program Name:   Electric Distribution Capability (EDC) 
 
Associated funding numbers: INVP 5471L & O, INVP 5890A-D & F 
 
Description:  Keeping our Customer’s energy supply affordable and reliable is a key to 
the overall economic, social and even medical health of our communities.   A critical part 
of our work in this area is assessing community needs and how our networks serve 
these.   Asset management and planning is the technical analysis, planning, options 
assessment & ultimately solution identification for investment is the process used to 
meet these needs.   In conjunction with the implementation of our Asset Management 
BMS standards we have identified that while our current suite of tools has met prior 
needs the advent of technologies such as predictive analytics, implementation of 
increasing grid digitization and the need to leverage data to optimize decision making is 
critical to mitigating bill impacts while meeting reliability cost and policy targets.    
 
To meet these goals, we must investment in modernizing key systems, further data 
integration and apply advancing analytic capabilities.  This program will address key 
needs throughout the distribution asset management, planning and distribution design 
process. 
 
Core components of this program will include: 
 
  
Distribution GIS Next Generation – INVP 5471L 
               The electric distribution Geographic Information System (“GIS”) is used in many 
Company electric distribution processes including asset management, operations, 
design, and engineering.   Most critically, GIS is used to provide a model of the 
Company’s distribution network, which we refer to as the “Connected Model”.   The 
connected model serves as the authoritative source of how our network transports 
electricity from the substation to the customers.   This model contains physical, 
electrical and logical relationships of our primary, secondary and downtown networks, 
protective, switchable & other assets and customer location & data.   This model is then 
used by downstream systems like Outage Management, Load Flow Planning (CYME), 
and, soon ADMS (Advanced Distribution Management System).   
  
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 447 of 457


--- Page 449 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 448 of 457 
INVP-5471L&O, INVP 5890A-D&F 
Electric Distribution Capability (EDC) 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
              The Company deployed its current GIS system (Smallworld 4.2) in 2011.   There are two 
main drivers for this investment.   First is age and performance challenges related to 
outdated underlying infrastructure.   As an example, we cannot migrate our current 
version to leverage cloud services to lower costs and increase scalability due to 
incompatibility.   The second driver is advancing business capabilities.   Examples include 
next generation tools to facilitate distribution design process and further advancing our 
ability to make the connected model available in CIM (Common Information Model) 
formats which will be necessary as we move toward a more interconnected grid 
requiring much greater levels of interoperability with industry solutions.    
  
              The business and engineering requirements to manage the distribution network is 
significantly different than those required for Transmission. Differences in how the 
networks operate, customer requirements, network complexity and sheer volume of 
assets are the primary reason we are requesting a separate GIS investment as requested 
as part of Project VOLT.   The enduring platform has not been decided at this time but 
the decision will incorporate an analysis of technical and business fit along with long 
term deployment and ownership costs or synergies with the proposed Transmission 
solution.   This analysis will be robust to ensure we make the most cost efficient decision 
while meeting the differing requirements. 
  
             CYME Gateway – INVP 5890B 
              CYME is the software used for load flow analysis on the electric distribution 
system.  This allows engineers to assess and create plans that address network impacts 
of potential DER interconnection, new system load, and many other scenarios.  With the 
increase in DER applications and the increasingly dynamic impacts to the electric 
network, the Company must enhance its ability to update our planning models more 
often and with additional data granularity. 
  
              This investment will continue the next step in Company’s migration toward near real-
time models that are automatically updated from the GIS connected model.   This work 
will leverage the connected model export enhancements of the Distribution GIS Next 
Generation project to enhance responsiveness to interconnection requests and provide 
network data through our DER System Portal (Distributed Energy Resources System 
Portal). 
  
             IDS Next Gen (Interruption Disturbance System) – INVP 5890D 
              IDS is used to track distribution/transmission/substation outages and disturbances and 
is integral to the Company’s ability to analyze and report on reliability 
performance.   This system was first deployed in 1999 and needs to be upgraded to 
more modern technology.  Additionally, enhancements to allow for greater data 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 448 of 457

07/22/2020 
 
integration with other analysis platforms and greater self-serve functionality are 
necessary to meet emerging business needs.  An analysis was conducted in 2018, which 
identified potential paths to address these needs.  This investment follows-on that 
effort to begin development and deployment starting in FY22.  During FY21 the 
company is conducting analysis to identify synergies with the Asset Management 
Insights work to minimize costs (to the extent possible).  Potentially this may include 
utilizing the same underlying technology, data integrations and process improvements.  
  
             Asset Management Insights -  Availability\Visualization\Analytics – 5471O 
             Changes to the modern electric grid and requirements to manage two-way power flows, 
ever increasing DER interconnection and emerging need to share granular, timely data 
needs require us to find better, faster and cost effective ways to meet customer and 
stakeholder expectations. 
  
        
At the same time technology that enables the collection, analysis and management of 
data are rapidly advancing.   Examples of these advanced technologies include data 
quality & governance toolsets, predictive analytics and AI (Artificial Intelligence). 
  
         
This investment is focused on applying these advanced technologies along with business 
process change to enable existing and future needs of the DSP (Distributed System 
Platform) future.   This suite of work includes grid facing and asset management use 
cases which focus on our commitment to continuous improvement.   We will apply 
these technologies to enhance of business ability to apply greater data driven insight to 
identify impending issues and react, challenge “status quo” operating and maintenance 
practices to find cost efficiencies and enhance reliability and analyze more solution 
scenarios to optimize capital network investments. 
  
          This investment is related but different from that proposed in Project VOLT.   Project 
VOLT will deliver core business process capability and data integration for Transmission 
and Substation (with Distribution Line expected to follow - FY26).  This is the how we 
will enhance and enable our business processes to ensure the right data is captured, 
managed and available at the right time.    
 
The Asset Management Insights investment will deliver a “next generation” asset 
analytics platform.   This platform will incorporate data being captured within the VOLT 
business processes, existing Distribution Line processes and externally available date 
(i.e. weather\climate) to unlock additional value through advanced analytics.   Benefits 
identified within the BCA are distribution asset management focused, however, this 
platform will be designed and developed with Transmission subject matter experts 
involved to ensure that it is applicable across the entire electric business.

In addition, this investment will be planned in coordination with our Grid Modernization 
Data Management investments. This will ensure technology, process and data alignment 
to leverage economies of scale and avoid redundancy to minimize overall cost. 
  
          The Company will undertake planning work in FY21 to identify the appropriate 
technology stack, use cases, and deployment paths. Development and deployment 
would take place starting in FY22   
  
          Copperleaf Investment Planning (D-Line) – 5890F 
              As part of Project VOLT an AIM (Asset Investment Management) platform will be 
deployed for Transmission and Substation work.   This is a follow-on investment to bring 
Distribution Line work into this platform in FY24/25.   This platform enhances our ability 
to manage our investments across the entire electric portfolio by providing functionality 
that provides a common, data driven approach to justify expenditures and model 
impacts of changes to capital and maintenance budgets.    Deploying this capability will 
enable faster assessment of project cost/benefit/risk, increase our ability to respond to 
business or resource changes and meaningfully communicate the impact to our 
customers, regulators and other stakeholders. 
          
Corporate PI – 5890A 
         
PI Historian is a system that currently stores data on the Company’s distribution and 
transmission networks coming from digital devices.   Originally, these systems were 
designed for use only in the Company’s Network Operations areas; however, efforts like 
hosting capacity analysis and DER opportunity zone identification haves shown that the 
data must be made more widely available.   The rich data contained in the PI Historian 
will provide greater insight to engineering, asset management and advanced analytics 
teams to achieve grid modernization goals. 
  
         
Specifically, this project will implement a replicated reporting environment that can be 
queried on demand without impacting the performance of the CNI (Critical Network 
Infrastructure) instance while honoring NERC critical infrastructure protection (“CIP”) 
restrictions.  The environment will be used to support the Distributed Generation (DG) 
Planning Portal, DG IOAP Phase 2 screening in addition to other modelling needs.  This 
project will also provide planning engineers and analysts with additional software tools 
to work with the data to better serve the Company’s customers and other Company 
stakeholders.

INVP-5471L&O, INVP 5890A-D&F 
Electric Distribution Capability (EDC) 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
         
Lastly, this project will allow the Company to consolidate data currently stored in 
separate systems and data stores, reducing complexity and enabling further analytical 
insights.  Data will be migrated from two additional datasets/systems.  These are: 
· 
Feedpro – a system used to record and store device readings that are not RTU 
(Remote Telemetry Unit) enabled and collected manually. 
· 
Network Device Settings – Many assets on our network have specific settings 
that are key to their proper operation.   This project will consolidate this 
information from several data sources allowing greater levels of data 
governance and furthering availability of this data to downstream systems 
and processes. 
  
              Computapole Replacement – Distribution Wood Pole Inspection System – INVP 5890C: 
        
Computapole is used to manage and deliver several of our inspection programs 
including wood pole.   The application is very aged and out of support from the 
vendor.   The Project VOLT Enablement scope seeks to deploy a new inspection system 
for our transmission and substation assets.  This project will add distribution line assets 
to the new system managed within Computapole.  This will allow the Company to 
continue to utilize a common platform and personnel resources to keep costs down. 
 
The EDC program is complementary to Project VOLT.  While the VOLT Enablement 
Program progresses the Company’s core enterprise EAM, Investment Management and 
work management capabilities, the EDC investment seeks to concurrently address 
critical data and analytical needs to meet changing customer needs and operational 
system requirements.  It is the Company’s goal to leverage these investments across 
transmission, substation and distribution line as appropriate.   
 
Initially, these will focus on distribution line assets. However, the strategic intention is to 
apply the analytical capabilities developed through this effort and lessons learned to 
transmission and substation.  Conversely, the Company anticipates leveraging the VOLT 
work and learnings to for distribution line starting in FY26.  Recognizing the significant 
cost and change management impacts that would be involved by simultaneously 
deploying the full VOLT and EDC scope to the entire electric business (T&D) we see this 
as a balanced approach that will address needs and contains bill impacts while 
progressing alignment.  Ultimately, the Company will continue to seek to leverage 
common platforms to contain costs and complexity, while still allowing flexibility to 
address unique requirements of each part of our business and impacts to customers and 
stakeholders. 
 
Project Justification:   
Exhibit___(EIOP-10)

Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan

Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 452 of 457 
INVP-5471L&O, INVP 5890A-D&F 
Electric Distribution Capability (EDC) 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
This project builds on the Asset & Data Management Maturity initiative that has been 
underway since FY19.   Improvements we are making in our processes need better, 
modern tools and data to unlock the full benefits.   Implementing this program will 
unlock this value through productivity savings, improved ability to optimize solutions 
(including non-wires), identify savings opportunities across whole life-cycle of our assets 
as well as enabling our ability to meet data sharing and interoperability needs as part of 
our emerging Distributed System Operator responsibilities.  In addition, several of these 
investments are necessary to address risk associated with systems that are on aged 
operating systems or hardware that are end of life. 
 
Customer Benefit:   
Customer benefits associated with this investment are primarily driven by cost 
containment while ensuring optimal data/risk driven network investments.    It will 
enable the company to identify and process greater levels of beneficial DER 
interconnection, help us meet environmental targets, maintaining/improving network 
reliability and enabling interoperability requires significant investment in our networks 
and tools to operate them.   It is critical that we optimize our planning and investment 
capabilities to find and deploy solutions that minimize bill impacts.   This investment is a 
key component to enabling our capability to identify those innovative and cost effective 
solutions. 
 
 
Alternatives:  
An alternative to this approach would be to leverage existing systems and build 
additional functionality that would provide some of the insight and data integration 
requirements.  This option may appear cheaper in the short term but presents several 
challenges.   This approach would not be recommended as it would keep us on 
outdated/unsupported platforms and hardware which are end of life and have 
performance issues.  In addition, it would not allow us to take advantage of industry 
learnings which have been incorporated in available software and keep pace with 
industry and technical capabilities our customers expect.  
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
Not Applicable 
 
Clean Leadership Community Protection Act (CLCPA) Alignment: 
This project will enable our ability to better identify and quantify environmental benefits 
as technical options are identified and analyzed.

Project / Program Name:  VOLT Enablement 
 
Associated funding numbers: TBD 
 
Description:  VOLT Enablement is a program intended to transform National Grid’s 
electric transmission and distribution business units to enable new asset management-
based capabilities.  The VOLT Enablement program is necessary to allow the Company 
to more effectively and efficiently manage its electric assets.  The program will transform 
National Grid’s capabilities in asset management, investment and resource planning, 
project management, field mobility, and asset accounting/reporting.  The program will 
implement three new IT systems, integrating those systems with existing corporate IT 
systems and data sources.  Standard business processes will be developed to leverage the 
integrated systems, share data, and minimize manual work activities.  Systems and 
processes to be implemented through the VOLT Enablement program will establish 
common ways of working, break down siloes, and replace non-value add work activities 
with activities that truly add value and insight.   
 
The three new IT systems to be implemented through the VOLT Enablement program 
are: (i) Enterprise Asset Management (EAM); (ii) Asset Performance Management 
(APM); and (iii) Asset Investment Planning (AIP).  The EAM will serve as a single 
repository for information on all electric T&D substation assets and T line assets, as well 
as serve as the work management platform for all the associated functions.  With EAM 
serving as the hub for asset management, National Grid will be able to trace lifetime costs 
back to every asset in the register.  Tracing lifetime costs—such as purchase price, 
installation, inspections, maintenance, repairs and removal—will enable whole-life 
costing capability to ensure we are optimizing replacement decisions by quickly 
removing high-cost assets from the system.   APM will leverage asset condition data, 
asset criticality scores and apply asset models to quantify asset health, facilitating the 
triggering implementation of risk-based asset maintenance and asset replacement 
decisions.  APM will incorporate both off-line inspection and test data as well as online 
asset monitoring information as inputs to the asset health models.  An improved 
understanding of the health and criticality of our assets, applying whole-life costing 
models will target inspection and maintenance activities as well as replacement decisions 
to bring efficiencies to our OPEX and CAPEX budgets.  AIP will collect all types of 
capital and operational expense investments (specific projects, programs, and blankets) 
and assess the risk/value of each investment to create prioritized and optimized work 
plans out to ten years or more.  The functionality of the AIP will facilitate investment 
scenario creation to efficiently create numerous investment portfolios as well as roll the 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 454 of 457


--- Page 456 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 455 of 457 
VOLT Enablement 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
individual risks/values up to the portfolio level.  Implementation of the AIP tool will 
enable the Company to better quantify the value of its capital investment plan and 
operating expense programs to regulators and communities, as well as facilitating 
improved decision making to benefit customers.     
 
These new tools will be integrated with the Company’s existing IT systems – namely the 
ESRI Graphical Information System, Oracle Primavera P6 project management system, 
and PowerPlan/SAP accounting systems – to share data, ensuring a “single-source of 
truth” while minimizing duplicative data entry activities.   
 
Work processes will be modified to ensure the out-of-the-box functionality of these 
systems is fully leveraged, thereby minimizing the need for costly software 
customizations. 
 
Project Justification:  Effective management of the condition of National Grid’s electric 
assets is required to achieve new levels of efficiencies and deliver a larger workplan 
while controlling cost increases.  Achieving these improved capabilities and levels of 
operational performance is not currently possible given our siloed systems and data as 
well as our manual work processes; VOLT Enablement is designed to address these 
issues. 
 
The primary objective of VOLT Enablement is to more effectively and efficiently 
manage National Grid’s CAPEX and OPEX budgets, so the Company can maximize the 
performance of the existing asset base and modernize the system to better serve 
customers and communities, all while mitigating upward cost pressures by driving cost 
efficiencies to control bill increases.   
 
Two of the key IT systems that the company use today—Computapole and STORMS—
are approaching end-of-life as vendor support is ending.  Vendor support for 
Computapole—the system that stores and tracks defects and resulting repairs for our 
mandated inspection and maintenance programs—is ending and the system is not 
compatible with newer Windows 7 and Windows 10 operating systems.  Some legacy 
Windows XP machines are being kept to maintain the full functionality of the 
Computapole application.  The STORMS work order system went through a partial 
upgrade in 2019—new servers and a upgraded scheduling module—with limited vendor 
support planned going forward.  Given that the parent company is not offering 
enhancements to their STORMS product a full-upgrade to a newer version is not 
possible. 
 
Customer Benefit:  VOLT Enablement will transform how National Grid manages its 
assets, CAPEX and OPEX work plans and projects.  Internal labor efficiencies will 
enable more timely project completion.  Improvement in these areas will reduce overall 
life cycle time of projects and the amount of “churn” in capital and operational work 
Exhibit___(EIOP-10) 
          Page 455 of 457


--- Page 457 ---
 
Significant Distribution Capital Investment Plan 
Projects and Programs 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
 
Page 456 of 457 
VOLT Enablement 
Version 1 – 
07/22/2020 
 
plans resulting in an opportunity to reduce costs and improve customer satisfaction.  
Given the current and projected economic climate, materials and contractor labor are 
trending up.  This program will facilitate improved visibility and planning allowing for 
more advanced resource procurement to help manage and mitigate some or all those cost 
increases.  These increased efficiencies and avoided cost increases will enable National 
Grid to accomplish more work with its capital and operational budgets thereby achieving 
its system modernization ambitions while continuing to effectively manage its assets.   
 
Alternatives:  
An alternative approach to achieving improved asset management capabilities to achieve 
levels of insight being delivered by VOLT Enablement would be to manually create data 
insights based on existing processes and siloed data sets and systems.  This option avoids 
investment in new IT systems, integrations and data sets, but results in increased costs 
over the long-term as labor resources would have to increase to manually generate, 
update and maintain the new data and resulting insights.  Furthermore, without 
implementation of the new EAM system, additional vendor support budgets would be 
needed to maintain systems that are approaching end-of-life; specifically Computapole in 
the near-term and STORMS in the longer term.

Exhibit __ (EIOP-14) 
 
Non-Wires Alternatives Recovery and Incentive Mechanisms 
 
 
 
 


--- Page 472 ---
Page 1 of 3 
NON-WIRES ALTERNATIVES (“NWA”) 
Overview 
The Non-Wires Alternative Incentive Mechanism (NWA Incentive Mechanism) establishes a methodology for 
determining incentives applicable to all NWA Projects. Through the NWA Incentive Mechanism, the Company 
may retain a share of the present value of net benefits identified through the Benefit Cost Analysis (BCA) 
mythology consistent with the BCA Handbook. The incentive amount available to the Company will be 
adjusted based on the difference between the forecast cost of achieving deferral and the actual costs of such. In 
the event the number of megawatts (MW) and/or the number of megawatt hours (MWh) required to defer the 
traditional project increases or decreases, the incentive amount would be further adjusted. The NWA Incentive 
Mechanism provides an incentive floor of $0 and a cap of 50% of the initially-identified net benefits.  
NWA Project Costs will be deferred with carrying costs.1 Recovery of such costs will be amortized over the 
contract period, with offsetting credits to the extent that an NWA Project defers the need for a traditional 
infrastructure project included in the Company’s Average Electric Plant in Service Balance. 
The Company also may petition the Commission to modify the general NWA Incentive Mechanism described 
below if doing so is in the public interest. 
BCA 
For all Projects, the Company will use a BCA to calculate the present value of the net costs and benefits of an 
NWA project. The BCA will consider all the benefit and cost categories in the Commission’s January 21, 2016 
Order Establishing Benefit Cost Analysis Framework (“BCA Order”) and use the Company’s BCA Handbook. 
A Project specific BCA will be developed to determine the Initial Net Benefits, and the Initial Incentive will be 
set as 30 percent of these Initial Net Benefits.   
NWA Incentive Mechanism 
The Company will establish an “Initial Incentive” equal to 30 percent of the present value of net benefits (Initial 
Net Benefits), i.e., the present value of net benefits projected at the time the Company has either entered into 
contracts with distributed energy resource (“DER”) providers for the entire NWA project portfolio, or when 
there is reasonable certainty on the price of the NWA project portfolio. To establish the Initial Incentive, the 
Company shall make a compliance filing as part of the implementation plan. Prior to making its compliance 
filing to set the Initial Incentive, the Company will seek input from Department of Public Service Staff.  
Once the NWA Project has become operational and implemented, the Company will calculate the difference in 
NWA Project Cost, which will be equal to the initially-forecast cost of the NWA Project, less the actual cost of 
the NWA Project. The Final Incentive will equal the sum of the Initial Incentive and 50 percent of difference in 
NWA Project Cost. The Final Incentive is subject to a floor of $0 and a cap of 50 percent of the Initial Net 
Benefits. The Final Incentive may be adjusted based on changes in NWA Project Cost.  
1 Carrying costs shall be at the pre-tax weighted average cost of capital. 
Exhibit__(EIOP-14) 
Page 1 of 3


--- Page 473 ---
 
Page 2 of 3 
 
Should additional MW and/or MWh be needed to achieve the initially proposed deferral of a traditional 
infrastructure project, or to increase the duration of the deferral, and the Company finds it beneficial to procure 
additional MW and/or MWh, the Company will seek input from Department of Public Service Staff and procure 
to meet the need. So long as it is feasible and remains cost beneficial to procure the additional MW and/or 
MWh to continue deferral, the Company will be authorized to receive cost recovery of the expenditures 
incurred in obtaining the additional MW or MWh, including carrying charges. However, the Company’s Final 
Incentive would not reflect either the costs2 or the benefits associated with the additional MW or MWh. In the 
event the Company determines that acquiring additional MW or MWh is technically or operationally infeasible, 
it will plan to implement an alternative solution. Recovery of any incentives related to that project will be halted 
without requiring a refund of the amounts already collected at that time. 
 
In the event fewer MW and/or MWh are needed to achieve the intended deferral of traditional infrastructure, the 
Company will only reduce the number of MW and/or MWh it plans to procure if both the need for reduced MW 
or MWh is shown to be a sustained downward trend over a three-year period, and the Company needs only 70 
percent or fewer of the initially forecast MW and/or MWh to achieve the intended deferral. The Company will 
true-up the incentive by converting the Initial Incentive to an Initial Unit Incentive by dividing its 30 percent 
share of Initial Net Benefits by the initial number of MW it forecasted. Similarly, the Difference in NWA 
Project Cost to achieve deferral will be calculated on a per-MW basis, the Unit Difference in NWA Project 
Cost, by dividing the actual NWA Project Cost by the number of MW required. The Final Incentive will be 
calculated as the sum of the Initial Unit Incentive plus or minus the Unit Difference in NWA Project Cost, 
multiplied by the reduced amount of MW determined to be necessary, subject to the same 50 percent share of 
Initial Net Benefits incentive cap and $0 incentive floor provisions. 
 
 
NWA Cost and Incentive Recovery 
 
Consistent with the Track Two Order, the Company’s Capital Investment Reconciliation Mechanism is revised 
to remove the financial disincentive utilities face when engaging in NWA projects. To the extent an NWA 
project results in the Company displacing a capital project that is reflected in the Average Electric Plant in 
Service Balances, the balance(s) will be reduced to exclude the forecasted net plant associated with the 
displaced project. The carrying charge associated with the displaced project will be applied as a credit against 
the recovery of the associated NWA project cost to be recovered from customers. In the event the carrying 
charge on the net plant of any displaced project is higher than the recovery of the associated NWA project costs, 
the difference will be deferred for the benefit of customers. 
 
NWA project costs and the Final Incentive under the NWA Incentive Mechanism will be allocated to each 
Service Class (SC) based on the following allocators: (1) 1CP for the transmission portion (if any) of the 
deferred traditional project; and (2) non-coincident demand allocator for the sub-transmission and distribution 
portions of the deferred traditional project. For example, the costs and incentives related to an NWA project 
which defers the need for sub-transmission infrastructure would be allocated to SCs based on their non-
coincident sub-transmission demand allocator. Similarly, the costs and incentives related to an NWA project 
which defers the need for primary-voltage distribution infrastructure would be allocated to SCs based on their 
non-coincident primary demand allocator. If an NWA project will benefit only certain classes of customers, the 
cost allocation will be limited to the benefitted classes. Once allocated to each SC, these costs would be 
recovered through an electric rate statement, which will be filed with the Commission and posted to the 
Company’s website, that indicates the NWA surcharge rate to be included in the delivery charge, and to roll the 
                                                          
2 The expenditures related to acquiring such additional MW will not be considered in the Difference in NWA 
Project Cost used to calculate the Final Incentive. 
Exhibit__(EIOP-14) 
Page 2 of 3


--- Page 474 ---
 
Page 3 of 3 
 
NWA surcharge into the delivery charge line item on customers’ bills, either the per kW or per kWh rate, on a 
demand-basis where available and on an energy-basis where necessary. 
 
 
Amortization of NWA Project Costs and Incentives 
 
The Company will recover its NWA project costs over the contract period. If an NWA solution consists of one 
or more contracts, then the amortization will equal the time between the first contract signing and the last 
contract expiring. The recovery period will begin when the NWA project costs are realized, and/or the first 
payment is made. Any unamortized costs plus carrying charges, will be incorporated into base rates when 
electric base rates are reset. 
 
The Company will be awarded and begin calculating and collecting the Final Incentive from customers once at 
least 70 percent of the MW it procured for the NWA project have become operational and have been verified 
through the Company’s measurement and verification procedures. For all NWA Projects, once awarded, the 
Company will amortize the Final Incentive of an NWA project over the remaining contract period for the NWA 
project, inclusive of carrying costs on the unamortized balance of the Final Incentive. 
 
 
Reporting Requirements 
 
The Company will submit a detailed implementation plan and BCA for each NWA project once there is 
reasonable certainty as to the costs of the NWA project portfolio. The implementation plan for each NWA will 
include, at a minimum: (1) detailed measurement and verification procedures; (2) the portfolio of component 
load reductions or DER to be implemented; (3) the anticipated costs of the NWA; (4) a demonstration of 
whether the costs of the NWA projects are incremental to the Company’s revenue requirement or will be 
displacing a project subject to the Capital Investment Reconciliation Mechanism; (5) a customer and 
community outreach plan, if applicable; and (6) the BCA results when available. The implementation plan for 
each project will be updated at least annually; however, the Company will also update relevant plans promptly, 
if it determines it needs to increase or decrease the number of MW or MWh required to effectuate an NWA 
project, or if the length of the deferral period for the traditional infrastructure solution associated with the NWA 
is modified. Annual implementation plans will be filed by January 31 of each year. If the number of MW, 
MWh, or length of deferral is modified, the Company shall also file an updated BCA, as appropriate. 
 
In addition, the Company also will file quarterly reports showing: (1) NWA project expenditures and all 
relevant details with respect to project costs; (2) a description of the NWA project activities; (3) anticipated 
project in-service dates; (4) NWA cost and incentive recoveries; and (5) identification of operational savings or 
other benefits. The quarterly reports shall be filed 60 days after the close of each calendar year quarter.
